For each question: the ordered sequence of tool calls each arm actually made, its input arguments, a truncated preview of what came back, and the token/cost breakdown (input / output / cache-creation / cache-read). Q01Group A — End-to-end / cross-module synthesisTrace the full lifecycle of a stock item from Sales Order through to final accounting: Sales Order, Delivery Note, and Sales Invoi…
Trace the full lifecycle of a stock item from Sales Order through to final accounting: Sales Order, Delivery Note, and Sales Invoice. Given that a Delivery Note can be submitted well before the corresponding Sales Invoice, how does ERPNext ensure the cost of goods sold is booked exactly once rather than twice? Name the relevant functions, classes and files across all three doctypes.

final answer
COGS is booked once because exactly one of the Delivery Note or the Sales Invoice posts the stock ledger entries and the stock-to-expense GL entries for an item. I read the guard code and the GL composers. I did not read the `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` mapper functions or `sales_order.py` beyond a grep. **1. Sales Order** (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`) - It posts no stock or GL entries. It only reserves stock and tracks progress. - `update_reserved_qty` handles the reservation. - `update_prevdoc_status`, `update_delivery_status` and `update_picking_status` keep `per_delivered`, `per_billed` and status up to date. - The mappers `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` create the downstream documents. They carry `so_detail` and `sales_order` onto the child rows. **2. Delivery Note** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`, class `DeliveryNote`) - It inherits from `SellingController` in `controllers/selling_controller.py`, whose `update_stock_ledger` writes the Stock Ledger Entries. It also inherits from `StockController` in `controllers/stock_controller.py`, whose `make_gl_entries` builds the GL entries. - `DeliveryNote.get_gl_entries` calls `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose`. That class is in `stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py` and extends `BaseStockGLComposer` in `stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`. - It posts Dr COGS / Cr Stock (inventory account) at valuation rate. `validate_expense_account` lets the debit go to the "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) account instead of COGS when `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` is set on the Company. - Billing is tracked through `update_billing_status`, which updates `billed_amt` and `per_billed`. **3. Sales Invoice** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`, class `SalesInvoice`) - `SalesInvoice.get_gl_entries` calls `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose`, in `accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. - **Invoice made from a Delivery Note (`update_stock = 0`):** - `make_item_gl_entries` posts only the income and receivable entries. The stock-to-COGS entries run only under `if cint(doc.update_stock) and perpetual inventory`, via `super().get_gl_entries()`. - `SalesInvoice.update_stock` is a field on the invoice. It defaults to 0 when the invoice is created from a Delivery Note. - So the invoice books no stock ledger entries and no COGS. - **Invoice created directly (`update_stock = 1`):** - The invoice posts the stock ledger entries and COGS itself. `on_submit` calls `update_stock_ledger` only when `update_stock == 1`. - `validate_delivery_note` throws if any item has a `delivery_note` while `update_stock` is set ("stock cannot be updated again"). The Delivery Note and the invoice therefore cannot both move stock for the same item. - **SDBNB reversal:** - `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` returns early if `update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - For each item linked through `delivery_note` or `dn_detail`, `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` finds the Delivery Note's expense account. `_is_sdbnb_reversal` confirms it is an SDBNB account that differs from the item's own expense account. - It reads the original valuation from the Delivery Note's Stock Ledger Entry, matching `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no = dn_detail`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` then credits SDBNB and debits the item's real COGS account, for `valuation_rate × stock_qty`. So the Delivery Note's cost parks in SDBNB and moves to COGS exactly once, when the invoice is submitted. - **Billing status:** - `update_billing_status_in_dn` sums the submitted invoice amounts per `dn_detail` and updates `billed_amt` and `per_billed` on the Delivery Note. - `update_prevdoc_status` does the same for the Sales Order. - **Cancellation:** - `make_reverse_gl_entries` handles it. For a cancelled invoice with `update_stock` set and perpetual inventory on, the stock GL entries are reversed (line 1067). **Summary of the flow** - Sales Order: no accounting. - Delivery Note: Dr COGS (or SDBNB) / Cr Stock. - Invoice from the Delivery Note: Dr Receivable / Cr Income, plus a Dr COGS / Cr SDBNB reclass only when SDBNB is in use. - Invoice created directly with `update_stock = 1`: the invoice posts the stock movement and COGS, and no Delivery Note is involved.

final answer
A stock item is only taken out of stock and booked to cost of goods sold (COGS) once. It happens in whichever document moves the stock, either the Delivery Note or a Sales Invoice with `update_stock=1`. A Sales Invoice built from a Delivery Note only reverses the interim "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) entry. I read the code paths below. I did not run any tests. ## 1. Sales Order - **Mapping:** `selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py` has `make_delivery_note()` and `make_sales_invoice()`. The Delivery Note mapper links each row back through `so_detail`. The Sales Invoice mapper does the same and sets the billed quantity to `qty - get_billed_qty(...)`. - **No accounting:** the Sales Order posts no stock ledger or general ledger (GL) entries. It only receives progress back from the later documents. - **Progress tracking:** `StatusUpdater` in `controllers/status_updater.py` drives `per_delivered` and `per_billed`. The Delivery Note's `status_updater` list (in `DeliveryNote.__init__`) updates `delivered_qty` on Sales Order Item. It also counts Sales Invoice Item rows where `update_stock=1` as deliveries, via `second_source_extra_cond`. ## 2. Delivery Note (`stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`) - **`DeliveryNote.on_submit`** runs in this order: - `update_prevdoc_status()` and `update_billing_status()`. - `update_stock_ledger()`, which writes the Stock Ledger Entries (SLEs). - `make_gl_entries()`. - `repost_future_sle_and_gle()`. - **GL entries:** `get_gl_entries()` calls `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose()` in `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`. That class extends `BaseStockGLComposer` in `stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`. It books Dr expense account, Cr stock account, with the amount taken from the SLE valuation. - **Choice of expense account:** `DeliveryNote.validate_expense_account()` decides the debit account. - If the company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` and a `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` account, every stock item's `expense_account` is set to that SDBNB account. - Otherwise it uses the default COGS account. - Items linked to a Sales Invoice (`against_sales_invoice`) are not allowed to use SDBNB. - On a sales return, if `disable_sdbnb_in_sr` is set, the default expense account is used instead. ## 3. Sales Invoice (`accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/`) - **Mapping from the Delivery Note:** `stock/doctype/delivery_note/mapper.py::make_sales_invoice()` sets `dn_detail` and `delivery_note` on each invoice row. `get_invoiced_qty_map()` and `get_returned_qty_map()` keep it to the unbilled remainder. - **Validation:** `validate_multiple_billing("Delivery Note", "dn_detail", "amount")` stops over-billing a Delivery Note row. - **`SalesInvoice.on_submit`:** - It calls `update_billing_status_in_dn()`, which sets `billed_amt` on Delivery Note Item and then calls `update_billing_percentage`. `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` in `delivery_note/services/billing_status.py` handles invoices billed against the Sales Order. - It only runs `update_stock_ledger()` if `update_stock == 1`. - It then runs `make_gl_entries()` and `get_gl_entries()`, which call `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.compose()` in `sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. That builds the receivable, tax and income entries through `make_customer_gl_entry`, `make_tax_gl_entries` and `make_item_gl_entries`. ## How COGS is booked exactly once There are two mutually exclusive paths: 1. **Sales Invoice with `update_stock=1`.** The invoice itself moves the stock and books COGS through its own SLEs. The Delivery Note's `status_updater` counts these rows as deliveries. 2. **Delivery Note first, then a Sales Invoice with `update_stock=0`.** - The Delivery Note books Dr SDBNB (or COGS), Cr Stock. - The invoice creates no SLE, so stock is not reduced again and no second COGS entry is made from stock. Revenue and the receivable are booked on the invoice. - If SDBNB is enabled, `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries()` runs. It returns early when `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. - For each row it calls `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item()`. That requires `delivery_note` or `dn_detail` and a stock item. It also requires `_is_sdbnb_reversal()`: the Delivery Note's `expense_account` must have account type "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and differ from the invoice row's `expense_account`. - It then reads the SLE of that Delivery Note row (`voucher_no` plus `voucher_detail_no = dn_detail`) and works out `valuation_rate = stock_value_difference / actual_qty`. The amount is `valuation_rate * item.stock_qty`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries()` posts Cr SDBNB and Dr the invoice row's COGS expense account for that amount. The SDBNB account therefore nets to zero. COGS is debited once, at invoice time, using the valuation recorded at delivery. If SDBNB is not enabled, the Delivery Note debits COGS directly and the invoice adds nothing, so COGS is still booked once. ## Cancellation and reposting - **Cancellation:** `DeliveryNote.on_cancel` calls `check_next_docstatus()`, which blocks cancelling a Delivery Note that has a submitted invoice. - **Reposting:** `repost_item_valuation.py::_update_post_delivery_billed_vouchers` reposts the dependent invoices when a Delivery Note's valuation is reposted. That keeps the SDBNB reversal matching the new valuation.

final answer
Here is how a stock item moves from Sales Order to Delivery Note to Sales Invoice, and how cost of goods sold (COGS) is booked once. I read the key code paths. I did not read the Sales Order, Delivery Note or Sales Invoice `on_submit` bodies in full, and I didn't run anything. **1. Sales Order** (`erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`, class `SalesOrder`, built on `SellingController`) - It books no accounting entries. - It can reserve stock through Stock Reservation Entry. - Its items carry the references that the later documents link back to. The Delivery Note Item keeps `so_detail`, and the Sales Invoice Item keeps `so_detail` and `dn_detail`. - The mapper functions `make_delivery_note` and `make_sales_invoice` create the downstream documents. - Billing and delivery progress (`delivered_qty`, `billed_amt`) is rolled up back onto the order by `update_prevdoc_status` and the status updater. **2. Delivery Note** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`, class `DeliveryNote`, built on `SellingController` and `StockController`) - On submit it calls `update_stock_ledger()`. This writes Stock Ledger Entries (SLEs) for the outgoing quantity, with `stock_value_difference` set to the valuation cost. The valuation is computed in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`. - It then calls `make_gl_entries()`, which calls `DeliveryNote.get_gl_entries()`. That delegates to `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` in `delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`, which extends `BaseStockGLComposer` in `erpnext/stock/services/base_stock_gl_composer.py`. - The base `compose()` posts Cr Stock in Hand (the inventory account) and Dr the item's `expense_account`, using the SLE values. - `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` adds no rows of its own. It only sets `enforce_pl_expense_account = False`, so the debit may land on a balance-sheet account. - `DeliveryNote.validate_expense_account()` decides which account takes the debit. If the Company has `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` on, the item's `expense_account` is set to the Company's `stock_delivered_but_not_billed` (SDBNB) account. That account has `account_type = "Stock Delivered But Not Billed"`. - The effect is that the delivery books Dr SDBNB and Cr Stock. The cost sits in an interim account and is not yet COGS. - Items with `against_sales_invoice` are blocked from using the SDBNB account. - The Delivery Note also updates `per_billed` and `billed_amt`. This is handled by `delivery_note/services/billing_status.py`, which `SalesInvoice` imports to update the billed amount on the Delivery Note. **3. Sales Invoice** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`, class `SalesInvoice`; GL logic in `sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`, class `SalesInvoiceGLComposer`) - The invoice always posts the revenue side: Dr Debtors, Cr Income and Taxes (`make_customer_gl_entry` and the related composer methods). - Which stock entries it posts depends on the `update_stock` flag, which is set per invoice: - **`update_stock = 1`** (the invoice delivers the goods itself, with no Delivery Note). `SalesInvoice.on_submit` calls `update_stock_ledger()`, and the stock and COGS entries come from the base stock composer. This follows the same path as a Delivery Note. The invoice never touches the SDBNB account. - **`update_stock = 0`, items linked to a Delivery Note** (the usual case). The invoice writes no SLEs. Its stock handling is `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries()`, which then calls `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item()`. **How COGS is booked exactly once** - **No second stock movement.** With `update_stock = 0` the invoice never calls `update_stock_ledger()`, so stock leaves the warehouse only once, at the Delivery Note. `validate_delivery_note` and the check around line 1025 require the linked Delivery Note to be submitted. - **The invoice moves the interim cost into COGS.** `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries()` returns early if `doc.update_stock` is set or perpetual inventory is off. For each item, `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item()` runs the following checks: - The row must have `delivery_note` or `dn_detail`. - The item must be a stock item. - The Delivery Note Item's `expense_account` must be an SDBNB account that differs from the invoice item's `expense_account`. `_is_sdbnb_reversal()` tests this. - The valuation amount is read from the Delivery Note's own SLE, matched by `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no` with `is_cancelled = 0`. It is `stock_value_difference / actual_qty` multiplied by the invoice's `stock_qty`, so a partial invoice reverses only the cost of the quantity it bills. - **The entries are a pair.** `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries()` posts Cr SDBNB and Dr the item's `expense_account` (COGS) for that amount. - **The result across the two documents.** The Delivery Note posts Dr SDBNB and Cr Stock. The invoice posts Dr COGS and Cr SDBNB. The SDBNB account nets to zero and COGS is debited once, at invoice time. - **If SDBNB is not enabled** and the Delivery Note debited COGS directly, `_is_sdbnb_reversal` is false. The invoice then adds nothing, so COGS is still booked only once, at delivery. **Cancellation, returns and reposting** - Cancelling a Sales Invoice with `update_stock` runs `update_stock_ledger()` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel` (around lines 534 to 542, and `make_gl_entries` at lines 1030 to 1067). - Returns go through `SalesInvoice.get_gl_entries`, and `update_billed_amount_in_delivery_note` is skipped for returns when it is off. - Repost Item Valuation recomputes the GL through the same `get_gl_entries()` methods.

final answer
Short version: ERPNext books the cost of goods sold (COGS) once because the stock ledger and the stock-valuation GL entries are posted by only one document. Each document's GL logic is gated on `update_stock`, `dn_detail` and `delivery_note`. If the Delivery Note posted the stock movement, the Sales Invoice posts no stock entries. When the Delivery Note books to the "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" (SDBNB) account, the invoice also reverses that interim booking. I didn't read `DeliveryNoteGLComposer` itself, and I took the invoice-side `update_stock` validation from search hits rather than reading it. Cancel and return paths weren't traced. ## 1. Sales Order - The class is `SalesOrder` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/sales_order.py`. It extends `SellingController`, which extends `StockController`, `AccountsController`, `TransactionBase` and `StatusUpdater`. - A Sales Order posts no stock or GL entries. It only reserves stock and holds the quantities that are delivered and billed. - `make_delivery_note` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py` creates the Delivery Note and sets `against_sales_order` and `so_detail` on each row. - `make_sales_invoice` in the same file creates the invoice directly from the order. Its `update_item` sets the quantity to `qty - billed qty` (or `qty - returned_qty`). - `StatusUpdater` uses `update_prevdoc_status` to roll the delivered and billed percentages back onto the order. ## 2. Delivery Note (the stock movement happens here) - The class is `DeliveryNote` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`. - `on_submit` runs in this order: - `update_prevdoc_status` and `update_billing_status` update the order's delivered qty and the billing percentage. - `update_stock_ledger` (inherited from `SellingController`, `selling_controller.py:676`) writes the outgoing Stock Ledger Entries. It does this per row through `get_sle_for_source_warehouse`, and the valuation rate comes from the stock ledger. - `make_gl_entries` (from `StockController`, `stock_controller.py:178`) posts the entries. `get_gl_entries` delegates to `DeliveryNoteGLComposer.compose` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/gl_composer.py`. These entries are Dr the item's expense account (COGS, or SDBNB) and Cr the inventory account, valued at the stock ledger amount. - `validate_expense_account` (`delivery_note.py:438`) decides the debit account. With `enable_stock_delivered_but_not_billed` set, it sets each stock item's `expense_account` to the company's SDBNB account. This applies unless the row has `against_sales_invoice`, in which case the SDBNB account is forbidden. Otherwise it uses the default expense account. - Billing status is handled by `BillingStatusService` and `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/services/billing_status.py`. - `update_billed_amount_based_on_so` sums invoice amounts billed directly against the order. It then spreads them across that order's Delivery Notes oldest first. - `update_billing_percentage` on the Delivery Note then updates the Delivery Note's own billing percentage. ## 3. Sales Invoice - The class is `SalesInvoice` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`. - It is created from a Delivery Note or from the order. It carries `delivery_note` and `dn_detail` on the row, plus `sales_order` and `so_detail`. - `SalesInvoice.get_gl_entries` calls `SalesInvoiceGLComposer(self).compose` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. That produces the receivable, income and tax entries, and the stock entries described below. - `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries` (around lines 1035–1068) posts the entries and reverses them on cancel. - `update_billing_status_in_dn` (line 1084) writes `Delivery Note Item.billed_amt` from the sum of submitted invoice rows with the same `dn_detail`. If a row has only `so_detail`, it calls `update_billed_amount_based_on_so`. It then calls `update_billing_percentage` on each Delivery Note. - `BillingValidationService.validate_multiple_billing("Delivery Note", "dn_detail", "amount")` (line 353) stops a delivery being over-billed. ## How COGS is booked exactly once The rule is that whichever document moves the stock books the COGS, and the invoice never moves stock a second time. 1. **The invoice posts no stock entries when it bills a Delivery Note.** Stock Ledger Entries and the stock-valuation GL entries come from `SellingController.update_stock_ledger` and the stock part of `SalesInvoiceGLComposer`. These apply only when `update_stock` is 1. The helper `stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` in the composer returns immediately when `doc.update_stock` is set. From the grep hits, `SalesInvoice.so_dn_required` and `validate_delivery_note` (lines 854 and 904) and the `selling_controller.py:543` check on `update_stock` look like the guards that stop an invoice carrying both. Because the invoice posts no stock ledger entries, the Delivery Note's outgoing stock is the only one. 2. **If SDBNB is enabled, the invoice reverses the interim booking.** - The Delivery Note debited SDBNB, a liability-like account, instead of the expense account. - `SalesInvoiceGLComposer.stock_delivered_but_not_billed_gl_entries` handles each row that has `delivery_note` or `dn_detail`. - `_get_sdbnb_booking_for_item` skips non-stock items. It reads the Delivery Note Item's `expense_account` and looks up the Delivery Note's Stock Ledger Entry for that `voucher_detail_no`. It computes the valuation as `stock_value_difference / actual_qty * item.stock_qty`. - `_is_sdbnb_reversal` requires that account's type to be "Stock Delivered But Not Billed" and to differ from the invoice row's `expense_account`. - `_append_sdbnb_gl_entries` then posts Cr SDBNB and Dr the item's real expense account (COGS). The amount is taken from the stock ledger, so it is proportional to the quantity billed and cannot exceed what was delivered. 3. **Without SDBNB, the Delivery Note books COGS straight to the expense account.** The invoice posts only the receivable and income entries, so there is no second COGS entry. 4. **Billing status links the two documents.** Each invoice row references the Delivery Note Item through `dn_detail`. Updating `billed_amt` and the billing percentage keeps the Delivery Note and the order showing how much is still unbilled. 5. **A stand-alone invoice with `update_stock=1`** runs `update_stock_ledger` itself, with no Delivery Note, and books COGS itself. In that case `delivery_note` and `dn_detail` are empty, so the SDBNB reversal does nothing.
Q02Group A — End-to-end / cross-module synthesisTrace how raw materials move from a company's own warehouse to a supplier for subcontracted job work, get tracked against a Subcon…

Name the relevant functions, classes and files across the whole flow.

final answer
Raw materials go out to the supplier on a Stock Entry, get tracked on the Subcontracting Order's `supplied_items`, and are consumed and costed when the Subcontracting Receipt is submitted. I read the function names and bodies below in the files listed. I did not read `make_rm_stock_entry`'s body, the SCR GL code, or the Stock Entry's own `on_submit` wiring, so those parts are inferred from names and call sites. ## 1. Subcontracting Order (SCO): demand and BOM explosion **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`, class `SubcontractingOrder(SubcontractingController)`. - **Creation and validation:** - `populate_items_table` builds the SCO from the Purchase Order's finished-good and service items. - `validate_purchase_order_for_subcontracting`, `validate_service_items` and `validate_supplied_items` check the order. - `calculate_service_costs` and `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` total the costs. - **Raw material table:** the shared `SubcontractingController.create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` calls `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`. That explodes the BOM through `_get_materials_from_bom` and `__add_supplied_or_received_item`. It fills the `supplied_items` child table with `required_qty`, `rm_item_code`, `main_item_code`, `reserve_warehouse` and `rate`. `set_rate_for_supplied_items` and `update_rate_for_supplied_items` set the rate. - **Bin quantities:** `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` update the ordered and reserved quantities. `reserve_raw_materials` and `cancel_stock_reservation_entries` handle stock reservation. - **Status and Purchase Order link:** `update_status` and the module function `update_subcontracting_order_status` recompute status. `update_subcontracted_quantity_in_po` writes the quantity back to the Purchase Order. ## 2. Transfer to the supplier: Stock Entry "Send to Subcontractor" - **Entry creation:** `make_rm_stock_entry` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` builds the Stock Entry from the SCO (or a Purchase Order). - `add_items_in_ste` adds each raw material row and sets `sco_rm_detail`, which links the row to the SCO `supplied_items` row. - `get_items_from_subcontract_order`, in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`, is the whitelisted wrapper for the Stock Entry form. - **Returns:** `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` and `get_materials_from_supplier` create return entries for unused raw material. - **Validation:** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` stops over-transfer. - `validate_subcontract_order` branches on the Buying Settings option `backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on`. - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` checks against the BOM requirement, using `_get_required_qty_for_bom`. - `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer` checks against the quantity transferred, and fills `sco_rm_detail` when it is missing. - **Tracking on the SCO:** `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` runs from the entry's `on_submit` and `on_cancel`. - It calls `_get_supplied_items_details`, which uses `get_supplied_items` to sum `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` per `sco_rm_detail`. - `_update_supplied_items_in_order` writes those totals to the SCO `supplied_items` rows. - `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` calls `Bin.update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting`. ## 3. Subcontracting Receipt (SCR): receipt, consumption and cost roll-up **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`, class `SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)`. - **Creating the SCR:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in the SCO file map the SCO into an SCR. - **Raw material list:** - `get_available_materials` and `__get_transferred_items` read what was actually sent to the supplier. - `__set_supplied_or_received_items` and `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer` apply the "BOM" or "Material Transferred for Subcontract" backflush basis from Buying Settings. - `set_batch_for_supplied_items`, `__set_serial_and_batch_bundle` and `__set_consumed_qty` handle batch, serial and consumed quantity. - `reset_supplied_items` and `reset_raw_materials` rebuild the table. - **Validation:** `validate_items_qty`, `validate_bom_required_qty`, `validate_accepted_warehouse`, `validate_closed_subcontracting_order`, `get_secondary_items` and `validate_secondary_items`. - **Cost roll-up:** `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` does the roll-up. - Each supplied item gets `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. - Those amounts are summed per finished-good row into `rm_supp_cost` and `rm_cost_per_qty`. - Secondary or scrap item cost is credited through `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. - The finished-good `rate` equals `rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. If the row has a BOM, `cost_allocation_per` from the BOM is applied. - Helpers are `SubcontractingController.calculate_additional_costs` (spreads additional costs by Amount or Qty) and `get_supplied_items_cost`. - `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` set raw-material valuation. - `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center` and `set_expense_account_for_subcontracted_items` set accounts. - **`on_submit` sequence:** 1. `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` 2. `validate_bom_required_qty` 3. `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status` (received quantity back to the SCO and Purchase Order) 4. `set_subcontracting_order_status` 5. `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` 6. `update_stock_ledger` 7. `make_gl_entries` 8. `update_status` 9. `auto_create_purchase_receipt` and `update_job_card` - **Reconciling consumption:** - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` uses `__update_consumed_materials` and `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` to write `consumed_qty` onto the SCO `supplied_items`. It is capped at `supplied_qty`. - `set_subcontracting_order_status` then calls `SubcontractingOrder.update_status` to move the SCO to Partially or Fully Received. - **Stock ledger** (`SubcontractingController.update_stock_ledger`): - It calls `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` first. - Finished goods are received into the item warehouse at `incoming_rate = item.rate`, with `recalculate_rate` set. Rejected quantity goes to the rejected warehouse. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` issues the consumed raw materials out of `supplier_warehouse` with `actual_qty = -consumed_qty`. - **GL:** `SubcontractingReceipt.get_gl_entries` builds the accounting entries for the receipt. ## 4. Reports and utilities These are not part of the transfer-and-receipt flow itself. Both are under `erpnext/buying/report/`. - `subcontract_order_summary` uses `get_supplied_items`. - `subcontracted_item_to_be_received` is another report on the same data.

final answer
The flow runs in three stages: transfer to the supplier, tracking on the Subcontracting Order (SCO), then consumption and cost roll-up on the Subcontracting Receipt (SCR). I read the key code but did not read every helper. That includes `__get_transferred_items`, `__update_consumed_materials`, `set_rate_for_supplied_items`, `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` and `get_supplied_items`, so their roles below come from names and call graphs. ## 1. SCO defines the raw-material requirement - **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`, class `SubcontractingOrder(SubcontractingController)`. - **Hooks:** `before_validate` and `validate` call `set_missing_values`. On submit it updates the ordered and reserved quantities. - **`populate_items_table`** builds the SCO items from the Purchase Order (PO) service items. - **Raw-material table:** the controller's `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` builds the `supplied_items` table (Subcontracting Order Supplied Item). It uses `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`, then `__identify_change_in_item_table`, `__prepare_supplied_or_received_items` and `__validate_supplied_or_received_items`. - **BOM explosion:** `_get_materials_from_bom` and `__add_supplied_or_received_item` explode the BOM into rows, and `__set_alternative_item` handles alternative items. - **Backflush mode:** the Buying Settings option `backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on` (BOM, or Material Transferred for Subcontract) decides the quantities. In transfer mode, `__get_transferred_items` and `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer` use what was actually sent. - **Costing on the SCO:** `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` sets `rm_cost_per_qty` from the BOM raw-material cost divided by BOM quantity. `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` sets `rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty` and then `amount`. - **Order and reserved quantities:** `update_ordered_qty_for_subcontracting` and `update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` update the Bin. - **Stock reservation:** `reserve_raw_materials` and `cancel_stock_reservation_entries` handle Stock Reservation Entries. ## 2. Transfer of raw materials to the supplier - **Creating the Stock Entry:** `make_rm_stock_entry` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` maps the SCO's `supplied_items` into a Stock Entry. It sets purpose "Send to Subcontractor", links `subcontracting_order` (or `purchase_order`), and links each row through `sco_rm_detail` (or `po_detail` for a PO). It calls `set_stock_entry_type`, and `add_items_in_ste` adds the rows. Its helper `get_item_details` looks up item defaults. - **Stock Entry Type:** `StockEntryType` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_type/stock_entry_type.py`. - **Return path:** `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` and `get_materials_from_supplier` handle returns of unused material. - **Stock Entry services:** these are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/`. - `material_transfer.py`: `MaterialTransferStockEntry.on_submit` and `on_cancel` call `update_transferred_qty` and `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. - `subcontracting.py`: `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry.validate` calls `validate_subcontract_order`, which checks quantities in one of two ways. - **BOM mode:** `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom` uses `_get_required_qty_for_bom`, the over-transfer allowance, and `_validate_transfer_qty`. That function uses `get_total_supplied_qty` and `get_total_returned_qty`. - **Transfer mode:** `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer`, `get_order_rm_detail` and `_link_rm_detail_if_missing`. - **Stock Entry itself:** `StockEntry` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py`. `on_submit` calls `reserve_stock_for_subcontracting`, which calls `SubcontractingOrder.reserve_raw_materials`. It also calls `update_wo_reservation_for_subcontracting`, which calls `WorkOrderStockReservation.release_reserved_qty_for_subcontract_transfer` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/reservation.py`. That helper uses `_subcontract_transferred_qty_by_item`. - **Stock movement:** the ledger entries move stock from the company warehouse (`s_warehouse`) to the supplier's warehouse (`t_warehouse`). ## 3. Tracking against the SCO - **Updating the SCO:** `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry.update_subcontract_order_supplied_items` runs on submit and cancel. It calls `_get_order_supplied_items` and `_get_supplied_items_details`, which uses `get_supplied_items`. `_update_supplied_items_in_order` writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` onto the SCO supplied-item rows. `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` then refreshes the Bin through `get_bin(...).update_reserved_qty_for_sub_contracting`. - **Pending report:** the report `subcontracted_raw_materials_to_be_transferred` under `erpnext/buying/report/` shows what is still to be sent. ## 4. Receipt: consumption and cost roll-up - **Creating the receipt:** `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` are in `subcontracting_order.py`. The inner `update_item` function fills the receipt rows. - **File:** `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`, class `SubcontractingReceipt(SubcontractingController)`. - **`validate` order:** 1. `reset_supplied_items` 2. `super().validate()`, which in `SubcontractingController` runs `validate_items` and `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` 3. `get_secondary_items` 4. `set_missing_values`, which calls `set_available_qty_for_consumption` and `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` 5. `get_current_stock`, which reads the supplier warehouse Bin 6. `set_supplied_items_expense_account`, `set_supplied_items_cost_center` and `set_supplied_items_inventory_dimensions` - **Rates:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm`, `update_rate_for_supplied_items` and `set_rate_for_supplied_items` set `rate` on the Subcontracting Receipt Supplied Item rows (class `SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem`, with `consumed_qty`, `rate` and `amount`). `__set_consumed_qty` sets the consumed quantity. Batch and serial numbers for the supplied items come from `set_batch_for_supplied_items`, `__set_serial_and_batch_bundle`, `__set_batch_nos` and `__set_serial_nos`. - **Cost roll-up in `calculate_items_qty_and_amount`:** 1. Each supplied row's `amount` is `consumed_qty × rate`. The amounts are summed per `reference_name` into `rm_supp_cost` and `rm_cost_per_qty` on the finished-goods row. 2. Secondary or scrap items form `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. 3. The finished item's `rate` is `rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + LCV per qty`. If a BOM is present, the amount is scaled by the BOM's `cost_allocation_per`. 4. Receipt totals `total_qty` and `total` are accumulated. - **Additional costs:** `SubcontractingController.calculate_additional_costs` distributes them by Amount or Qty. `get_supplied_items_cost` is a helper that sums the supplied-item cost for a row. - **On submit (`on_submit`):** - `validate_bom_required_qty` - `update_status_updater_args`, then `update_prevdoc_status`, which updates `received_qty` on the SCO - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`, which calls `__update_consumed_materials` and `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. This writes `consumed_qty` back to the SCO supplied-item rows, capped at `supplied_qty`. - `update_stock_reservation_entries` - `update_stock_ledger`, then `make_gl_entries`, then `repost_future_sle_and_gle` - `update_status`, `auto_create_purchase_receipt` and `update_job_card` - `set_subcontracting_order_status` updates the SCO status. - **Stock ledger** (`SubcontractingController.update_stock_ledger`): - `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` refreshes the SCO Bin quantities. - A positive Stock Ledger Entry (SLE) for each finished-goods row goes to the receiving warehouse, with `incoming_rate = item.rate` and `recalculate_rate`. Rejected quantity goes to the rejected warehouse. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` posts a negative SLE for each `supplied_items` row from `supplier_warehouse`, using `consumed_qty` and `dependant_sle_voucher_detail_no = reference_name`. - **General ledger:** `SubcontractingReceiptGLComposer._make_item_gl_entries` is in `subcontracting_receipt/services/gl_composer.py` (`compose` is its entry point). It debits the inventory account for each item's SLE `stock_value_difference`. It credits the expense account for that value minus the service cost. It credits `service_expense_account` for `service_cost_per_qty × qty`. It also uses the supplied items' `expense_account` and `cost_center`. - **Repost:** `repost_future_sle_and_gle` revalues the supplied-item rates when a backdated stock reconciliation changes costs. `test_supplied_items_cost_after_reposting` covers this. - **Cancel:** `on_cancel` reverses the same steps.

final answer
The raw-material flow runs in three stages: transfer to the supplier, tracking on the Subcontracting Order (SCO), and consumption plus cost roll-up on the Subcontracting Receipt (SCR). I read the code for most of it. I did not open `SubcontractingOrder.validate`, `populate_items_table`, `reserve_raw_materials`, or most of `stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py`. I inferred their behavior from names and call sites. **Core classes** - `SubcontractingController(StockController)` in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py` is the shared engine. - `SubcontractingOrder` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py` extends it. - `SubcontractingReceipt` in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py` extends it. - The child tables are `SubcontractingOrderSuppliedItem` and `SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem` (`subcontracting_receipt_supplied_item.py`). **1. Setting up the order's required raw materials** - The SCO is created from a Purchase Order that contains the service items. - `SubcontractingOrder.validate_purchase_order_for_subcontracting`, `validate_service_items` and `populate_items_table` handle that step. - `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` calls `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`, which fills the `supplied_items` table from the BOM. The helpers are `__prepare_supplied_or_received_items`, `_get_materials_from_bom`, `__add_supplied_or_received_item` and `__validate_supplied_or_received_items`. - `set_rate_for_supplied_items` and `update_rate_for_supplied_items` set the rate on each row. - `calculate_supplied_items_qty_and_amount` totals the supplied items on the SCO. - `SubcontractingOrder.reserve_raw_materials` optionally reserves stock. `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` updates the Bin quantities. **2. Transfer to the supplier** - `make_rm_stock_entry(subcontract_order, rm_items, order_doctype, target_doc)` builds a Stock Entry with purpose "Send to Subcontractor". - It uses `get_mapped_doc`, and its `post_process` fills the item rows. - Each row goes from `s_warehouse` (the order's warehouse or reserve warehouse) to `t_warehouse` (the order's `supplier_warehouse`). - It links each row back through `sco_rm_detail`, or `po_detail` when the source is a Purchase Order, and sets `subcontracted_item` and `main_item_code`. - It enforces `over_transfer_allowance` from Buying Settings. - `get_materials_from_supplier` and `make_return_stock_entry_for_subcontract` handle returning unused materials. `add_items_in_ste` swaps the source and target warehouses for the return. - In `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py`, `StockEntry` uses `subcontract_data` to pick the SCO or Purchase Order field names. - `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry` in `stock_entry/services/subcontracting.py` handles validation and tracking: - `validate_subcontract_order`, `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom`, `_validate_transfer_qty`, `get_total_supplied_qty` and `get_total_returned_qty` run on validate. - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. That writes `supplied_qty` back onto the order's supplied-items rows, and `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting` adjusts the reserved quantity. - `get_supplied_items` and `get_items_from_subcontract_order` are helpers in the same file. - `StockEntry` also runs `reserve_stock_for_subcontracting`, `update_subcontracting_order_status` and `validate_closed_subcontracting_order`. - The report `buying/report/subcontracted_raw_materials_to_be_transferred` shows what is still pending transfer. **3. Receipt, consumption and cost roll-up** - `make_subcontracting_receipt` and `get_mapped_subcontracting_receipt` in the SCO file map the order to an SCR. Their `update_item` callback sets the pending quantity. - `SubcontractingReceipt.validate` does the following: - `reset_supplied_items`, then `super().validate()`. - The SCR's own `set_missing_values` is also called; I did not read its body. - `set_available_qty_for_consumption` and `get_current_stock` check what the supplier warehouse holds. - Expense account, cost center and inventory dimensions are set on the supplied items. - `__get_transferred_items`, `__get_received_items`, `__get_consumed_items` and `__update_consumed_materials` work out what was transferred and what is left. - `get_available_materials` and `__get_qty_based_on_material_transfer` take consumption from the transferred material. `set_batch_for_supplied_items` and the serial/batch bundle helpers handle batch and serial numbers. - `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` does the cost roll-up: - Each supplied row gets `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. - The row amounts are summed per finished-good row as `rm_supp_cost`. - `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / received_qty`. - `item.rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + landed-cost-voucher cost per qty`. - Secondary or scrap items subtract their cost via `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. - If the row has a BOM, the amount is scaled by `BOM.cost_allocation_per`. - `calculate_additional_costs` spreads `additional_costs` across items by amount or quantity. - `get_supplied_items_cost` returns the raw-material cost for one finished-good row. - `validate_bom_required_qty` raises `BOMQuantityError` if consumption doesn't match what the BOM requires. **4. Posting on submit** - `SubcontractingReceipt.on_submit` runs these in order: 1. `validate_closed_subcontracting_order` 2. `validate_bom_required_qty` 3. `update_status_updater_args` and `update_prevdoc_status` 4. `set_subcontracting_order_status` 5. `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` 6. `update_stock_ledger` 7. `make_gl_entries` 8. `repost_future_sle_and_gle` 9. `update_status` 10. `auto_create_purchase_receipt` 11. `update_job_card` - `set_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order` calls `__update_consumed_qty_in_subcontract_order`. That writes `consumed_qty` back onto the order's supplied-items rows, capped at `supplied_qty`. - `update_stock_ledger` does two things: - It posts a stock ledger entry for each finished good into the accepted warehouse, with `incoming_rate = item.rate` and `recalculate_rate = 1`. Rejected quantities go to the rejected warehouse at a rate of 0. - `make_sl_entries_for_supplier_warehouse` posts a negative `consumed_qty` from `supplier_warehouse` for each raw material. - `get_gl_entries` builds the GL entries, using the expense accounts set on the supplied items. - On cancel, the same steps run in reverse. **5. Order status** - `SubcontractingOrder.update_status` and `set_subcontracting_order_status` recalculate the status from received, transferred and consumed quantities. - `update_subcontracted_quantity_in_po` updates the linked Purchase Order. - Module-level `set_subcontracting_order_status` and `update_subcontracting_order_status` are also in the SCO file. - `update_ordered_and_reserved_qty` refreshes the Bin's ordered and reserved quantities.

final answer
Here is the flow, built from the two code searches I ran. I didn't open every function body, so a few steps below are described from names and call relationships only. I've marked those. ## 1. Subcontracting Order (SCO) defines the raw materials - **`SubcontractingOrder`** is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_order/subcontracting_order.py`. It extends `SubcontractingController`, which extends `StockController` and then `AccountsController`. - Its `validate`, `on_submit` and `set_missing_values` methods build the `supplied_items` child table. That table is `SubcontractingOrderSuppliedItem`. - **Where the rows come from:** I did not read the controller's supplied-item-building code (the `__prepare_supplied_or_received_items` / `__set_supplied_items` helpers, by name only). Judging from `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`, rows are produced from the BOM, or from the materials already transferred. - **Row fields:** each row carries `rm_item_code`, `main_item_code`, `required_qty`, `reserve_warehouse`, `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty`. - The shared logic is in `erpnext/controllers/subcontracting_controller.py`. `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items(raw_material_table)` calls three helpers in order: - `__identify_change_in_item_table` - `__prepare_supplied_or_received_items` - `__validate_supplied_or_received_items` - `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received` is a thin wrapper around `set_materials_for_subcontracted_items`. - The SCO's `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` (line 206) totals the order's items. - The reserved quantity for subcontracting is also maintained from here. That is by name only (`update_reserved_qty_for_subcontract` and `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`). I did not read its body. ## 2. Transfer raw materials to the supplier - **`make_rm_stock_entry(subcontract_order, rm_items, order_doctype, target_doc)`** is whitelisted, in `subcontracting_controller.py` (line 1344). - It uses `get_mapped_doc` to map the submitted SCO (or a Purchase Order, for the legacy flow) to a Stock Entry. The mapped fields are `supplier`, `supplier_name`, `supplier_address`, and `to_warehouse` from `supplier_warehouse`. - Its `post_process` sets `purpose = "Send to Subcontractor"` and links `subcontracting_order` (or `purchase_order`) on the Stock Entry. It then calls `set_stock_entry_type`. - For each raw material it adds an item row. `s_warehouse` is the item's `warehouse` or `reserve_warehouse`, and `t_warehouse` is the order's `supplier_warehouse`. - Each row's `sco_rm_detail` (or `po_detail` for a Purchase Order) points back at the SCO supplied-item row. The row also carries `main_item_code`, `subcontracted_item` and the serial/batch fields. - The quantity is `qty`, or else `required_qty - total_supplied_qty`. A row is skipped once the transferred percentage reaches 100 plus the Buying Settings `over_transfer_allowance`. - A related helper, `add_items_in_ste` (line 1469, same file), also adds items to a Stock Entry. ## 3. Tracking the transfer against the SCO The Stock Entry side is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/`. - **`SendToSubcontractorStockEntry`** (in `subcontracting.py`, extends `BaseStockEntry`): - `validate` calls `validate_subcontract_order`. This depends on the Buying Settings value `backflush_raw_materials_of_subcontract_based_on`. - For "BOM" it calls `validate_subcontracting_order_for_bom`. That uses `_get_required_qty_for_bom`, which also handles alternative items, and `_validate_transfer_qty`, which enforces the allowance and net of returns. It then calls `_link_rm_detail_if_missing`. - For "Material Transferred for Subcontract" it calls `validate_subcontracting_order_for_transfer`. - `get_order_rm_detail` looks up the matching SCO supplied-item row. - `on_submit` and `on_cancel` both call `update_subcontract_order_supplied_items`. - That method calls `_get_order_supplied_items`, `_get_supplied_items_details`, `_update_supplied_items_in_order` and `_update_reserved_qty_for_subcontracting`. It uses `get_supplied_items` to sum the transferred quantities per row. It writes `supplied_qty`, `returned_qty` and `total_supplied_qty` onto the SCO supplied-item rows. It then adjusts the reserved quantity. - `material_transfer.py` has the same-named method on the base material-transfer class. It instantiates `SendToSubcontractorStockEntry(self.doc).update_subcontract_order_supplied_items()` after `update_transferred_qty`. - **Result of the transfer:** the stock now sits in the supplier warehouse, and the SCO shows how much was supplied. ## 4. Subcontracting Receipt (SCR) consumes the materials and rolls up cost - **`SubcontractingReceipt`** is in `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/subcontracting_receipt.py`. It also extends `SubcontractingController`. - It is created from the SCO, normally with `make_subcontracting_receipt`, which I did not open. - Its `supplied_items` table is `SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem`. - `before_validate`, `validate` and `on_submit` call the controller methods. - **Reconciling what is available:** - `get_available_materials` works out what is still at the supplier. - `__update_consumed_materials(doctype, return_consumed_items)` deducts what earlier receipts already consumed from `available_materials`. It does this per `(rm_item_code, main_item_code, order)`, and also removes consumed serial and batch numbers. - The consumption itself is set up through `create_raw_materials_supplied_or_received("supplied_items")`. - `__validate_supplied_or_received_items`, including `__validate_batch_no` and `__validate_serial_no`, checks consumption against what was supplied. - **Valuing the raw materials:** `set_valuation_rate_for_rm` is in `subcontracting_controller.py`. For each supplied row it calls `get_incoming_rate` from `erpnext/stock/utils.py`. That call uses the `supplier_warehouse` and a negative `consumed_qty` (positive on returns). If the rate changed, it updates `row.rate` and `row.amount` and calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount`. - **Cost roll-up:** `SubcontractingReceipt.calculate_items_qty_and_amount` (line 496) does the work. 1. For each supplied item, `amount = consumed_qty * rate`. These are summed per `reference_name`, which is the receipt item row. 2. Secondary or scrap items are valued as (`received_qty - process_loss_qty`) × `rate`, or `qty` for legacy scrap. They are summed per parent item as `secondary_items_cost_per_qty`. 3. For each finished-good row: `rm_supp_cost` is the raw-material total. `rm_cost_per_qty = rm_supp_cost / (received_qty or qty)`. The landed-cost voucher amount is divided by `qty`. 4. `rate = rm_cost_per_qty + service_cost_per_qty + additional_cost_per_qty + lcv_cost_per_qty`. 5. Where a BOM is present, `received_qty = qty + rejected_qty + process_loss_qty`, and `amount = received_qty * rate`. - On submit, the receipt posts stock ledger entries and GL entries. The SCR issues the consumed raw materials from the supplier warehouse and receives the finished goods. The General Ledger postings come from `StockController`. I did not read that posting code. - **Back-updating the order:** the SCO's received quantities and status are updated. The related status services are `SubcontractingService` and `StatusService` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/services/`. `update_receiving_percentage` is there, and it is also instantiated from the SCO's `update_status`. ## 5. Related classes - `SubcontractingOrderItem` and `SubcontractingOrderServiceItem` are the SCO's finished-good and service rows. - `SubcontractingReceiptSuppliedItem` is the receipt's table of consumed raw materials. - `PurchaseReceiptItemSupplied` is the equivalent for the legacy Purchase Receipt and Purchase Invoice flow. - `LandedCostTaxesandCharges` supplies the additional costs. - `SubcontractingInwardOrder` is a separate customer-provided-material flow that reuses the same controller. The controller's `__init__` branches on that doctype.

final answer
I'm tracing the last piece, how the GL composer posts the landed-cost entries, and I've read enough of the main flow to answer. **Distributing charges across items** (`erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`) - `get_items_from_purchase_receipts` loads the receipt lines into the `items` table, using the helper `get_pr_items`. - `validate` calls `set_total_taxes_and_charges`, which sums the base amounts of the `taxes` rows (freight, customs and so on) into `total_taxes_and_charges`. - `set_applicable_charges_on_item` does the split. It uses the field named by `distribute_charges_based_on`: Qty, Amount, or Distribute Manually. - Each line gets `applicable_charges = item.<basis> × total_taxes_and_charges / Σ basis`. - Any rounding remainder goes onto the last line. - If the total basis is zero, it throws an error. - With Distribute Manually, the user enters the amounts and the function skips the split. - `validate_applicable_charges_for_item` runs on submit. It checks that the line charges add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`, allowing a rounding tolerance. It puts a small difference on the last row, and throws if the difference is larger. **Updating receipts that are already submitted** (`on_submit` and `on_cancel` both call `update_landed_cost`) 1. For each linked receipt, `update_landed_cost` loads the document with `frappe.get_doc`. This works for a Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Subcontracting Receipt or Stock Entry. 2. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` is a module-level function in the same file. It sums `Landed Cost Item.applicable_charges` over all submitted (docstatus 1) LCVs for each receipt row. It matches rows through `purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry. The sum goes into `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Because it only counts docstatus 1, cancelling an LCV removes its share on the next pass. 3. `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is defined in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. It recomputes `item.valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / qty_in_stock_uom`. Subcontracting Receipts call `calculate_items_qty_and_amount` instead. 4. `item.db_update()` saves the changed item rows directly to the database. `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items` then sets `Serial No.purchase_rate` to the new valuation rate. 5. Reposting stock and GL entries happens in a second loop over the receipts: - It sets `docstatus = 2` and calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old Stock Ledger and GL entries. - It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields`, then `update_stock_ledger` again. This writes new Stock Ledger Entries at the new valuation rate. - It calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` for a Purchase Receipt, or plain `make_gl_entries()` for other types. - It calls `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This revalues later transactions that depend on the changed stock value. **GL postings** - `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. It builds the entries and takes the `via_landed_cost_voucher` flag. - For stock items it takes `stock_value_diff` from `get_stock_value_difference`, which now reflects the revalued Stock Ledger Entries. For fixed assets it uses `base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount`. - It calls `doc.get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries()`, which wraps the module-level `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries` in the voucher file. That function splits each LCV expense account's amount across the receipt rows. It uses the same basis (qty, amount or manual charges) as the original distribution. - The inner helper `make_landed_cost_gl_entries(d)` then credits the LCV expense accounts and debits the stock or asset account. - If an asset was created from the receipt, `doc.update_assets(d, d.valuation_rate)` updates it too. I did not open the body of `make_landed_cost_gl_entries`, and I only read part of the end of `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries`. The description of the credit and debit accounts comes from the function name and the surrounding code, not from reading it.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) works in two stages. First it splits its charges across the receipt items. Then, on submit, it re-posts the stock and GL entries of the receipts it points at. ## 1. Distributing charges across items All of this is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, in the class `LandedCostVoucher`. - **Loading items:** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts()` fills the `items` table (`Landed Cost Item`). It reads each receipt's rows through `get_pr_items`. Each row gets its qty, rate, amount, `receipt_document`, and a link back to `purchase_receipt_item` or `stock_entry_item`. - **Total charges:** `validate()` calls `set_total_taxes_and_charges()`. That sums `base_amount` over the `taxes` table, which holds the freight and customs charges. - **Splitting the total:** `set_applicable_charges_on_item()` does the split. The `distribute_charges_based_on` field chooses the basis: Qty, Amount, or Distribute Manually. - For Qty or Amount, each item gets `item_value * total_taxes_and_charges / total_item_value`, rounded to the field's precision. The result is stored in `item.applicable_charges`. - Any rounding difference goes onto the last item. - It throws an error if the basis total is zero. - **Client side:** `landed_cost_voucher.js` has the same logic in `set_applicable_charges_for_item()` and `set_total_taxes_and_charges()`. - **On submit:** `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()` checks that the sum of `applicable_charges` matches `total_taxes_and_charges`. It absorbs a rounding difference on the last row and otherwise throws. It also handles the manual case. ## 2. Retroactively updating receipts that are already submitted `on_submit()` and `on_cancel()` both call `update_landed_cost()`. It works in two passes over `purchase_receipts`. **Pass 1 – update the item rows** 1. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` is a module-level function in the same file. For every receipt item it sums `Landed Cost Item.applicable_charges` over all submitted LCVs (docstatus 1) that reference that item. The sum goes into `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`. Because it sums every submitted LCV, several LCVs on one receipt accumulate. On cancel, the cancelled LCV drops out of the sum. 2. `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. It recomputes `item.valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / qty_in_stock_uom`. For a Subcontracting Receipt it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` instead. 3. Each item row is saved with `item.db_update()`. The receipt itself is not re-submitted. 4. For receipts other than Stock Entry and Subcontracting Receipt, `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items()` writes the new valuation rate to `Serial No.purchase_rate`. 5. Fixed-asset items are checked first by `validate_asset_qty_and_status()`. Their rate is updated later, when the asset GL entries are made. **Pass 2 – re-post stock and GL** For each receipt, in this order: 1. Set `docstatus = 2`, then call `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old Stock Ledger Entries and GL entries. 2. Set `docstatus = 1`, then call `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `update_stock_ledger(...)` again. This re-posts the stock entries with the new valuation rate. 3. Call `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` for a Purchase Receipt, or plain `make_gl_entries()` for the other types. 4. Call `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. This recalculates the later stock ledger and GL entries that depend on these receipts. **How the GL entries reflect the charges** - In `purchase_receipt.py`, `get_gl_entries()` delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose()` in `purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - In `gl_composer.py`, `make_landed_cost_gl_entries(item)` adds the landed cost to stock-in-hand. The credit side comes from the account-wise map built by `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` in `landed_cost_voucher.py`. That map reads the submitted LCVs' `taxes` rows and their expense accounts. The composer also uses `landed_cost_voucher_amount` in its valuation and fixed-asset amounts. - `PurchaseReceipt.is_landed_cost_booked_for_any_item()` checks whether any item has landed cost booked. I did not read `gl_composer.py` or `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries` in full, so the exact account split is inferred from the names and a keyword search.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) spreads its charges across line items, stores each line's share, and then reposts the already-submitted receipts. The core of this is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. I read that file, `buying_controller.py` and the grep hits in the Purchase Receipt GL composer. I did not read the full `gl_composer.py` or `purchase_receipt.py`, and I did not run anything. ## 1. Distributing charges across items - **`get_items_from_purchase_receipts()`** loads the receipt lines into the LCV `items` table. Each row is a `Landed Cost Item` and carries qty, amount, and links back to the receipt and its item row (`purchase_receipt_item`, or `stock_entry_item` for a Stock Entry). - **`set_total_taxes_and_charges()`** sums `base_amount` across the `taxes` table. This is where the freight and customs charges (`Landed Cost Taxes and Charges`) are added up. - **`set_applicable_charges_on_item()`** does the split. `validate()` calls it, though I didn't read that method body, so the call is from the graph. - It skips the split when `distribute_charges_based_on` is "Distribute Manually". - Otherwise it takes the chosen field (Qty, Amount or Distribute Manually) and totals it over all items. - Each item gets `applicable_charges = item.<field> × total_taxes_and_charges / total_item_cost`, rounded to the field precision. - Any rounding remainder is added to the last item. - It throws an error if the basis total is zero. - **`validate_applicable_charges_for_item()`** runs on submit. It checks that the item `applicable_charges` add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. A difference under 2 units of precision is absorbed into the last row. A larger difference raises an error. This is the check that matters for manual distribution. ## 2. Retroactively updating the submitted receipts `on_submit()` calls `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()`, then `update_landed_cost()`, then `update_claimed_landed_cost()`. `on_cancel()` calls `update_landed_cost()` and `update_claimed_landed_cost()`, so cancelling an LCV reverses the effect. `update_landed_cost()` loops over `purchase_receipts` twice. **Pass 1: recompute the rate** For each receipt document (Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Subcontracting Receipt or Stock Entry): 1. It loads the document with `frappe.get_doc`. 2. On a non-cancel, `validate_asset_qty_and_status()` checks that fixed-asset lines have enough linked, unsubmitted assets. 3. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` (module level) re-queries `Landed Cost Item`. For each receipt row it sums `applicable_charges` over submitted LCVs (`docstatus == 1`) that match `receipt_document` and the item row. It writes the result to `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`, so several LCVs on one receipt accumulate, and a cancelled LCV drops out. 4. For a Subcontracting Receipt it calls `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()`. For the others it calls `BuyingController.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. That sets `item.valuation_rate = (base_net_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / (qty × conversion_factor)`. 5. `item.db_update()` saves the new amounts and rates directly. The submitted receipt is not re-validated or re-submitted. 6. For Purchase Receipt and Purchase Invoice, `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items()` writes the new `valuation_rate` to `Serial No.purchase_rate` for non-asset serialised items. **Pass 2: repost stock and GL** For each receipt: 1. It temporarily sets `docstatus = 2` and calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, then `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old Stock Ledger and GL entries. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` and `update_stock_ledger(...)` again. This reposts the stock ledger entries at the new valuation rate. 3. For a Purchase Receipt it calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. Other receipt types call `make_gl_entries()`. This reposts the GL entries. 4. `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` revalues later transactions, such as issues, that used this stock. ## 3. Where the GL postings pick up the charges - `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries()` in `purchase_receipt.py` delegates to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose(...)` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - That composer adds `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount` into the inventory (stock-in-hand) debit. - `make_landed_cost_gl_entries()` in the composer credits the LCV expense accounts per item. The per-account amounts come from `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` in `landed_cost_voucher.py`. That function finds submitted `Landed Cost Purchase Receipt` parents for the receipt and builds a map keyed by `(item_code, item row name)`. - Fixed-asset lines with a landed cost amount are handled separately (`is_fixed_asset and landed_cost_voucher_amount` in the composer). - `has_landed_cost_amount(doc)` is a guard: it skips all of this when no line has an LCV amount.

final answer
A Landed Cost Voucher (LCV) first spreads its charges across its own item rows. On submit or cancel, it then rewrites the already-submitted receipts' item valuation, stock ledger and GL entries. I read the main file and the calling code. I did not open the GL composer or the stock-ledger repost logic, so those steps are described from their call sites only. ## 1. Spreading charges across items This is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/landed_cost_voucher/landed_cost_voucher.py`, class `LandedCostVoucher`. - **Loading items:** `get_items_from_purchase_receipts()` calls `get_pr_items()` to pull each receipt's item rows into the LCV `items` table. Those rows are `Landed Cost Item`, and they carry `purchase_receipt_item` (or `stock_entry_item`) and `receipt_document`. - **Total charge:** the `taxes` table (`Landed Cost Taxes and Charges`) holds the freight, customs and similar rows. `total_taxes_and_charges` is the sum of their `base_amount`. - **Distribution:** `set_applicable_charges_on_item()` runs from `validate`. - It reads the basis from `distribute_charges_based_on`, which is "Qty", "Amount" or "Distribute Manually". - For Qty or Amount, each row gets `applicable_charges = item.<basis> * total_taxes_and_charges / sum(<basis>)`. - Any rounding difference is added to the last row. - It throws if the total of the chosen basis is zero. - **Manual mode:** the user enters `applicable_charges` directly. `validate_applicable_charges_for_item()` checks that they add up to `total_taxes_and_charges`. ## 2. Pushing the charges back onto submitted receipts `on_submit` and `on_cancel` both call `update_landed_cost()`. It works in two passes over `purchase_receipts`. **Pass 1: item valuation** 1. It loads the receipt with `frappe.get_doc(receipt_document_type, receipt_document)`. 2. `set_landed_cost_voucher_amount(doc)` sums `applicable_charges` from all submitted (docstatus 1) `Landed Cost Item` rows for that receipt, per receipt item. It writes the sum to `item.landed_cost_voucher_amount`, matching on `purchase_receipt_item` or `stock_entry_item`. Because it sums every submitted LCV, cancelling one removes its share. Submitting a second one adds to the first. 3. `doc.update_valuation_rate(reset_outgoing_rate=False)` is in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`. It recomputes the item's valuation rate as `(net_rate_amount + item_tax_amount + landed_cost_voucher_amount + amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice) / qty_in_stock_uom`. Subcontracting Receipts use `calculate_items_qty_and_amount()` instead. 4. `item.db_update()` saves `landed_cost_voucher_amount` and the new rate on the already-submitted receipt, without re-submitting it. 5. For receipts other than Stock Entry and Subcontracting Receipt, `update_rate_in_serial_no_for_non_asset_items()` updates the serial numbers' rate. **Pass 2: stock ledger and GL** The receipt is reposted by cancelling its entries and re-creating them: 1. It sets `doc.docstatus = 2`, calls `update_stock_ledger(allow_negative_stock=True, via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`, then `make_gl_entries_on_cancel()`. This reverses the old stock ledger entries (SLEs) and GL entries. 2. It sets `docstatus = 1` and calls `make_bundle_using_old_serial_batch_fields(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. 3. It calls `update_stock_ledger(...)` again. This runs through `BuyingController.update_stock_ledger`, then `StockController.make_sl_entries` and `stock_ledger.make_sl_entries`, with the flag `via_landed_cost_voucher=True`. The new SLEs use the updated incoming rate. 4. For a Purchase Receipt it calls `make_gl_entries(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)`. That reaches `StockController.make_gl_entries` (`erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py:178`), then `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` (`erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py:478`), then `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose`. Other receipt types call `make_gl_entries()` with no flag. 5. `repost_future_sle_and_gle(via_landed_cost_voucher=True)` revalues later transactions in the same item and warehouse. The Stock Controller and Stock Ledger Service handle this, and it creates a Repost Item Valuation entry. ## 3. How the landed-cost GL lines are built `get_item_account_wise_lcv_entries(doc)` in `landed_cost_voucher.py` serves the Purchase Receipt GL composer. It returns early if `has_landed_cost_amount(doc)` is false. Otherwise it finds the submitted LCVs linked through `Landed Cost Purchase Receipt` and loads each one. It then splits each item's cost across the expense accounts of the LCV's charge rows, in proportion to the distribution basis. For "Distribute Manually" it uses `applicable_charges`. The composer uses that split so the landed cost goes to the stock-in-hand account and the "expenses included in valuation" account.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints by passing a `methods` argument to Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator. The decorator comes from the Frappe framework, which isn't in this repo, so I haven't read its code. The description of how it enforces the restriction is from my knowledge of Frappe, not from this codebase. **Mechanism** An endpoint is declared like this: ```python @frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"]) def some_endpoint(...): ``` Plain `@frappe.whitelist()` allows GET, POST, PUT and DELETE by default. With `methods=["POST"]`, I understand Frappe checks the request's HTTP verb when it dispatches `/api/method/<dotted.path>` or `/api/method/run_doc_method`. If the verb isn't in the list, it raises a `frappe.PermissionError` or "method not allowed" (HTTP 403/405) before the function body runs. This means a state-changing call can't be triggered by a GET, such as an `<img src>` tag, a link, or a CSRF-style request. It must be a POST, which also carries CSRF-token protection. The decorator is also used on class methods of a controller, for example `Customer.get_customer_group_details`. For those, the same check applies to `run_doc_method`. **Endpoints with the restriction** (all `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`) - `erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py` - `create_opening_voucher` - `set_customer_info` - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders` - `erpnext/selling/doctype/customer/customer.py`: `Customer.get_customer_group_details` - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections` - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py`: the whitelisted function at line 12 - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/lead.py` (line 383) and `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/mapper.py` (line 113) - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/opportunity.py` (line 392) and `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/mapper.py` (line 127) - `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py` (line 58) - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` (line 25) - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py` (line 1083) - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py` (lines 372, 454 and 468) and `erpnext/projects/doctype/project/project.py` (lines 661 and 812) - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py` (lines 1012 and 1123) and `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py` (line 435) - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py` (lines 119, 149 and 308) - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bisect_accounting_statements/bisect_accounting_statements.py` (lines 124, 187, 201 and 215) - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py` (lines 120 and 276) - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py` (line 194) - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (line 881) For the entries given only as a line number, I saw the decorator line but didn't read the function name. My search for names was limited to the `selling` and `controllers` directories, and the first search was capped at 40 results, so there are likely more such endpoints. The same decorator also appears with `allow_guest=True` on `erpnext/templates/utils.py` line 11, which means it can be used for guest-accessible POST-only endpoints too.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints by passing a `methods` argument to Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator. I found the pattern by searching the repo. I did not read Frappe's own source, so the enforcement description below comes from how the decorator is used here and from my knowledge of Frappe. **Mechanism** State-changing endpoints are declared as `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])` instead of a bare `@frappe.whitelist()`. When a request comes in through `/api/method/...` or a document-method call, Frappe's request handler compares the HTTP verb with the list in `methods`. If the verb isn't listed, such as a GET, Frappe rejects the call with a `PermissionError`/405 "Not Allowed" before the function body runs. This prevents state changes from simple GET requests. Those include URL fetches, `<img>` tags and link prefetches, which makes CSRF-style attacks much harder. Frappe also enforces CSRF tokens on POST. Some read-only endpoints are marked explicitly with `methods=["GET"]`. In `bank_reconciliation_tool.py` these are at lines 388 and 900. **Concrete restricted endpoints** - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py` - `Issue.split_issue` is a document method. - `make_issue_from_communication` - `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py`: `create_supplier_quotation` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py` - `add_node` - `create_transaction_deletion_request` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py`: `create_user` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`: `revise_budget` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py`: `add_node` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many functions, with POST decorators at lines 119, 149, 308, 459 and others - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bisect_accounting_statements/bisect_accounting_statements.py`: several methods (lines 124, 187, 201, 215) - `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reposting_settings/stock_reposting_settings.py`: line 72 - `erpnext/setup/doctype/department/department.py`: line 98 - `erpnext/assets/doctype/location/location.py`: line 227 - `erpnext/buying/doctype/supplier_scorecard/supplier_scorecard.py`: line 188 - `erpnext/support/doctype/service_level_agreement/service_level_agreement.py`: line 785 - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_ledger_invariant_check/stock_ledger_invariant_check.py`: line 309 My search output was capped at 30 results, so there are likely more endpoints with this decorator than the ones listed here.
