final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator. I found the pattern with a grep and a read of the matching definitions. I did not read Frappe's own source, so the description of what the framework does is from my knowledge of Frappe rather than from this repo. **Mechanism** - A state-changing endpoint is declared as `@frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"])`. A plain `@frappe.whitelist()` accepts any HTTP method, including GET. - Frappe records the allowed methods for the function when the decorator runs. When a request arrives at `/api/method/<dotted.path>`, the request handler checks the HTTP method against that list. - If the method isn't allowed, Frappe rejects the request with a `PermissionError`, and the request is typically answered with a 403 or 405. A GET therefore never reaches the function body. - This blocks CSRF-style attacks, such as a link or `<img>` tag that triggers a change. It also stops caches, prefetchers and crawlers from replaying a state change. - Read-only endpoints are marked the other way, with `methods=["GET"]`. Examples are some functions in `bank_reconciliation_tool.py` and `bank_account.py`. - The restriction also applies to methods on document classes, not only module-level functions. An example is `split_issue` in `issue.py`. **Endpoints with the POST-only restriction** - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py`: `make_work_orders` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/material_request/material_request.py`: `raise_work_orders` - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`: `make_purchase_receipt` - `erpnext/buying/doctype/request_for_quotation/mapper.py`: `create_supplier_quotation` - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`: `make_quality_inspections` - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py`: `update_company_master_and_address` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`: `revise_budget` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/batch/batch.py`: `split_batch` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/warehouse/warehouse.py` and `erpnext/assets/doctype/location/location.py`: `add_node` - `erpnext/support/doctype/issue/issue.py`: `split_issue` (a method on the Issue class) and `make_issue_from_communication` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/mapper.py`: `make_lead_from_communication` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/mapper.py`: `make_opportunity_from_communication` - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bank_reconciliation_tool/bank_reconciliation_tool.py`: many POST-only functions - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/bisect_accounting_statements/bisect_accounting_statements.py`: several POST-only methods The grep output was capped at 40 results, so there are more of these than the ones listed.

final answer
ERPNext restricts these endpoints with the `methods` argument of Frappe's `@frappe.whitelist` decorator. I found this by searching the code. I didn't open Frappe's own source, so the enforcement step below is from my knowledge of Frappe. **Mechanism** State-changing endpoints are declared like this: ```python @frappe.whitelist(methods=["POST"]) def some_endpoint(...): ... ``` The plain `@frappe.whitelist()` allows GET, POST, PUT and DELETE. Passing `methods=["POST"]` records an allow-list of HTTP verbs for that function. When a request comes in to `/api/method/<dotted.path>`, Frappe's request handler checks the request verb against that list before it runs the function. If the verb isn't listed, such as a GET, Frappe rejects the call with a 405 "Method Not Allowed" (`frappe.PermissionError`/`NotAllowed`-style error). A GET therefore can't change data through a link, an `<img>` tag or a cross-site GET. The `frappe.call` client in `rfq.js` uses `type: "POST"`, so it matches the restriction. **Concrete endpoints with `methods=["POST"]`** - `erpnext/setup/doctype/company/company.py` - `add_node` - `create_transaction_deletion_request` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/lead.py` - `add_lead_to_prospect` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/lead/mapper.py` - `make_lead_from_communication` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/opportunity.py` - `set_multiple_status` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/opportunity/mapper.py` - `make_opportunity_from_communication` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/employee/employee.py` - `create_user` - `erpnext/setup/doctype/department/department.py` - `add_node` - `erpnext/assets/doctype/location/location.py` - `add_node` - `erpnext/quality_management/doctype/quality_procedure/quality_procedure.py` - `add_node` - `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/mapper.py` - `make_work_orders` - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` - two POST-only endpoints - `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py` - one POST-only endpoint, near line 1654 - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py` - one POST-only endpoint, near line 658 - `erpnext/projects/doctype/task/task.py` - three POST-only endpoints - `erpnext/projects/doctype/project/project.py` - When raw materials for a Work Order are transferred in several partial batches (e.g. via pick lists that could only be partially fulfilled), how does ERPNext compute the work order's "material transferred for manufacturing" progress, and what prevents transferring more material than the work order requires? Name the relevant functions and files.

final answer
The "material transferred for manufacturing" figure is recomputed from the submitted Stock Entries each time. A separate check caps it at the work order qty plus an allowance. I read the code but did not run it. ## How the progress is computed The work is split across three places. **1. `WorkOrder.update_work_order_qty`** (`manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py`, `StatusService`) - It loops over `_QTY_PURPOSES`, which pairs "Material Transfer for Manufacture" with `material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. `_update_qty_for_purpose` handles each pair. - `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty` sums `fg_completed_qty` over all submitted (`docstatus == 1`) Stock Entries for that work order and purpose. Only non-additional-transfer entries are counted (`_stock_entry_filter`). - `_update_qty_for_purpose` saves that sum with `db_set`. - Each partial transfer therefore adds its own `fg_completed_qty` to the total. The field is never incremented by hand. **2. `RequiredItemsService`** (`manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`) - `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` runs `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)`. This sums the actual per-item quantities transferred. - It writes each result to `required_items.transferred_qty`. - It then calls `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. - `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` does the same recompute without touching the per-row quantities or stock reservations. `StatusService` calls it so the Not Started / In Process decision uses fresh data. **3. `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`** handles the partial pick list case: - If the work order uses operations with `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns early. The Job Card flow sets the field itself, in `job_card.py` (`get_transferred_qty_from_stock_entry`). - If the sum of `fg_completed_qty` is non-zero (direct Stock Entries or excess transfers), it uses that sum. - Pick list transfers set `fg_completed_qty = 0`, so the sum is zero. It then uses a minimum-fraction approach: - For each required item, it divides the quantity transferred by the quantity required. - It takes the smallest of those fractions and caps it at 1.0. - The result is `min_fraction × work_order.qty`. - The smallest fraction is used so that one item arriving in full does not mark the order as fully transferred while another item is still short. ## What prevents over-transfer - **`StatusService._update_qty_for_purpose`** is the hard cap. If the summed quantity exceeds `qty + allowance% × qty`, it throws `StockOverProductionError` with the message "… cannot be greater than planned quantity …". The cap is checked when the work order quantities are updated, which happens when a Stock Entry is submitted. `_qty_allowance` picks the allowance: first `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order`, then, for transfers, `transfer_extra_materials_percentage` from Manufacturing Settings. - **`WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty`** (`work_order.py`) covers extra transfers. `update_work_order_qty` calls it when `additional_transferred_qty` is set. It throws if `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + additional_transferred_qty` exceeds `qty × (1 + transfer_extra_materials_percentage/100)`. - **`get_pending_raw_materials`** (`stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`) limits what a new Stock Entry proposes: - `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` works out `pending_to_issue = required_qty - transferred_qty` per item. - `_is_overproduction_allowed` checks the allowance. - `_resolve_transfer_qty` returns the pending quantity when the desired quantity can't be transferred, and 0 when nothing is pending. - If nothing is left, it shows "All items have already been transferred for this Work Order." - **Pick list candidates** are filtered in `pick_list.py` (around line 1350) with `wo.qty > wo.material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. Work orders that are already fully transferred don't appear.

final answer
**Computing "Material Transferred for Manufacturing"** I read the code that computes this and the validation that caps it. I didn't read `_update_qty_for_purpose`, `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty` or `validate_additional_transferred_qty` in full, so what I say about them comes from their names, call sites and comments. 1. When a "Material Transfer for Manufacture" Stock Entry is submitted or cancelled, `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.update_job_card_and_work_order` runs. It lives in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py`. - It calls `wo_doc.run_method("update_work_order_qty")`. - That is `StatusService.update_work_order_qty` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py`. It loops over `_QTY_PURPOSES` and calls `_update_qty_for_purpose`. - It also calls `validate_additional_transferred_qty` on the work order if `additional_transferred_qty` is set. - Item-level transferred quantities are tracked separately, in `required_items[].transferred_qty`. 2. The partial-batch case is handled by `RequiredItemsService.recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing(transferred_items)` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py`. Its callers are `update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` and `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. It works in this order: - If the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns early. Job Card transfers use the minimum completed quantity across operations instead. - Otherwise it calls `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty("Material Transfer for Manufacture", ...)`. This is the sum of `fg_completed_qty` over the submitted transfer entries. If the sum is non-zero, it is written with `db_set`. This path covers direct stock entries and excess-transfer tracking. - Pick-list entries set `fg_completed_qty = 0`, so the sum is zero and it falls through to the item-level calculation. It builds `required_by_item` from the required items that are included in manufacturing. For each item it divides the actual transferred quantity by the required quantity. It takes the minimum of those fractions and caps it at 1.0. - The result, `min_fraction * work_order.qty`, is saved as `material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. - The minimum means a partly fulfilled pick list can't mark the work order as fully transferred while any one item is still short. **Preventing over-transfer** - `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.validate_component_and_quantities` calls `_validate_no_excess_transfer`, both in `material_transfer.py`. - For each required item, `_validate_no_excess_transfer` computes the pending quantity as the sum of `required_qty - transferred_qty` across the work order's `required_items`. - It then sums the quantity of the stock entry rows that have an `s_warehouse`, matching on `item_code` or `original_item` for substitutes. - If that sum exceeds the pending quantity, it throws "Cannot transfer X of Item Y. Maximum transferable quantity is Z" with the title "Excess Material Transfer". - The check is skipped for return entries. - It is also skipped when Manufacturing Settings has `backflush_raw_materials_based_on = "Material Transferred for Manufacture"`. - Because pending quantity is `required_qty - transferred_qty`, each partial batch reduces what later batches may transfer. - The work-order-side check is `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty` in `work_order.py`. It validates any extra transfer beyond the required quantity.

final answer
ERPNext computes the progress in `required_items.py`. Two checks stop over-transfer: a cap on the work order total in `status.py`, and a per-item cap when the transfer entry is built. I read all the code below except `material_transfer.py` around line 234 and the `validate_additional_transferred_qty` body. I only saw those through grep hits. **How the progress is computed** - **Trigger:** `StatusService.update_work_order_qty` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py` runs when a Stock Entry is submitted or cancelled. For each purpose in `_QTY_PURPOSES` it calls `_update_qty_for_purpose`. - **Required-items pass:** `RequiredItemsService.update_transferred_qty_for_required_items` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/required_items.py` sets each required-item row's `transferred_qty` from submitted Stock Entries. It gets those per-item totals from `_material_transfer_qty_by_item(is_return=0)`. It then calls `recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`. - **Refresh before status:** `refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` recomputes only the work order field, without touching the rows or stock reservations. `StatusService` calls it (through `WorkOrder.refresh_material_transferred_for_manufacturing`) before deciding the status, so pick-list transfers don't leave the work order wrongly "Not Started". - **`recompute_material_transferred_for_manufacturing` has three branches: 1. If the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, it returns without setting the field. Job Card transfers are tracked separately. 2. If `StatusService.get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty("Material Transfer for Manufacture", ...)` returns a non-zero value, that value is used. It is the sum of `fg_completed_qty` over submitted, non-additional Material Transfer for Manufacture Stock Entries linked to the work order. 3. Otherwise, which is the pick-list case because those entries have `fg_completed_qty = 0`, it uses a minimum-fraction rule: - For each included item, it takes `transferred_qty / required_qty`. - It takes the minimum of those fractions and caps it at 1.0. - The field is `min_fraction * work_order.qty`, saved with `db_set`. - Partial batches therefore only count as far as the scarcest raw material allows. For example, if 50% of one item is moved, the work order shows 50% of its qty as transferred, even if other items are fully moved. **What stops over-transfer** - **Total cap:** `_update_qty_for_purpose` computes `completed_qty = work_order.qty + allowance% * qty`. If the summed transferred qty is greater than that, it throws `StockOverProductionError` ("cannot be greater than planned quantity"). The allowance comes from `_qty_allowance`, which reads Manufacturing Settings. It uses `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order`, and for transfers it falls back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage`. - **Extra transfers:** extra transfer entries are tracked separately in `additional_transferred_qty`. `update_work_order_qty` validates that field through `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty`. - **Per-item cap:** `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` limits each item when the Stock Entry is built. - `get_pending_raw_materials` uses `max_qty = wo_doc.qty`. - `_is_overproduction_allowed` and `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` scale each item by `fg_completed_qty * required_qty / max_qty`. - The cap includes the extra-transfer percentage limit (`transfer_limit_qty`). - A "Row #… Cannot transfer {qty} of Item…" error is raised near line 234 when the limit is exceeded. I did not read that block.

final answer
When raw materials go out in several partial batches, ERPNext doesn't add a running total to the work order. Each time, it recomputes `material_transferred_for_manufacturing` from all submitted Stock Entries, and it blocks the update if the total is too high. I read the code but didn't run it. **How the progress is computed** - **Entry point:** `WorkOrder.update_work_order_qty()` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/work_order.py` delegates to `StatusService.update_work_order_qty()` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/services/status.py`. The Stock Entry's `update_job_card_and_work_order` path calls it on submit and cancel. - **What it updates:** it loops over `_QTY_PURPOSES`, which is `("Manufacture", "produced_qty")`, `("Material Transfer for Manufacture", "material_transferred_for_manufacturing")` and `("Material Transfer for Manufacture", "additional_transferred_qty")`. - **Recompute, not increment:** `_update_qty_for_purpose` calls `get_transferred_or_manufactured_qty(purpose, fieldname)`. For a transfer purpose, that runs a query summing `Stock Entry.fg_completed_qty` over every Stock Entry where: - `work_order` matches this work order, - `docstatus = 1`, - `purpose` is "Material Transfer for Manufacture", - `is_additional_transfer_entry` matches the field being computed (`_stock_entry_filter`). - **Partial batches:** each partial transfer carries its own `fg_completed_qty`, so the progress is the sum of the finished-goods quantity those transfers cover. Cancelled or draft entries drop out of the sum. - **Saving:** the result is written with `doc.db_set(fieldname, qty)`. - **Job Card transfers:** if the work order has operations and `transfer_material_against == "Job Card"`, `_skip_transfer_purpose` skips this calculation. Job Card transfers are tracked in `job_card.py` instead. - **Semi-finished goods:** `update_work_order_qty` returns immediately when `track_semi_finished_goods` is set. **What stops over-transfer** 1. **Work-order total cap:** in `_update_qty_for_purpose`, the allowed amount is `qty + qty * allowance / 100`. If the recomputed total exceeds it, the code throws `StockOverProductionError` ("… cannot be greater than planned quantity …"). The allowance comes from `_qty_allowance`: Manufacturing Settings `overproduction_percentage_for_work_order`, falling back to `transfer_extra_materials_percentage` for transfers. 2. **Extra-transfer cap:** if `additional_transferred_qty` is set, `WorkOrder.validate_additional_transferred_qty()` checks that `material_transferred_for_manufacturing + additional_transferred_qty` stays within `qty + transfer_extra_materials_percentage%`. 3. **Per-item cap at validation:** `MaterialTransferForManufactureStockEntry.validate_component_and_quantities` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/material_transfer.py` calls `_validate_no_excess_transfer()` when the entry has a work order but no `fg_completed_qty`. - It builds the pending quantity per item as the sum of `required_qty - transferred_qty` over the work order's `required_items`. - It compares that with the quantity being moved out of source warehouses, matching alternative items through `original_item`. - If the quantity exceeds what's pending, it throws "Excess Material Transfer … Maximum transferable quantity is …". - It does nothing for returns or when backflush is "Material Transferred for Manufacture". 4. **BOM component check:** when `fg_completed_qty` is set, the same method runs `_check_bom_component_qty` against the BOM items, but only if Manufacturing Settings has `validate_components_quantities_per_bom` on. 5. **Suggested quantities:** `get_pending_raw_materials` and `_calculate_item_transfer_qty` pre-fill only the remaining quantity, capped by `wo_doc.qty`, so a later partial transfer starts from what's still needed.

final answer
## What `make_gl_entries` does today The function is in `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34`. Its signature is `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. **Flow when `cancel` is false** (it does nothing if `gl_map` is empty): 1. **Budget validation.** Unless Accounts Settings has `use_legacy_budget_controller` set, or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher, it runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. 2. **Dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends mirror entries for any accounting dimension configured with an automatic balancing account. It only does this when the dimension takes more than one value across the entries. 3. **Pre-checks.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` run next. 4. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`**: - **Cost-center allocation.** `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` runs for everything except Period Closing Vouchers. - **Merging.** If `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` runs (details below). - **Sign handling.** `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` always runs. It flips negative debits into credits, and the reverse, on the base, account-currency and transaction-currency fields. If `post_net_value` is set, it nets debit against credit. 5. **Saving.** - **More than one entry.** For non-Period Closing Vouchers it first calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (with `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding`, `from_repost`). It then calls `save_entries`. - **Exactly one entry.** It throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". 6. **Cancel.** When `cancel` is true, it calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)` and none of the steps above run. **`save_entries`**: - It runs `validate_cwip_accounts`, skipped when `from_repost` is set. - It calls `process_debit_credit_difference`, which handles round-off (see below). - It checks the freezing date with `check_freezing_date`. - It runs `validate_against_pcv`, skipped for Period Closing Vouchers. - For each entry it calls `validate_allowed_dimensions`, then `make_entry`. `make_entry` creates a "GL Entry", sets flags (`from_repost`, `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding`), submits it, and calls `validate_expense_against_budget`. That last call is skipped for reposts and Period Closing Vouchers. It does run for cancelled Journal Entries. **Merging and deduplication (`merge_similar_entries`)**: - Entries with `_skip_merge` are passed through untouched. - The merge key (`get_merge_properties`) is: account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - Entries with the same key are summed on debit, credit, the `*_in_account_currency` fields and the `*_in_transaction_currency` fields. `check_if_in_list` finds a match with a linear scan. - Entries whose debit and credit are both zero at company-currency precision are dropped. The exception is the Journal Entry whose own voucher_type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss", which is kept. **Round-off handling (`process_debit_credit_difference`)**: - It rounds debit and credit to the GL Entry debit precision and sums the difference. It does the same for transaction-currency amounts. - The allowance is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. - If the difference exceeds the allowance, it raises "Debit and Credit not equal…". Exchange Gain Or Loss journals are exempt. - If the difference is within the allowance but at least one unit of precision, `make_round_off_gle` adds or adjusts a round-off entry: - **Account.** It uses the company's `round_off_account`, falling back to `default_expense_account`. For opening entries it uses `round_off_for_opening`, and throws if that is missing. - **Cost center.** It uses the voucher's own `cost_center` if present, otherwise the company's `round_off_cost_center`. - **Reuse.** An existing entry on the round-off account is reused. If the remaining difference is negligible, that entry is removed instead. - **Dimensions.** `update_accounting_dimensions` fills the dimensions on the round-off entry. - It then re-checks the difference after rounding. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of `general_ledger.make_gl_entries`** (the module-level function): - **Accounts** - `sales_invoice.py` (`SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries`, ~line 1045) - `purchase_invoice.py` (~line 678), plus `purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py` - `payment_entry.py` (lines 1298 and 1316; it passes `merge_entries` and `adv_adj`) - `journal_entry.py` (~line 878) - `invoice_discounting.py` (line 190; it passes `update_outstanding="No"`) - `period_closing_voucher.py` (line 475) and `process_period_closing_voucher.py` (line 438); both pass `merge_entries=False` - `process_deferred_accounting.py` (line 54; cancel) - `deferred_revenue.py`, which has its own wrapper `make_gl_entries` (line 519) that calls the core function (lines 444 and 580) - **Stock and controllers** - `controllers/stock_controller.py` (`StockController.make_gl_entries`, line 205; it passes `from_repost`). This is the path for Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation, Subcontracting Receipt, and Landed Cost Voucher re-posting. - **Assets** - `asset.py` (line 981) - `asset_repair.py` (line 315) - `asset_capitalization.py` (line 390; it passes `merge_entries=False`) **Indirect callers** go through each document's own `make_gl_entries` method: - **Repost Accounting Ledger** (`repost_accounting_ledger.py`, lines 192–213) calls it for both normal and `from_repost=True` flows, and for cancel. - **`accounts/utils.py`** (line 1705, repost of stock vouchers) calls `voucher_obj.make_gl_entries(gl_entries=expected_gle, from_repost=True)`. - **Landed Cost Voucher** (`landed_cost_voucher.py`, lines 352–354) calls it on the linked receipts. - **`controllers/ledger_preview.py`** (line 43) calls it for the preview. - **Patches** under `patches/v12_0`, `v13_0` and `v15_0` call it as well. **Things to watch if you change the signature or merge behavior:** - **Keyword arguments.** Callers pass `cancel`, `adv_adj`, `merge_entries`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost` by keyword. Some pass `gl_map=` by name (`process_deferred_accounting`). Keep these names, or update all of the call sites above. - **Document-level wrappers.** The wrappers on SalesInvoice, PurchaseInvoice, PaymentEntry, JournalEntry and the others forward only some of these arguments, so a new parameter needs plumbing through each one. - **`process_gl_map` and `merge_similar_entries`.** `process_gl_map` is also used elsewhere, for example in `ledger_preview` and the repost comparison in `accounts/utils.py`, so a merge change affects those too. A grep for `process_gl_map` and `merge_similar_entries` will show the full set. - **Merge key changes.** Changing the key alters what the payment ledger sees, because `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs on the merged map. It also changes what Repost Accounting Ledger compares against existing entries. - **Round-off interaction.** Round-off runs after merging and relies on `gl_map[0]` for voucher_type, company and posting date. Reordering or filtering entries could break that assumption.

final answer
`make_gl_entries` is the single entry point for posting a voucher's GL map. It sits at `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34-74`. I read the code directly. The caller list comes from a grep for `make_gl_entries(` and for imports from `general_ledger`. Some of those hits are same-named methods on the document classes, and I did not check each one. ## What it does today The signature is `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. If `gl_map` is empty it does nothing. **Submit path (`cancel=False`):** 1. **Budget check.** `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()` runs unless the legacy budget controller is enabled in Accounts Settings or the voucher type is "Period Closing Voucher". 2. **Accounting dimension offsets.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends extra entries to `gl_map` in place. It does this when an accounting dimension is set to auto-balance and the entries carry more than one value for it. 3. **Validations.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` (from `services/gl_validator.py`). 4. **`process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)`:** - Unless the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher, `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits entries by Cost Center Allocation percentages. It validates the budget against the main cost center first, unless `from_repost` is set. Entries on the round-off account go to the first allocated cost center without being split. - If `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` merges and de-duplicates (details below). - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` flips negative debits or credits to the opposite side. This is done for the account, account-currency and transaction-currency fields. If an entry has `post_net_value`, it also nets the debit against the credit. 5. **Posting.** - If more than one entry remains, it calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (skipped for Period Closing Voucher), then `save_entries`. - If exactly one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". - If none remain, it does nothing. **Cancel path (`cancel=True`):** it calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)` and skips all of the above. ## Merging and de-duplication - **Merge key.** `get_merge_properties` returns account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type and advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - **How it merges.** `merge_similar_entries` builds the key with `get_merge_key` and finds a match with `check_if_in_list`. A match gets its debit and credit summed in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - **Opt-out.** Entries with `_skip_merge` are never merged. - **Zero filter.** Entries that are zero after rounding to the GL Entry debit precision are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry whose type is "Exchange Gain Or Loss". ## Round-off and balance handling This happens in `save_entries`, after merging: - `validate_cwip_accounts` runs unless `from_repost` is set. - `process_debit_credit_difference` rounds every entry to precision and computes the debit/credit difference. - The allowance is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for everything else. - If the difference exceeds the allowance, it throws "Debit and Credit not equal", except for Exchange Gain Or Loss journal entries. - If the difference is within the allowance but at least one unit of the last decimal, `make_round_off_gle` adds or adjusts a round-off entry: - It uses the company's round-off account and cost center, or the "Round Off for Opening" account when any entry has `is_opening == "Yes"`. It throws if that account isn't set. - It reuses an existing entry on the round-off account if there is one, and removes it if the remaining difference is negligible. - It clears the party and against_voucher fields and copies the accounting dimensions from the voucher. - The difference is then re-checked. - Next come `check_freezing_date` and `validate_against_pcv` (the latter skipped for Period Closing Voucher), then `validate_allowed_dimensions` for each entry. - `make_entry` creates and submits each GL Entry with flags `from_repost`, `adv_adj` and `update_outstanding`. It then runs `validate_expense_against_budget`, unless the entry is from a repost, a Period Closing Voucher, or a non-journal cancellation. ## What a change would affect **Callers that import the function directly** (these are the ones to update if the signature changes): - **Accounts:** - Sales Invoice and Purchase Invoice. - Payment Entry, which has two call sites: one passes `merge_entries`, the other passes `update_outstanding`. - Journal Entry. - Period Closing Voucher (`merge_entries=False`). - Process Period Closing Voucher (`summarize_and_post_ledger_entries`, `merge_entries=False`). - Invoice Discounting (`update_outstanding="No"`). - Process Deferred Accounting, which cancels via `gl_map=` as a keyword argument. - `deferred_revenue.py`. - Repost Accounting Ledger (`start_repost`). - `accounts/utils.py` (`repost_gle_for_stock_vouchers`). - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` (`get_accounting_ledger_preview`). - **Stock:** `controllers/stock_controller.py` is the shared path for Stock Entry, Delivery Note, Purchase Receipt, Stock Reconciliation, Landed Cost Voucher and Subcontracting Receipt. It passes `from_repost`. Those doctypes also have files with their own `make_gl_entries(` call. - **Assets:** - Asset (`asset.py`). - `asset/mapper.py` (a call site I did not read). - Asset Repair. - Asset Capitalization (`merge_entries=False`, `from_repost`). - **Patches:** `patches/v12_0/repost_stock_ledger_entries_for_target_warehouse.py`, `patches/v13_0/modify_invalid_gain_loss_gl_entries.py` and `patches/v15_0/repost_gl_entries_with_no_account_subcontracting.py`. **Keyword arguments to watch.** Callers pass `cancel`, `adv_adj`, `merge_entries`, `update_outstanding`, `from_repost` and `gl_map=`. Renaming or reordering any of these breaks callers. **Merge behavior risks:** - Changing `merge_similar_entries` or `get_merge_properties` changes the GL Entries of every voucher. - It also changes the Payment Ledger Entries, because `create_payment_ledger_entry` is fed the merged map. - Reports that rely on GL Entry rows would see different rows. A cost center allocation split or a round-off entry placed before or after merging gives different results. - `process_gl_map` is also called from elsewhere, so check its other callers before changing it. **Other behaviors to preserve:** - Period Closing Voucher exemptions (budget check, cost center allocation, payment ledger entry, `validate_against_pcv`). - The Exchange Gain Or Loss exceptions. - The cancel path, which is separate from the submit path.

final answer
## What `make_gl_entries` does today The signature is `make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)`. It is in `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34`. It does nothing if `gl_map` is empty. **Cancel path (`cancel=True`)** - It skips every check below and calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. **Submit path** 1. **Budget validation.** It runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()`. It does this only when `use_legacy_budget_controller` is off and the voucher type is not Period Closing Voucher. 2. **Offsetting entries.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` adds accounting-dimension offsetting entries. 3. **Pre-save validations.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` run next. 4. **Processing.** `process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)` does three things: - Unless the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher, `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits entries across cost centers. - If `merge_entries` is true, `merge_similar_entries` runs (details below). - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` always runs. It flips negative debits to credits and negative credits to debits. This happens separately for the base, account-currency and transaction-currency fields. If `post_net_value` is set, it nets debit against credit. 5. **Entry count check.** If more than one entry remains: - For non-Period Closing Vouchers, `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs. It receives `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. - `save_entries` then runs. If exactly one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". **Merging and deduplication (`merge_similar_entries`)** - Entries with `_skip_merge` are kept as they are. - Other entries get a merge key from `get_merge_properties`. The key fields are: - account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no - against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no - advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no - every accounting dimension - `check_if_in_list` finds an earlier entry with the same key. The debit and credit amounts are then summed into that entry, in all three currency variants (base, account and transaction). - Afterwards, entries whose debit and credit both round to zero are dropped. The one exception is a Journal Entry of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss". The rounding precision comes from the GL Entry `debit` field in the company currency. **`save_entries`** - If `from_repost` is false, it runs `validate_cwip_accounts`. - It then calls `process_debit_credit_difference`, which is the round-off handling. - It runs `check_freezing_date`, and `validate_against_pcv` for non-Period Closing Vouchers. - For each entry, it runs `validate_allowed_dimensions` and then `make_entry`. - `make_entry` creates and submits a GL Entry with these flags: - `ignore_permissions` - `from_repost` - `adv_adj` - `update_outstanding` - `notify_update = False` - `ignore_links`, set for cancelled entries or when the ledger is immutable - Unless it is a repost or a Period Closing Voucher, it then runs `validate_expense_against_budget`. **Round-off (`process_debit_credit_difference`)** - It rounds each entry's debit and credit to the company-currency precision and sums the difference. It does the same for the transaction-currency amounts. - The allowance (`get_debit_credit_allowance`) is `5/10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry. It is 0.5 for every other voucher type. - If the difference is larger than the allowance, it raises `raise_debit_credit_not_equal_error`. Exchange Gain Or Loss journal entries are exempt. - If the difference is at least one unit of the last decimal place but within the allowance, it calls `make_round_off_gle`. That function: - Picks the account from `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`. When any entry is opening, it uses "Round Off for Opening" instead, and throws if that is not set. - Reuses an existing round-off row if one is already in `gl_map`. It removes that row if the difference then nets to zero. Period Closing Vouchers skip this reuse. - Otherwise it builds a new entry with: - the first entry's voucher fields - the round-off cost center - no party and no against_voucher - dimensions copied via `update_accounting_dimensions` - After that, it checks the difference again against the allowance. ## What a change would affect **Direct callers of the module-level function** - Accounts: - `SalesInvoice.make_gl_entries` (`sales_invoice.py:1030`) - `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` (`purchase_invoice.py:671`) - `PaymentEntry.make_gl_entries` (`payment_entry.py:1292`). It passes `cancel`, `adv_adj` and `merge_entries`, and has a second call at line 1316 that passes `update_outstanding`. - `JournalEntry.make_gl_entries` (`journal_entry.py:866`) - `InvoiceDiscounting` (passes `update_outstanding="No"`) - `PeriodClosingVoucher` and `ProcessPeriodClosingVoucher` (both use `merge_entries=False`) - `ProcessDeferredAccounting` (`cancel=1`) - `deferred_revenue.py` (its own wrapper plus two call sites) - Assets: - `Asset` - `AssetRepair` - `AssetCapitalization` (`merge_entries=False`, `from_repost`) - Stock: - `StockController.make_gl_entries` (`stock_controller.py:178`) is the base for the stock documents below. It calls with `from_repost`. - The stock documents are Stock Entry, Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Stock Reconciliation, Subcontracting Receipt and Landed Cost Voucher. - These also build their GL maps through the `gl_composer.py` and `base_stock_gl_composer.py` services, which import `process_gl_map`. That function is affected too if you change its merge behavior. **Indirect callers through the per-document `make_gl_entries` methods** - `repost_accounting_ledger.py` calls these methods for submit, cancel and repost. - `accounts/utils.py:1705` does the same, with `from_repost=True`. - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` calls them for the ledger preview. - `asset/mapper.py` calls them on Journal Entry. - Patches under `v12_0`, `v13_0` and `v15_0` also call them. **Other risks** - The wrappers have different signatures, such as `cancel=1` and positional `make_gl_entries(1)`. Changing the argument order or names would break the positional calls. - Tests also call the function and `process_gl_map` (for example `test_journal_entry.py`). - Changing the merge keys or the zero-entry filter changes: - the number of GL Entries created - the Payment Ledger Entries, since `create_payment_ledger_entry` runs after merging - round-off amounts - budget validation, which receives the unmerged map - The `merge_entries=False` callers (Period Closing Voucher, Process Period Closing Voucher, Asset Capitalization) depend on the entries staying unmerged. The `_skip_merge` flag has the same dependency.

final answer
`make_gl_entries(gl_map, cancel=False, adv_adj=False, merge_entries=True, update_outstanding="Yes", from_repost=False)` is at `erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py:34`. It is the single entry point that turns a voucher's list of GL dicts into saved GL Entry documents. I read that file in full. The caller list comes from a grep for imports and calls, so I haven't opened each caller or counted its arguments. ## What it does today **Flow when `cancel=False` and `gl_map` is non-empty** 1. **Budget validation.** It runs `BudgetValidation(gl_map).validate()` unless Accounts Settings has `use_legacy_budget_controller` on or the voucher is a Period Closing Voucher. 2. **Dimension offsetting.** `make_acc_dimensions_offsetting_entry` appends balancing entries for accounting dimensions that have an offsetting account. It only does this when the dimension takes more than one value in the map. 3. **Period and account checks.** `validate_accounting_period` and `validate_disabled_accounts` run next. 4. **Processing.** `process_gl_map(gl_map, merge_entries, from_repost)` does three things in order: - `distribute_gl_based_on_cost_center_allocation` splits entries across the sub-cost-centers of an active Cost Center Allocation. It skips this for Period Closing Voucher. It validates expense against budget unless `from_repost`. The round-off account is moved to the main cost center instead of being split. - `merge_similar_entries`, only if `merge_entries` is true (see below). - `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`, which turns negative amounts into the opposite side. 5. **Saving.** - If more than one entry remains, it calls `create_payment_ledger_entry` (skipped for Period Closing Voucher) and then `save_entries`. - If exactly one entry remains, it throws "Incorrect number of General Ledger Entries found…". - If the map is empty after processing, it does nothing. **Cancel path.** With `cancel=True` it skips all of the above and calls `make_reverse_gl_entries(gl_map, adv_adj, update_outstanding)`. **Validations inside `save_entries`** - `validate_cwip_accounts`, unless `from_repost`. - `process_debit_credit_difference`, which handles balancing and round-off (see below). - `check_freezing_date`. - `validate_against_pcv`, which rejects posting into a closed period and checks opening entries. It is skipped for Period Closing Voucher. - `validate_allowed_dimensions` for each entry. - `make_entry` for each entry, which creates and submits a `GL Entry` with the flags `adv_adj`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. **Merging and deduplication** - The merge key from `get_merge_properties` is: account, cost_center, party, party_type, voucher_detail_no, against_voucher, against_voucher_type, project, finance_book, voucher_no, advance_voucher_type, advance_voucher_no, plus all accounting dimensions. - Entries with `_skip_merge` are never merged. - Matching entries are found by `check_if_in_list`. Their debit and credit are summed in company currency, account currency and transaction currency. - After merging, entries whose debit and credit are both zero at the GL precision are dropped. The exception is a Journal Entry of type "Exchange Gain Or Loss", which keeps its zero entries. - Negative handling in `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`: if debit and credit are both negative and equal, both are sign-flipped. A negative debit is moved to credit, and a negative credit is moved to debit. Entries with `post_net_value` are netted to one side. **Round-off (`process_debit_credit_difference`, `get_debit_credit_difference`, `make_round_off_gle`)** - Debit and credit are rounded to GL Entry precision and summed, in company currency and in transaction currency. - The allowed difference is `5 / 10^precision` for Journal Entry and Payment Entry, and 0.5 for every other voucher type. - A difference above the allowance throws "Debit and Credit not equal…". Exchange Gain Or Loss journals are exempt. - A difference at or above one unit of precision but within the allowance calls `make_round_off_gle`. The balance is re-checked afterwards. - `make_round_off_gle` reads the company's round_off_account (falling back to default_expense_account), round_off_cost_center and round_off_for_opening, via `get_round_off_account_and_cost_center`. It throws if the account or cost center is missing. - If the entries are opening entries, it uses `round_off_for_opening` and throws if that isn't set. The round-off entry is marked `is_opening = "Yes"`. - It reuses an existing round-off entry in the map, adjusting it or removing it if the net becomes zero. Otherwise it appends a new entry. The new entry copies voucher_type, voucher_no, company, posting_date and remarks from the first entry, and gets its accounting dimensions from `update_accounting_dimensions`. - Party and against-voucher fields on the round-off entry are cleared. ## What a change would affect **Same module (`erpnext/accounts/general_ledger.py`)** - `process_gl_map`, `merge_similar_entries`, `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative`, `save_entries`, `process_debit_credit_difference` and `make_round_off_gle` are the internals. - `process_gl_map` is also called from other places. `process_debit_credit_difference` is also called from `erpnext/accounts/utils.py`. `toggle_debit_credit_if_negative` is also used by `repost_gle_for_stock_vouchers` in `erpnext/accounts/utils.py`. Changing merge behavior at that level would reach these callers too. **Accounts doctypes** - Sales Invoice, Purchase Invoice, Journal Entry, Payment Entry - Period Closing Voucher and Process Period Closing Voucher - Invoice Discounting - Repost Accounting Ledger, Process Deferred Accounting - `erpnext/accounts/deferred_revenue.py`, which has its own wrapper also named `make_gl_entries` - `erpnext/accounts/utils.py`, in the stock-voucher reposting code **Stock and controllers** - Stock Entry, Stock Reconciliation, Purchase Receipt, Delivery Note, Landed Cost Voucher - Subcontracting Receipt - `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`, where `StockController.make_gl_entries` is the shared path for stock vouchers - `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py`, which builds the ledger preview **Assets** - Asset, with the `mapper.py` module - Asset Repair - Asset Capitalization **Patches** - `v15_0/repost_gl_entries_with_no_account_subcontracting`, `v13_0/modify_invalid_gain_loss_gl_entries` and `v12_0/repost_stock_ledger_entries_for_target_warehouse` call it. **Method overrides.** Many document classes define their own `make_gl_entries` method. The codegraph tool shows eight `AccountsController` implementations, including `PaymentEntry`, `StockController`, `InvoiceDiscounting` and `PeriodClosingVoucher`. These methods call the module function. Some are also invoked from outside, such as the repost and cancel flows. Those methods are not the same function, but they are the direct callers. **Checks before changing** - **Signature.** Search for positional callers of `adv_adj`, `merge_entries`, `update_outstanding` and `from_repost`. Some of these pass `merge_entries=False` or `from_repost=True`. - **Merge key and zero-filtering.** Changing these alters the entries seen by `create_payment_ledger_entry`, the payment-ledger and outstanding updates, and reposting. - **Round-off.** The allowance of 0.5 versus the tight 5-cent allowance, and the opening-entry round-off account, are validated per voucher type.

final answer
No, the report doesn't break. It skips that group value and carries on. The code is in `get_data()` in `erpnext/controllers/trends.py`, which all the Trends reports share. When both "Group By" and "Based On" are set, `get_data()` works in three steps: 1. **Main rows.** It runs the main query (`data1`), which returns one row per based-on key. Each row gets a blank cell inserted at the group-by column index, and the row is appended to `data`. 2. **Distinct group values.** For each based-on row, it runs a `SELECT DISTINCT(sel_col)` query (`row`). This finds the group-by values (Item, Customer or Supplier) that go with that based-on key. 3. **Detail rows.** For each distinct group value, it runs a detail query (`row1`). The query filters on both the group-by column and the based-on key and groups by currency and `sel_col`. If the detail query for one group value returns nothing, the code hits this check: ```python if not row1: continue ``` The `continue` skips only that group value. No detail row is appended for it, and nothing indexes into `row1[0]`, so there is no `IndexError`. The loop moves on to the next group value, and then the next based-on row. After the loop, `calculate_total_row` still runs and appends the total row. The report therefore shows fewer detail lines for that based-on row, but it doesn't crash. The `if not row1: continue` guard is what prevents the crash. Without it, `row1[0][0]` and `row1[0][j]` would raise an `IndexError` on an empty result. I only read the code. I didn't run the report.

final answer
No, the report doesn't break. The shared `get_data()` in `erpnext/controllers/trends.py` skips that case. When `group_by` is set, `get_data()` runs three queries: 1. **Main query (`data1`).** It runs the based-on query for each based-on row, grouped by `conditions["group_by"]`. The code appends each row to `data` with a blank cell inserted at the group-by column index. 2. **Distinct query (`row`).** For each based-on row it selects the distinct group values (`sel_col`, such as item, customer or supplier). It filters on `based_on_key = data1[d][0]`. 3. **Detail query (`row1`).** For each distinct group value it fetches the per-period figures. It filters on both `sel_col = row[i][0]` and `based_on_key = data1[d][0]`. If the detail query `row1` returns nothing, the loop hits this guard (lines 210–211): ```python if not row1: continue ``` That group value is skipped. No detail row is appended, and the code doesn't reach `row1[0][0]` or `row1[0][j]`, so there is no `IndexError`. The skip is silent: the user sees no empty or zero-filled row for that pair. The based-on row from step 1 is still in the output. The grand total row is built from `data1`, the main-query results, by `calculate_total_row(data1, ...)`. Skipped detail rows therefore don't change the totals.

Only non-empty results are written into the row. `des` is a blank list sized to the number of columns, and it is filled with the group value, the currency and the period values. Q08Group B — Caller/callee trace + impact analysisWhen ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" acro…
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, how does the system compute the "requested amount" across multiple matching line items, and why does the placement of rate relative to the aggregate Sum() function matter for correctness?

Baseline (no tools) 75.0%

turns: 2
tool calls: 1
cost: $0.0284
elapsed: 14.2s
inputoutputcache createcache readtotal
41,0182,55012,01415,586
Grep×1
  1. #1Grep
    {"pattern": "def get_requested_amount|def get_ordered_amount|def get_actual_expense", "path": "/benchmarks/dataset/repos/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py", "output_mode": "content", "-n": true, "-A": 40}
    → 689:def get_requested_amount(params): 690- item_code = params.get("item_code") 691- 692- child = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request Item") 693- parent = frappe.qb.DocType("Material Request") 694- 695- data = ( 696- frappe.qb.from_(child) 697- .join(parent) 698- .on(parent.name == child.parent) 6…
final answer
**How the requested amount is computed** The code is `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`. Its Material Request check works like this: - It joins `Material Request Item` (child) to `Material Request` (parent). It filters on these conditions: - the item code matches - `docstatus == 1` - `child.stock_qty > child.ordered_qty`, so only lines with a quantity still unordered count - `material_request_type == "Purchase"` - `status != "Stopped"` - the conditions from `get_other_condition`: the same `expense_account`, the optional budget-against dimension (cost center or project), and a `schedule_date` inside the fiscal-year range - It then runs one SQL aggregate over all matching lines: `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - Each line contributes its pending quantity multiplied by its own rate. The lines are added together, and `Coalesce` returns 0 when nothing matches. The Purchase Order side is `get_ordered_amount`. It sums `child.amount - child.billed_amt` over lines where `amount > billed_amt`, with the same kind of filters. It uses the date field `transaction_date` and skips Closed orders. **Why the placement of rate matters** The code comment says: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid." - **Correct arithmetic:** Different lines can have different rates. The right total is Σ(pending_qty × rate), which is `Sum(qty * rate)`. Writing `Sum(qty) * rate` would multiply the total quantity by a single rate. That rate would be one arbitrary row's value, or an error. The result would be wrong whenever matching lines have different rates. - **PostgreSQL validity:** `rate` outside `Sum()` is a non-aggregated column in an aggregate query with no `GROUP BY`. PostgreSQL rejects that. MariaDB/MySQL accepts it without `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` and silently picks an indeterminate row's rate. Keeping `rate` inside the aggregate avoids both the wrong number and the error. I did not look at how the caller combines this amount with the other budget figures.

    → {"count":8,"file_count":5,"query":"budget requested amount material request purchase order Sum qty rate","results":[{"file":"/benchmarks/dataset/repos/erpnext/erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py","source":"540: def compare_expense_with_budge…
final answer
When ERPNext checks a Material Request or Purchase Order against a Budget, it computes the requested amount in SQL. Each matching Material Request Item contributes (open quantity × its own rate), and the database sums those per-line products. The rate has to sit inside `Sum()` so that each line is priced at its own rate. **How the requested amount is computed** There are two implementations. I read both in the code but did not run either. - **Older path:** `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (lines 689–714). - It joins `Material Request Item` to `Material Request` and selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - The filters are: - same `item_code` - `docstatus == 1` - `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only lines with something still open count - `material_request_type == "Purchase"` - `status != "Stopped"` - the conditions from `get_other_condition`: same expense account, same budget-against dimension, and a `schedule_date` within the fiscal year - A code comment on the select says: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Newer path:** `BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` (lines 239–271). - It uses the same idea: `Sum((IfNull(mri.stock_qty,0) - IfNull(mri.ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(mri.rate,0))`. - It is keyed by (dimension type, dimension, GL account) and filtered by company, `docstatus` 1, Purchase type, not Stopped, the fiscal-year date range, and the item codes in the current document. - The result is stored in `to_validate[key].requested_amount`. `compare_expense_with_budget` then uses the result: - For a Material Request, the amount is `requested_amount + ordered_amount`. - For a Purchase Order, the amount is `ordered_amount`. - It adds that amount to `actual_expense` and compares the total with the budget amount. - If the total exceeds the budget, it stops or warns. **Why the placement of rate matters** - **Correctness:** - Matching lines can have different rates. - `Sum(qty * rate)` gives the sum of each line's qty × rate, which is the real committed value. - Writing `Sum(qty) * rate` is wrong. It multiplies the total quantity by a single rate, and that rate belongs to only one of the lines. - The same applies to `Sum(qty) * Max(rate)` or any other single-row rate. It over- or under-states the requested amount, so the budget check could pass when it should fail, or the other way round. - **PostgreSQL validity:** - If `rate` sits outside the aggregate, it becomes a bare non-aggregated column in a grouped query. - PostgreSQL rejects that unless the column is in `GROUP BY`. MySQL/MariaDB would silently return an arbitrary row's rate. - With rate inside `Sum()`, the query is valid on both databases and deterministic. The `Coalesce` and `IfNull` wrappers handle two cases. One is no matching rows, where `Sum` returns NULL and the result becomes 0. The other is NULL quantities or rates, which are treated as 0.

final answer
**How the requested amount is computed** ERPNext has two implementations. Both sum the outstanding value of every matching Material Request line in a single SQL aggregate, so there is no per-row loop in Python. 1. `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py` (around L689): - It joins `Material Request Item` (child) to `Material Request` (parent). - It filters on the following: - the item code - `docstatus == 1` - `stock_qty > ordered_qty`, so only lines with something still outstanding count - `material_request_type == "Purchase"` - `status != "Stopped"` - the extra dimension conditions from `get_other_condition` - It selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - It returns `data[0][0]`, or 0 if there are no rows. 2. `BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py` (around L239): - It uses the same join and filters. - It also filters on company and on `transaction_date` within the fiscal year range. - It filters on `expense_account`, taken from `key[2]`. - It filters on the dimension column and value from `key`, which has the shape `(dimension_type, dimension, GL account)`. - For Purchase Order and Material Request documents it also restricts to the item codes on the current document. - It selects `Sum((IfNull(stock_qty,0) - IfNull(ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(rate,0))`. - It stores the result in `self.to_validate[key].requested_amount` as `amount or 0`. So the requested amount is the sum over matching lines of (stock quantity − already ordered quantity) × rate. Each line is the remaining quantity valued at its own rate. **Why the placement of rate relative to Sum() matters** - **Correct math:** the rate has to be inside the aggregate, as in `Sum((qty - ordered) * rate)`. Each line's outstanding quantity is then multiplied by that line's own rate before the lines are added. The code comment says the same: "rate inside the aggregate: Sum(qty * rate) is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid". - **Wrong result if outside:** `Sum(qty - ordered) * rate` multiplies the total quantity by a single rate. That rate is either an arbitrary one or a grouped one, because a bare `rate` column is not aggregated. Lines with different rates are then valued wrongly, which gives a wrong total and a wrong budget check. - **PostgreSQL validity:** PostgreSQL rejects a non-aggregated column in a select list alongside an aggregate without a `GROUP BY`. MariaDB tolerates it and silently returns an arbitrary row's rate. With the rate inside `Sum()`, the whole expression is an aggregate, so the query is valid on both databases and gives the same answer. I didn't run the queries. This comes from reading the two functions.

final answer
The "requested amount" is the budget's open Material Request commitment. It is summed in SQL across every matching Material Request Item, and the rate has to sit inside the `Sum()`. **Where it's computed** There are two implementations. Both sum over all matching Material Request Item rows, not over a single line. 1. `get_requested_amount(params)` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py:689`. `compare_expense_with_budget` calls it. - It joins `Material Request Item` (child) to `Material Request` (parent). - It selects `Coalesce(Sum((child.stock_qty - child.ordered_qty) * child.rate), 0)`. - It filters on: - the item code; - `parent.docstatus == 1`; - `child.stock_qty > child.ordered_qty`, so only lines with quantity still unordered count; - `material_request_type == "Purchase"`; - `status != "Stopped"`; - the dimension and account conditions from `get_other_condition`. - It returns `data[0][0]`. 2. `BudgetValidation.get_requested_amount(key)` in `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py:239`. This is the newer controller, and it runs for each (dimension, dimension value, expense account) key. - It uses the same join and similar filters: company, submitted, Purchase type, not Stopped, the fiscal-year date range, the `expense_account` and the dimension. - It restricts to the item codes on the current document. - It selects `Sum((IfNull(mri.stock_qty,0) - IfNull(mri.ordered_qty,0)) * IfNull(mri.rate,0))`. - It stores the result in `to_validate[key].requested_amount`. - `handle_material_request_overlimit` then compares `requested_amount + current_requested_amount` against the annual budget and the accumulated monthly budget. The result is Warn or Stop, raised as `BudgetError`. The Purchase Order side works the same way. `get_ordered_amount` sums `amount - billed_amt` over Purchase Order Item rows. **Why the rate's placement relative to `Sum()` matters** The code comment in `budget.py` says it directly: "rate inside the aggregate: `Sum(qty * rate)` is the correct requested amount and is PG-valid." - **Correctness:** each line has its own rate. The correct total is the sum of each line's `(stock_qty - ordered_qty) * rate`. That is Σ(qtyᵢ × rateᵢ). - **Wrong form:** `Sum(qty) * rate` multiplies the total quantity by one rate. That would give (Σqtyᵢ) × rate, which is wrong when matching lines have different rates. - **Bare column outside the aggregate:** `Sum(qty) * child.rate` leaves `rate` as a bare column in a query with no `GROUP BY`. MySQL tolerates that and picks an arbitrary row's rate. Postgres rejects it ("column must appear in GROUP BY or be used in an aggregate"). - **Result of the inside form:** per-line multiplication happens before the aggregate. Each line is priced at its own rate, and the query is valid on both MySQL and Postgres. - **Null handling:** the `Coalesce` and `IfNull` wrappers turn a null sum or null columns into 0. An empty match therefore gives 0, not `None`.

final answer
`POS Invoice.reset_mode_of_payments` rebuilds the payments table from the POS Profile by calling the shared helper `update_multi_mode_option`. That helper uses `doc.get()` for the Sales-Invoice-only field, so it doesn't raise on a POS Invoice. **`reset_mode_of_payments`** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py:796`) - It is a whitelisted method, so the client can call it. - It runs only if `self.pos_profile` is set. - It loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. - It calls `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`, then sets `self.paid_amount = 0`. - `SalesInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` (`sales_invoice.py:723`) does the same thing. So does the POS service class method at `services/pos.py:173`. **`update_multi_mode_option`** (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py:339`) 1. It computes `mop_refetched`, which is true when the doc already has payment rows and `is_created_using_pos` is not set. 2. It clears the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 3. It collects the `mode_of_payment` of every row in `pos_profile.payments`. 4. It calls `get_mode_of_payments_info(mode_of_payments, doc.company)` once to fetch each mode's default account and type. 5. For each profile row, it looks up that mode's info. - If there is no info (no default Cash or Bank account), it adds a link to the mode to `invalid_modes` and moves on. - Otherwise it sets `payment_mode.default = row.default` and calls the inner `append_payment`. - `append_payment` adds a row with `default`, `mode_of_payment` (from `mop`), `account` (from `default_account`) and `type`. 6. If `invalid_modes` is non-empty, it throws a "Missing Account" error listing the modes. - The message-choosing check is `invalid_modes == 1`, which compares a list to an integer. It is never true, so the plural message is always used. This looks like a bug. 7. If `mop_refetched` is true, it shows a toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **Why it doesn't break on a POS Invoice** `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. A direct attribute read could raise `AttributeError` there, and the code comment says as much. The helper reads it with `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`, which returns `None` when the field is missing. For a POS Invoice, `mop_refetched` therefore depends only on whether payment rows already exist. I read the code but didn't run it.

final answer
`POS Invoice.reset_mode_of_payments` rebuilds the payments table from the POS Profile. The helper `update_multi_mode_option` uses `doc.get()` so it doesn't fail on a doctype without `is_created_using_pos`. **Entry point.** `POS Invoice.reset_mode_of_payments` is whitelisted and lives in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (line 796). - It does nothing unless `self.pos_profile` is set. - It loads the profile with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", self.pos_profile)`. - It calls `update_multi_mode_option(self, pos_profile)`. - It then sets `paid_amount = 0`. `SalesInvoice` has the same method, delegating to `POSService.reset_mode_of_payments` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py` (line 173). **Repopulating the table.** The helper is `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)` in the same `services/pos.py` file (line 339). 1. It records whether the table already had rows (see the guard below), then clears it with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 2. It collects the `mode_of_payment` from each row in `pos_profile.payments`. It passes them with `doc.company` to `get_mode_of_payments_info`, which returns each mode's default account and type. 3. For each profile row it looks up that info. If a mode has no info, it adds a link to the mode to an `invalid_modes` list and moves on. 4. For each valid mode it sets `payment_mode.default = row.default`. The inner `append_payment` then appends a payments row with `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` (from `default_account`) and `type`. 5. If any modes were invalid, it throws a "Please set default Cash or Bank account in Mode of Payment(s) …" error titled "Missing Account". 6. If the table had rows before and the doc wasn't created via POS, it shows a toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **Avoiding the break.** `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. The helper therefore reads it as `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`, not `doc.is_created_using_pos`. The full expression is `mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos")`. On a POS Invoice, `get()` returns `None` instead of raising `AttributeError`. The shared helper then works on both doctypes. There is one bug in the error branch. It tests `invalid_modes == 1`, which compares a list to an integer and is always false. The singular message is never used. It should be `len(invalid_modes) == 1`.

`reset_mode_of_payments` clears the invoice's payments table and rebuilds it from the POS Profile. It does this through the shared helper `update_multi_mode_option`. I read the source but did not run it. **Entry point** - `POSInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice/pos_invoice.py` (line 796). - `SalesInvoice.reset_mode_of_payments` delegates to `POSService(self).reset_mode_of_payments()`. That is in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/sales_invoice.py`, with the logic in `services/pos.py`. - Both versions do the same three things: 1. If `pos_profile` is set, load it with `frappe.get_cached_doc("POS Profile", pos_profile)`. 2. Call `update_multi_mode_option(doc, pos_profile)`. 3. Set `paid_amount = 0`. **How `update_multi_mode_option` repopulates the table** This is a module-level function in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/services/pos.py` (line 339). 1. It checks whether the table already had rows (`mop_refetched`, covered below). 2. It empties the table with `doc.set("payments", [])`. 3. It collects `mode_of_payment` from each row of `pos_profile.payments`. 4. It looks up each mode's account and type in one batch with `get_mode_of_payments_info(mode_of_payments, doc.company)`. 5. For each profile row, it takes the matching info and sets `payment_mode.default = row.default`. A mode with no info, meaning no default Cash or Bank account for the company, is skipped and its Mode of Payment link is recorded in `invalid_modes`. 6. It appends a new `payments` row through the inner `append_payment`. The row gets `default`, `mode_of_payment`, `account` (taken from the mode's `default_account`) and `type`. 7. If any modes were invalid, it calls `frappe.throw` with a "Please set default Cash or Bank account in Mode of Payment(s) …" message and the title "Missing Account". 8. If `mop_refetched` is true, it shows an orange toast: "Payment methods refreshed. Please review before proceeding." **How it avoids breaking on a doctype without Sales-Invoice-only fields** `is_created_using_pos` exists on Sales Invoice but not on POS Invoice. The helper therefore reads it with `doc.get("is_created_using_pos")` instead of `doc.is_created_using_pos`: ```python mop_refetched = bool(doc.payments) and not doc.get("is_created_using_pos") ``` `doc.get` returns `None` for a missing field, where attribute access would raise `AttributeError`. On a POS Invoice the flag is therefore treated as false. A reopened draft that already had payment rows gets `mop_refetched = True`, so the cashier sees the "refreshed" toast. A code comment in the helper states this reason. One thing I noticed in the invalid-modes branch: it tests `invalid_modes == 1`, which compares a list to an integer and is never true. The singular message can therefore never be chosen. It looks like it should be `len(invalid_modes) == 1`.
Q10Group B — Caller/callee trace + impact analysisHow does ERPNext compute a transaction item's final rate from its price list rate, margin, discount and applied pricing rules — an…
How does ERPNext compute a transaction item's final rate from its price list rate, margin, discount and applied pricing rules — and what happens when the user manually overrides the rate? Trace the current per-item rate calculation in the shared taxes-and-totals controller, naming the functions involved.

final answer
The per-item rate is computed in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. The class is `calculate_taxes_and_totals`, and the work is split between `calculate_item_values` and `calculate_item_rate`. I read the code but didn't run it. **Call chain** - The class's `calculate()` calls `calculate_item_values()` (line 80), and `calculate_net_total()` follows it (line 85). - `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document `is_consolidated` or `discount_amount_applied` is set. - Otherwise, for each item it: - rounds the item's float fields with `round_floats_in`, leaving valuation and incoming rate unrounded; - calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`; - sets `net_rate = rate` and `amount = rate * qty`, with special handling when qty is 0 on a return or debit note; - sets `net_amount = amount`; - calls `_set_in_company_currency` for `price_list_rate`, `rate_with_margin`, `rate`, `net_rate`, `amount` and `net_amount`. **`calculate_item_rate(item)`** (lines 166–222) 1. If there is no `price_list_rate`, it calls `remove_margin` and `remove_discount`, sets `rate_with_margin` to 0, and returns. The rate is then whatever the user entered. 2. `has_pricing_rules` is true when `item.pricing_rules` is set and the document's `ignore_pricing_rule` is off. 3. If pricing rules apply: - It first calls `remove_margin(item)`. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each rule with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule is used for margin only if it has a `margin_type` and a `margin_rate_or_amount`, and either the type is "Percentage" or the rule's currency matches the document currency. - A qualifying rule copies its `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` onto the item. If several rules qualify, the last one wins. 4. `rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`: - With no `margin_type`, it returns the price list rate. - With "Percentage", it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin/100)`. - Otherwise it returns `price_list_rate + margin`. 5. If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. 6. `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`. So the formula is `rate = price_list_rate (+ margin) − discount`. Pricing-rule discounts reach `discount_percentage` or `discount_amount` on the item before this runs. This function only reads them; the margin is the one thing it pulls from the rules itself. **Manual rate override** (the rest of `calculate_item_rate`) - If pricing rules apply, or the item has no rate, `item.rate = calculated_rate` and the function returns. A manual rate is overwritten whenever a pricing rule is active. - If `item.rate == calculated_rate`, nothing changes. - Otherwise the user's rate is kept and the margin and discount are rewritten to match it: - **Rate above the price list rate:** `margin_type` becomes "Amount" and `margin_rate_or_amount = rate − price_list_rate`. `rate_with_margin` is set to `rate`, and `remove_discount` clears the discount. - **Rate at or below the price list rate:** `rate_with_margin` is set to `price_list_rate`, and `discount_amount = price_list_rate − rate`. `discount_percentage` is set to 0, and `remove_margin` clears the margin. The helpers `remove_margin`, `remove_discount` and `get_rate_with_margin` are module-level functions near the end of the file (around lines 1172–1192).
