final answer
The per-item rate is computed in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. The class `calculate_taxes_and_totals` calls `calculate_item_values()`, which calls `calculate_item_rate(item)` for each row. **Entry point: `calculate_item_values()`** - It returns immediately if the document is consolidated (`is_consolidated`) or `discount_amount_applied` is set. - Otherwise, for each row it calls `self.doc.round_floats_in(item, ...)`, skipping `valuation_rate`, `incoming_rate` and `sales_incoming_rate`. - It then calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. - Next it sets `item.net_rate = item.rate`. - After that it computes `item.amount` from `rate × qty`. The code also handles the zero-qty return and debit-note cases (I only read the first lines of those branches). **`calculate_item_rate(item)` step by step** 1. **No price list rate.** If `item.price_list_rate` is falsy, it calls `remove_margin(item)` and `remove_discount(item)` and sets `rate_with_margin = 0`. It then returns, leaving `item.rate` as it was. 2. **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules` is `item.pricing_rules and not doc.ignore_pricing_rule`. When true: - It first calls `remove_margin(item)`. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` (from `accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py`) and loads each rule with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule's margin is copied onto `item.margin_type` and `item.margin_rate_or_amount` only if the rule has both a margin type and a margin value. The margin type must also be Percentage, or the rule's currency must match the document currency. - Rules that set only a discount do not change anything here. The pricing-rule application step has already written their `discount_percentage` or `discount_amount` onto the item. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`: - With no `margin_type`, it returns `price_list_rate`. - For Percentage, it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin_rate_or_amount / 100)`. - Otherwise, it returns `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. - Each result is rounded to the `rate_with_margin` precision. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. The percentage is applied to the margin-adjusted rate, not to the raw price list rate. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = flt(rate_with_margin - discount_amount, precision("rate"))`. 6. **Choosing between the calculated rate and the stored rate:** - If pricing rules apply, or `item.rate` is empty, then `item.rate = calculated_rate` and the function returns. The calculated rate always wins in this case. - If `item.rate == calculated_rate`, nothing changes. **Manual rate override** (a `rate` that differs from `calculated_rate` and no pricing rules apply): the user's rate is kept, and margin and discount are rewritten to match it. - **Rate above `price_list_rate`:** - `margin_type` becomes `"Amount"`. - `margin_rate_or_amount` becomes `rate - price_list_rate`. - `rate_with_margin` becomes `rate`. - `remove_discount(item)` zeroes both discount fields. - **Rate at or below `price_list_rate`:** - `rate_with_margin` becomes `price_list_rate`. - `discount_amount` becomes `price_list_rate - rate`. - `discount_percentage` becomes 0. - `remove_margin(item)` clears the margin. The helpers are `remove_margin` (sets `margin_type` to None and the margin value to 0) and `remove_discount` (sets both discount fields to 0). **Client-side counterpart** - `public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` has `apply_pricing_rule_on_item(item)`. It uses `blanket_order_rate` instead of `price_list_rate` for Sales Order and Quotation items when one is set. It then applies the same margin, then discount, then rate sequence and sets `rate` with `frappe.model.set_value`. - `public/js/controllers/transaction.js` triggers it from the `price_list_rate` and `margin_rate_or_amount` handlers. The `margin_type` handler also calls it, according to the call graph. After that, `calculate_taxes_and_totals()` runs. - For doctypes outside the listed item doctypes, the `price_list_rate` handler uses a simpler `price_list_rate * (1 - discount_percentage / 100)`. The server-side `calculate_item_rate` is the authoritative calculation and handles the manual override.

final answer
The per-item rate is computed in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class. I read the code directly and did not run it. **Call chain** - `calculate_taxes_and_totals.calculate()` (about line 46) calls `calculate_item_values()` (line 224). - `calculate_item_values()` returns early if the document `is_consolidated` or `discount_amount_applied` is already set. - Otherwise, for each item it does the following: 1. It runs `round_floats_in`. 2. It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`. 3. It sets `net_rate = rate`. 4. It sets `amount = rate * qty`. For a return or debit note with no qty, the amount is `±rate`. 5. It sets `net_amount = amount`. 6. It calls `_set_in_company_currency` to fill `base_price_list_rate`, `base_rate_with_margin`, `base_rate`, `base_net_rate`, `base_amount` and `base_net_amount`. Each value is multiplied by `conversion_rate`. **`calculate_item_rate(item)` (lines 166–222)** 1. **No price list rate.** If `item.price_list_rate` is empty, `remove_margin` and `remove_discount` clear the margin and discount, and `rate_with_margin` is set to 0. The function then returns, so the user's rate is left as typed. 2. **Pricing rules.** `has_pricing_rules` is true when `item.pricing_rules` is set and `doc.ignore_pricing_rule` is not. - In that case `remove_margin(item)` runs first. - Then `get_applied_pricing_rules` loops over the rules. Each rule is loaded with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule's margin is copied to `item.margin_type` and `item.margin_rate_or_amount` only if the rule has a margin type and value, and either the type is Percentage or the rule currency matches the document currency. 3. **Margin.** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)` (line 1182): - With no margin type, it returns `price_list_rate`. - For Percentage, it returns `price_list_rate * (1 + margin_rate_or_amount / 100)`. - Otherwise it returns `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. 4. **Discount.** If `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. If only `discount_amount` is set, it is used as is. 5. **Calculated rate.** `calculated_rate = rate_with_margin - discount_amount`, rounded to the rate precision. **What happens when the user overrides the rate** - **Rate empty, or pricing rules applied.** `item.rate = calculated_rate` and the function returns. A pricing rule therefore overrides a manual rate. - **Rate equals `calculated_rate`.** Nothing changes. - **Rate differs from `calculated_rate` and is above `price_list_rate`.** The code treats the difference as a margin: - `margin_type` becomes "Amount". - `margin_rate_or_amount` becomes `rate - price_list_rate`. - `rate_with_margin` becomes `rate`. - `remove_discount` clears the discount. - **Rate differs from `calculated_rate` and is at or below `price_list_rate`.** The code treats the difference as a discount: - `rate_with_margin` becomes `price_list_rate`. - `discount_amount` becomes `price_list_rate - rate`. - `discount_percentage` becomes 0. - `remove_margin` clears the margin. In both override cases the typed rate is kept and the margin and discount fields are rewritten to match it. The client-side `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` has a matching `calculate_taxes_and_totals`. I did not read it.

final answer
The per-item rate is computed server-side in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in the class `calculate_taxes_and_totals`. I read the code and didn't run it. The client-side JS controllers only do part of this, as noted at the end. ## Call chain `calculate_taxes_and_totals.__init__` calls `calculate()`. `calculate()` calls `_calculate()`, and `_calculate()` calls `validate_conversion_rate()` and then `calculate_item_values()`. For each item, `calculate_item_values()` does the following: 1. It returns early if the document is consolidated (`is_consolidated`) or if `discount_amount_applied` is set. 2. It rounds the item's float fields with `round_floats_in`, leaving out `valuation_rate`, `incoming_rate` and `sales_incoming_rate`. 3. It calls `calculate_item_rate(item)`, which sets `item.rate`. 4. It sets `net_rate = rate`. 5. It sets `amount = flt(rate * qty)`. If `qty` is 0, a return uses `-rate` (except on Purchase Receipt) and a debit note uses `rate`. 6. It sets `net_amount = amount`. 7. It calls `_set_in_company_currency` for `price_list_rate`, `rate_with_margin`, `rate`, `net_rate`, `amount` and `net_amount`. ## `calculate_item_rate(item)` - **No price list rate:** if `item.price_list_rate` is falsy, it calls `remove_margin(item)` and `remove_discount(item)`, sets `rate_with_margin = 0`, and returns. Whatever rate the user typed is left alone. - **Pricing rules:** `has_pricing_rules = item.pricing_rules and not doc.ignore_pricing_rule`. - If true, it calls `remove_margin` first. - It then loops over `get_applied_pricing_rules(item.pricing_rules)` and loads each rule with `frappe.get_cached_doc("Pricing Rule", ...)`. - A rule that has a `margin_type` and `margin_rate_or_amount` copies both onto the item. This applies when the margin type is Percentage, or when the rule's currency matches the document currency. - **Margin:** `item.rate_with_margin = get_rate_with_margin(item)`. - With no `margin_type`, this is just `price_list_rate`. - With Percentage, it is `price_list_rate * (1 + margin_rate_or_amount / 100)`. - Otherwise it is `price_list_rate + margin_rate_or_amount`. - **Discount:** if `discount_percentage > 0`, then `discount_amount = rate_with_margin * discount_percentage / 100`. - **Calculated rate:** `calculated_rate = flt(rate_with_margin - discount_amount, precision("rate"))`. ## Choosing between the calculated rate and a manual rate - **Pricing rules apply, or `item.rate` is 0:** `item.rate = calculated_rate` and the function returns. The calculated value always wins here. - **`item.rate == calculated_rate`:** nothing changes. - **Manual override** (the rate differs from the calculated one and no pricing rules apply): the user's rate is kept, and margin and discount are rewritten to match it. - **`rate > price_list_rate`:** `margin_type` becomes "Amount" and `margin_rate_or_amount = rate - price_list_rate`. Then `rate_with_margin = rate`, and `remove_discount(item)` zeroes `discount_percentage` and `discount_amount`. - **`rate <= price_list_rate`:** `rate_with_margin = price_list_rate` and `discount_amount = price_list_rate - rate`. `discount_percentage` is not touched in this branch. ## Helpers - `remove_margin` sets `margin_type = None` and `margin_rate_or_amount = 0`. - `remove_discount` zeroes `discount_percentage` and `discount_amount`. - `get_rate_with_margin` is the margin step above. ## Client side - In `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`, `calculate_item_values()` does not compute the rate. It only sets `net_rate = rate`, `amount`, `net_amount`, `total_weight` and the company-currency fields. - In `erpnext/public/js/controllers/transaction.js`, `price_list_rate()` calls `apply_pricing_rule_on_item` for most transaction item doctypes. Otherwise it sets `rate = price_list_rate * (1 - discount_percentage / 100)`. It then calls `calculate_taxes_and_totals()`. I didn't read `apply_pricing_rule_on_item` or `margin_rate_or_amount` in `transaction.js`.

When a Dunning is created from an overdue Sales Invoice, how is the outstanding amount on each Overdue Payment row computed, and how does this behave correctly when the invoice is in a foreign currency but posts against a receivable account in a different currency?

final answer
**Where it's computed:** `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (line 570 onward). It uses `get_mapped_doc` to map a Sales Invoice to a Dunning. **Mapping the rows** - Each Payment Schedule row becomes an Overdue Payment row. The field map copies `name` to `payment_schedule` and `parent` to `sales_invoice`. - Only rows that pass the condition are mapped: `doc.outstanding > 0 and getdate(doc.due_date) < getdate()`. - Because the other fields share names, `outstanding` is copied straight from the Payment Schedule row. That value is in the invoice's transaction currency. **Override in `postprocess_dunning`** - Before this override, the postprocess step set `row.outstanding` to the invoice's `outstanding_amount` whenever the invoice had exactly one payment schedule row, and the row's `payment_schedule` matched it. - `outstanding_amount` is stored in the party (receivable) account currency, so that is wrong when the account currency differs from the invoice currency. - The code now checks `source.party_account_currency and source.party_account_currency != source.currency`. - If the currencies differ, it sets `row.outstanding = source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. - If they match, it sets `row.outstanding = source.get("outstanding_amount")`. - The code comment says the payment schedule's outstanding is kept in transaction currency. It is updated as payments are allocated, so it stays correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates (#56006). **Multiple schedule rows:** When the invoice has more than one payment schedule row, the override is skipped. Each row keeps its own mapped `outstanding`, which is already in transaction currency. **After mapping:** `target.validate()` runs, which triggers the `Dunning` methods in `dunning.py`: - `validate_overdue_payments` computes `row.interest = row.outstanding * daily_interest * row.overdue_days`. - `validate_totals` sums the rows into `total_outstanding`. Then `grand_total = total_outstanding + dunning_amount`. - `validate_same_currency` is also part of the validation. I did not open `validate_same_currency`, so I haven't checked what it enforces.

final answer
**Where it happens:** `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (line 570 onward). It calls `get_mapped_doc` to map Sales Invoice to Dunning. I read this code and did not run it. **Default mapping.** Each Payment Schedule row of the invoice becomes an Overdue Payment row if both of these hold: - `doc.outstanding > 0` - `getdate(doc.due_date) < getdate()` The row's `outstanding` comes straight from the Payment Schedule's own `outstanding` field. The field map also sets `payment_schedule` to the schedule row name and `sales_invoice` to the parent invoice. **Override in `postprocess_dunning`.** This only applies when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row. In that case the matching Overdue Payment row (matched on `payment_schedule`) gets its `outstanding` set as follows: - If `source.party_account_currency` is set and differs from `source.currency`, then `row.outstanding = source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. - Otherwise, `row.outstanding = source.outstanding_amount`. **Why the currency check matters.** An invoice's `outstanding_amount` is held in the party (receivable) account currency. An Overdue Payment row, like the Dunning itself, is in the invoice's transaction currency. If the two currencies differ, using `outstanding_amount` would put a number in the wrong currency on the row. The Payment Schedule's `outstanding` is kept in transaction currency and is updated as payments are allocated. The code comment says this keeps it correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates (issue #56006). So in the mismatch case the code uses the schedule's outstanding and skips the account-currency figure. With several schedule rows, the override is skipped and each row keeps its own schedule `outstanding`, which is already in transaction currency. **Validation afterwards.** `target.validate()` runs `Dunning.validate` in `dunning.py`, which does the following: - `validate_same_currency` checks that every invoice's `currency` equals the Dunning `currency`. - `validate_overdue_payments` sets `overdue_days` and computes `interest = outstanding * rate_of_interest/100/365 * overdue_days`. - `validate_totals` sums `total_outstanding` and `total_interest`, then computes `grand_total = total_outstanding + dunning_amount`. All of these use the transaction-currency `outstanding`.

final answer
When a Dunning is created from an overdue Sales Invoice, each Overdue Payment row's outstanding amount comes from one of two places. Which one depends on how many payment-schedule rows the invoice has and on whether the invoice currency matches the receivable account currency. I read this in `create_dunning` and did not run it or its tests. **Where it happens:** `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (line 570). The Dunning form's "Fetch Overdue Payments" button calls it through `erpnext.utils.map_current_doc`. **Step 1: mapping.** - `get_mapped_doc` turns each Sales Invoice `Payment Schedule` row into an `Overdue Payment` row. - Only rows with `outstanding > 0` and `due_date < today` are mapped. - The mapping copies the schedule row's `name` into `payment_schedule` and the `parent` into `sales_invoice`. - Mapped rows take their `outstanding` from the schedule row's own `outstanding`, which is in the invoice's transaction currency. **Step 2: `postprocess_dunning`.** - This runs only when the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row. - It finds the Overdue Payment row whose `payment_schedule` matches that row's name and overwrites `row.outstanding`. - If `party_account_currency` is set and differs from the invoice `currency`, it uses `source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding`. - Otherwise it uses the invoice's `outstanding_amount`. - It then calls `target.validate()`. **Why the currency check:** - The Sales Invoice's `outstanding_amount` is in the party (receivable) account currency. - The Overdue Payment row, like the Dunning, is in the invoice's transaction currency. - If the two currencies differ, copying `outstanding_amount` would put a number in the wrong currency. - The payment schedule's `outstanding` is kept in transaction currency and is reduced as payments are allocated. So it stays correct even when the invoice and its payments post at different exchange rates. The code comment cites issue #56006. **Afterwards:** `Dunning.validate` (`dunning.py`) runs `validate_same_currency`, `validate_overdue_payments`, `validate_totals`, `set_party_details` and `set_dunning_level`. `validate_totals` sets `total_outstanding` to the sum of the rows' `outstanding`.

final answer
When a Dunning is created from an overdue Sales Invoice, each Overdue Payment row's outstanding amount comes from the Payment Schedule row it maps from, with one override in `postprocess_dunning`. That override uses the invoice's `outstanding_amount` when the invoice has a single schedule row and its currency matches the receivable account's. I read the code but didn't run it or check its tests. **Where it happens:** `create_dunning` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/sales_invoice/mapper.py` (line 570). The Dunning form's "Fetch Overdue Payments" button calls it through `erpnext.utils.map_current_doc`. **How the rows are built:** 1. `get_mapped_doc` maps each Sales Invoice `Payment Schedule` row to an `Overdue Payment` row. - It only maps rows where `outstanding > 0` and `due_date < today`. - `name` becomes `payment_schedule` and `parent` becomes `sales_invoice`. - The Payment Schedule's own `outstanding` field is copied to the row's `outstanding`. That value is in the invoice's transaction currency and is updated as payments are allocated. 2. `postprocess_dunning` then adjusts the amount, but only if the invoice has exactly one payment schedule row (lines 586–597). It finds the Overdue Payment row whose `payment_schedule` matches that single row and sets `row.outstanding` as follows: - **Same currency:** if `party_account_currency` is empty or equal to the invoice `currency`, it uses `source.outstanding_amount`. That is the invoice's whole remaining balance, which includes anything not tied to the schedule, such as returns or write-offs. - **Different currency:** if `party_account_currency` differs from `currency`, it uses `source.payment_schedule[0].outstanding` instead. 3. `target.validate()` then runs. The Dunning's `validate_same_currency` rejects any invoice whose currency differs from the Dunning's. `validate_overdue_payments` computes `interest = outstanding * rate_of_interest/100/365 * overdue_days`. `validate_totals` sums `total_outstanding` and builds `grand_total`. **Why the currency check matters:** the Sales Invoice's `outstanding_amount` is stored in the receivable account's currency (`party_account_currency`). The Overdue Payment row and the Dunning are in the invoice's transaction currency. Suppose the invoice is in USD but posts to an EUR receivable account. Copying `outstanding_amount` straight into the row would put EUR figures in a USD field, and the interest and totals would be wrong. The code comment cites issue #56006, where payments post at different exchange rates. The Payment Schedule `outstanding` is already in transaction currency, so using it avoids that mismatch. The row, the interest and the Dunning totals then stay consistent with the Dunning's currency. **Multi-row invoices:** with more than one payment schedule row, the override is skipped. Each row keeps its own Payment Schedule `outstanding`, which is already in transaction currency.

Each of the three whitelisted functions is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/item/item.py`. Each starts with an explicit `frappe.has_permission(...)` check and raises before it does any work. I read the guards and the start of each function, not the rest of the opening-stock function. 1. **`get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`** is the attribute autocomplete for the child table. - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item")`. With no `ptype` given, that checks the default `read` permission on the Item doctype. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("No Permission"))`. - Only after that does it run `frappe.get_all("Item Attribute Value", ...)`, filtered by `parent` and by `attribute_value LIKE %…%`. - The check is on the Item doctype, not on a specific document. 2. **`get_item_prices(item_code)`** feeds the item prices tab preview. - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read")`, so it checks read access on the Item Price doctype, not on Item. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - Only after that does it build the `frappe.qb` query on Item Price. - That query is a raw query-builder query, which does not apply permissions itself. The up-front check is therefore the only guard on this call. 3. **`make_opening_stock_entry(item_code, company, qty, valuation_rate, warehouse=None)`** creates the opening stock. - It calls `frappe.has_permission("Item", "write", item_code)`, which checks write access on that specific Item document. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - Only after that does it load the document with `frappe.get_doc("Item", item_code)` and check `is_stock_item`. - It then goes on to create the Stock Reconciliation through the helper `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`. That helper is not whitelisted and has no check of its own. - From what I read, the guard checks write access on the Item only. It does not check create access on Stock Reconciliation.

All three whitelisted functions are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/item/item.py`. Each one checks permission at the top, before it does any work. - **Attribute autocomplete, `get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`:** - It runs `frappe.has_permission("Item")`, which checks the default `read` permission on the Item doctype. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("No Permission"))`. - Only after that does it query `Item Attribute Value` with `frappe.get_all`, filtering on `parent` and a `like` match on `attribute_value`. - `get_all` skips per-record permission checks, so the doctype-level check at the top is the only guard. - **Item price preview, `get_item_prices(item_code)`:** - It runs `frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read")`. The check is on the Item Price doctype, not Item. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - It then reads prices with a `frappe.qb` query. That query also skips permission checks, so the guard above is the only one. - The query excludes cancelled and expired prices and returns at most 10 rows, plus a `has_more` flag. - **Opening stock creation, `make_opening_stock_entry(item_code, company, qty, valuation_rate, warehouse=None)`:** - It runs `frappe.has_permission("Item", "write", item_code)`, so it needs write permission on that specific Item. - If the check fails, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - After the check, it validates the request. The item must be a stock item and must not use serial or batch numbers. No stock ledger entries can exist yet, `qty` must be positive, `valuation_rate` cannot be negative, and the warehouse must belong to the company. It also needs a Temporary account for the company. - It then calls the helper `create_opening_stock_reconciliation` to create the Stock Reconciliation. - The permission check covers only Item write access. I did not see a separate check for permission to create a Stock Reconciliation. I did not read `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`'s body, so I can't say whether it enforces one.

The Item form (`item.js`) calls them by their dotted path. **1. Attribute autocomplete: `get_item_attribute(parent, attribute_value="")`** (line ~1526) - It runs `if not frappe.has_permission("Item"): frappe.throw(_("No Permission"))`. - This call passes no `ptype`, so it checks the default `read` permission on the Item doctype. - It doesn't check any particular Item document, only the doctype. - It then uses `frappe.get_all` on "Item Attribute Value", filtered by `parent` and a `LIKE` on `attribute_value`. - `get_all` skips permission checks, so the guard above is the only protection. - The error is a plain `frappe.throw`, not a `PermissionError`. - `item.js` calls it from around line 1483. **2. Item price preview: `get_item_prices(item_code)`** (line ~1589) - It runs `if not frappe.has_permission("Item Price", "read"): frappe.throw(_("Not permitted"), frappe.PermissionError)`. - It checks read permission on the Item Price doctype, not on Item. - The query is built with `frappe.qb`, which bypasses permission filtering, so this check is the only gate. - It returns at most 10 non-cancelled, unexpired prices plus a `has_more` flag. - `item.js` calls it from `render_item_prices` (line ~861). **3. It checks write permission on the specific Item document, so row-level rules such as user permissions also apply. - The check runs before `frappe.get_doc("Item", item_code)`. - After that come business-rule checks, not permission checks. The item must be a stock item and must not have serial or batch numbers. - It then calls the helper `create_opening_stock_reconciliation`, which is not whitelisted. That helper builds and inserts a "Stock Reconciliation" with purpose "Opening Stock" using `stock_reco.insert()`. I didn't read the rest of `make_opening_stock_entry`, so I haven't confirmed whether the insert is wrapped in any permission bypass. - `item.js` calls it from around line 1001. None of the three relies on the form's UI to restrict access. Each re-checks permission on the server and raises an error if the user lacks it. Two raise `frappe.PermissionError`. `get_item_attribute` raises a plain validation error with the message "No Permission".

final answer
Reserved stock is recorded as submitted Stock Reservation Entry (SRE) documents. A running total of them is cached on the Bin, and the stock ledger checks that total on every stock movement. **1. Where the promise is recorded** - The record is the **Stock Reservation Entry** doctype, in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each entry has an item, a warehouse and a `reserved_qty`. It also has `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty`. - It carries a voucher link. Today `validate_stock_reservation_settings` allows only a **Sales Order** as the voucher type. - It has `reservation_based_on`, which is either Qty or Serial and Batch. For Serial and Batch, the serial and batch numbers are stored in child Serial and Batch Entry rows. - Entries are created by `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items` and by `make_stock_reservation_entries` / `make_stock_reservation_entry` (in the `StockReservation` helper class in the same file). The Sales Order side lives in `selling/doctype/sales_order/services/reservation.py`, where `validate_reserved_stock` is also defined. - `validate_amended_doc` and `validate_mandatory` run when an entry is saved. - `on_submit` runs `update_reserved_qty_in_voucher`, `update_status` and `update_reserved_stock_in_bin`. - `on_cancel` runs `update_unreserved_qty_in_sre` and the same updates. - `cancel_stock_reservation_entries` handles bulk cancellation. **2. How "still available to promise" is calculated** - `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no, ignore_sre)` in `stock_reservation_entry.py` returns `get_stock_balance` minus the sum of `reserved_qty - delivered_qty - transferred_qty - consumed_qty`. That sum covers only submitted entries (`docstatus=1`) that are not yet fully delivered. - It locks the rows it reads (`for_update`), so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. - For batches it uses `get_batch_qty` instead. - `get_available_serial_nos_to_reserve` takes the available serial numbers and removes the ones already reserved in submitted entries with `reservation_based_on == "Serial and Batch"`. - `validate_with_allowed_qty` (a method on the SRE class) checks a new reservation against these figures. - `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse` and `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_items_and_warehouses` compute the total reserved per item and warehouse. - `Bin.update_reserved_stock` in `stock/doctype/bin/bin.py` writes that total to the Bin's `reserved_stock` field. The SRE's `update_reserved_stock_in_bin` calls it on submit and cancel. - The reports (`stock/report/reserved_stock/reserved_stock.py`, `stock_balance.py`) read the same totals. **3. How an unrelated shipment is stopped** - `update_stock_ledger` in `stock/stock_ledger.py` reads the Bin's `reserved_stock` into `args.reserved_stock` (line 161). It passes that value to `update_entries_after` in `repost_current_voucher`. - In the `update_entries_after` class, `validate_negative_stock` uses `diff = qty_after_transaction + actual_qty - reserved_stock`. If `diff` is below zero, the transaction fails as insufficient stock. So a shipment can only take stock that is not reserved. - `validate_future_sle_not_negative` (near line 2399) calls the module-level `validate_reserved_stock(kwargs)` when `reserved_stock` is set. It compares the warehouse balance with `reserved_stock` and throws a "Reserved Stock" error if the balance falls short. - For serial and batch items, `validate_reserved_serial_nos` throws "Serial Nos are reserved in Stock Reservation Entries, you need to unreserve them". `validate_reserved_batch_nos` compares each batch's available quantity with its reserved quantity. - Stock Reconciliation has its own `validate_reserved_stock` in `stock_reconciliation.py`. - The customer's own delivery is not blocked. When a Delivery Note delivers against the Sales Order, `delivered_qty` on the entry goes up. That lowers the reserved quantity as the stock leaves, so the stock isn't counted as reserved twice. The Delivery Note code that does this is in `stock/doctype/delivery_note/delivery_note.py`. I read the code for the reservation record, the availability functions and the ledger checks. I did not read the Delivery Note code in detail or run any of this.

final answer
**Short version:** the promise is a submitted Stock Reservation Entry (SRE). Available-to-promise is the stock balance minus the SRE's unfulfilled quantity. The stock ledger refuses any movement that would dip into that reserved quantity. ## 1. Where the promise is recorded - The record is the **Stock Reservation Entry** doctype, in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each submitted SRE (`docstatus == 1`) holds the `item_code`, `warehouse`, `voucher_type`, `voucher_no` and `voucher_detail_no`. For a customer promise, the voucher is the Sales Order and its item row. - It also holds `reserved_qty`, and the counters `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty` that record how much of the promise has been fulfilled. For serial or batch items it records the specific serial and batch numbers. - Sales Order items get their SREs from `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items` in the same file. Work Orders, Subcontracting Orders and Purchase Receipts do it through `StockReservation.make_stock_reservation_entries` and `transfer_reservation_entries_to`. - The `StockReservationEntry` class validates a reservation before submit. `before_submit` and `on_update_after_submit` call `validate_reservation_based_on_serial_and_batch`. That in turn calls `validate_with_allowed_qty`, which checks the amount against what is available to reserve. ## 2. How "available to promise" is calculated - The function is `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no=None, ignore_sre=None)` in `stock_reservation_entry.py`. The docstring gives the formula: Actual Qty − Reserved Qty. - Actual qty comes from `get_stock_balance` in `erpnext/stock/utils.py`, which reads the last Stock Ledger Entry's `qty_after_transaction`. - Reserved qty is `Sum(reserved_qty − delivered_qty − transferred_qty − consumed_qty)` over submitted SREs for that item and warehouse where `delivered_qty < reserved_qty`. - `ignore_sre` excludes the entry being edited, so it doesn't count against itself. - The query takes a `FOR UPDATE` lock, so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. On Postgres it locks the Bin row and the matching SREs instead. - With a `batch_no`, it uses `get_batch_qty` from `batch.py` instead of the plain stock balance. - There is a near-duplicate method, `StockReservation.get_available_qty_to_reserve`, which does the same sum without the locking. It is used by `make_stock_reservation_entries`, `transfer_reservation_entries_to` and `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items`. - For serial numbers, `get_available_serial_nos_to_reserve` does the equivalent job. ## 3. How an unrelated shipment is stopped All of this is in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`, in the Stock Ledger Entry posting path: - `update_entries_after.get_reserved_stock()` computes the outstanding reserved quantity for the item and warehouse. It uses the same sum as above, limited to SREs created at or before the posting datetime. The result is stored as `self.reserved_stock`. - The `Bin` doctype also carries a `reserved_stock` field (read around line 161 of the same file), and it is passed into the validation arguments as `args.reserved_stock`. - `validate_negative_qty_in_future_sle` ends with `if args.reserved_stock: validate_reserved_stock(args)`. - `validate_reserved_stock(kwargs)` takes `get_stock_balance(...) − reserved_stock`. If the difference is negative beyond a 0.0001 tolerance, it throws "{n} units of {item} needed in {warehouse}… " under the title "Reserved Stock". An outgoing Delivery Note, Stock Entry or similar therefore fails if it would eat into reserved stock. - The negative-stock error path (around line 1860) appends "As X units are reserved for other sales orders, you are allowed to consume only Y units". If everything is reserved, it says you can't consume the stock at all. - Serial and batch items have their own checks: - `validate_reserved_serial_nos` blocks serial numbers that are in an SRE (it uses `get_sre_reserved_serial_nos_details`). - `validate_reserved_batch_nos` blocks the reserved part of a batch (it uses `get_sre_reserved_batch_nos_details`). - `StockReconciliation.validate_reserved_stock` (`stock_reconciliation.py`) also refuses to change quantities for items and warehouses that still have reservations. It uses `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_items_and_warehouses`. ## 4. How the owner's own shipment gets through The customer's own delivery is checked against its own reservation, not blocked by it. As the delivery posts, the SRE's `delivered_qty` goes up (`StockReservation.update_delivered_qty`). That shrinks the "reserved − delivered" figure, so the reservation is consumed rather than blocking its own shipment. I did not read `update_delivered_qty` itself, so this part rests on the method name and its call from `transfer_reservation_entries_to`. ## Related, but separate `get_pos_reserved_qty` in `pos_invoice.py` handles the different case of stock sold on unconsolidated POS invoices. It is not an SRE.

final answer
ERPNext records the promise as a **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE). It then blocks other shipments by having the stock-ledger posting check that the item's balance still covers everything that is reserved. I read the code paths below but did not run anything. **Where the promise is recorded** - The SRE doctype is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each submitted SRE holds the item, warehouse, voucher (for example a Sales Order and its line) and the batch or serial numbers if any. It also holds `reserved_qty` and the running counters `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty`. - `create_stock_reservation_entries_for_so_items()` creates SREs for Sales Order items, and the `StockReservation` helper class (`make_stock_reservation_entries`, `cancel_stock_reservation_entries`) does the same for other vouchers. Manufacturing has its own reservation code in `production_plan/services/reservation.py`. - `SRE.update_status()` moves an entry between Reserved, Partially Reserved, Partially Delivered, Delivered, Closed and Cancelled. - `SRE.update_reserved_stock_in_bin()` calls `Bin.update_reserved_stock()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/bin/bin.py`. That keeps a cached `reserved_stock` figure on the Bin for each item and warehouse. **How "available to promise" is calculated** - `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no, ignore_sre)` in the SRE file returns the actual balance minus the open reservations. - The balance comes from `get_stock_balance()`, or from `get_batch_qty()` when a batch is given. - The open reservations are the sum of `reserved_qty − delivered_qty − transferred_qty − consumed_qty` over submitted SREs for that item and warehouse that are not yet fully delivered. - The aggregate query locks the matching rows (`for_update`), so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. Postgres has no gap locks, so it locks the Bin row first. - `SRE.validate_with_allowed_qty()` uses this to reject over-reserving. - `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse()` and its multi-item variant return the reserved totals. `get_sre_reserved_serial_nos_details()` and `get_sre_reserved_batch_nos_details()` do the same for serial numbers and batches. **How it stops an unrelated shipment from taking the stock** - In `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`, the stock-ledger processing class sets `self.reserved_stock = self.get_reserved_stock()` (line 622). That method sums the open SRE quantity for the item and warehouse, counting only SREs created at or before the posting time. - The negative-stock validation, which ends at line 2399, then runs `if args.reserved_stock: validate_reserved_stock(args)`. - `validate_reserved_stock()` compares `get_stock_balance()` with the reserved quantity. If the balance would fall below it, it throws "X units of Item needed in Warehouse … to complete this transaction" (title "Reserved Stock"). So a Delivery Note or other issue for a different customer can't push the balance under what is reserved. The delivery that the reservation belongs to still goes through. As it posts, it increases `delivered_qty`, which lowers the open reservation. - `Stock Reconciliation` has its own `validate_reserved_stock()` (line 829 of its file), called from its validation paths, so a reconciliation can't cut stock below the reserved amount. - `validate_reserved_serial_nos()` and `validate_reserved_batch_nos()` in `stock_ledger.py` do the same for serial-numbered and batched stock. I found no calls to them in the files I searched, so I can't say where they are invoked.

final answer
The promise is recorded as a **Stock Reservation Entry** (SRE). The "available to promise" figure is the stock balance minus the open reservations. The stock ledger refuses any outgoing movement that would eat into that reserved quantity. ## Where the promise is recorded - **Doctype:** `Stock Reservation Entry`, in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_reservation_entry/stock_reservation_entry.py`. - Each submitted SRE (`docstatus == 1`) ties an item and warehouse to a source voucher, such as a Sales Order, Work Order or Production Plan. - Its quantity fields are `reserved_qty`, `delivered_qty`, `transferred_qty` and `consumed_qty`. - A reservation can be based on plain Qty or on Serial and Batch. `before_submit` calls `set_reservation_based_on` to set this. - **Creating reservations:** - For sales orders, `SalesOrderStockReservation` in `erpnext/selling/doctype/sales_order/services/reservation.py` does the work, and `validate_reserved_stock` is called from `sales_order.py:229`. - The `StockReservation` helper class in `stock_reservation_entry.py` does the same for Work Orders and Production Plans. Its `make_stock_reservation_entries` method clamps the reserved quantity to what is still available. It calls `throw_stock_not_exists_error` if nothing is available. - **Bin cache:** `Bin.update_reserved_stock` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/bin/bin.py:217` stores the total on `Bin.reserved_stock`. It gets the total from `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse`. ## How "still available to promise" is calculated - **Function:** `get_available_qty_to_reserve(item_code, warehouse, batch_no=None, ignore_sre=None)` at `stock_reservation_entry.py:693`. - Without a batch, the result is `get_stock_balance(item, warehouse)` minus the sum of `reserved_qty - delivered_qty - transferred_qty - consumed_qty`. - That sum covers submitted SREs where `delivered_qty < reserved_qty`. - With a batch, it returns `get_batch_qty(...)` instead. - It locks the rows with `FOR UPDATE`, so two concurrent reservations can't both claim the same stock. - `ignore_sre` lets a reservation be recalculated without counting itself. - **Class method:** `StockReservation.get_available_qty_to_reserve` (`stock_reservation_entry.py:1270`) does the same calculation, without the locking or batch handling. - **Serial numbers:** `get_available_serial_nos_to_reserve` handles serial-number items. - **Reserved totals:** `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_item_and_warehouse`, `get_sre_reserved_qty_for_items_and_warehouses` and `get_sre_reserved_qty_details_for_voucher` give reserved totals. The item dashboard and stock balance report use them. ## How it blocks an unrelated shipment The block is in the stock ledger, in `erpnext/stock/stock_ledger.py`. - **Reserved quantity per ledger entry:** `update_entries_after.__init__` sets `self.reserved_stock = self.get_reserved_stock()` (line 622). `get_reserved_stock` (line 631) sums `reserved_qty - (delivered + transferred + consumed)` for submitted SREs on that item and warehouse. - **Negative-stock check:** `update_entries_after.validate_negative_stock` (line 1357) computes `qty_after_transaction + sle.actual_qty - reserved_stock`. If the result is below 0, it records an exception and the posting fails. In effect, a delivery or issue can only use stock above the reserved amount. - **Error message:** The message builder at around line 1860 appends text such as "As N units are reserved for other sales orders, you are allowed to consume only X units". If the whole balance is reserved, it says "the full stock is reserved for other transactions". - **Check before posting:** `validate_reserved_stock(kwargs)` at `stock_ledger.py:2470` runs when `args.reserved_stock` is set (line 2399). It throws "Reserved Stock" if `get_stock_balance - reserved_stock` is negative. - **Serial and batch items:** - `validate_reserved_serial_nos` blocks any serial number that is reserved in an SRE. - `validate_reserved_batch_nos` compares available batch quantity against reserved batch quantity. - `serial_and_batch_bundle.py:~2995` skips this check only when `ignore_reserved_stock` is set. - **Stock Reconciliation:** it has its own `validate_reserved_stock` (`stock_reconciliation.py:829`). This stops a reconciliation from lowering stock below what is reserved. - **The reserving voucher itself:** When the promised customer's own delivery goes out, it increments `delivered_qty` on the SRE. The reservation then shrinks by the same amount as the stock leaves, so that delivery isn't blocked by its own reservation.
Q14Group C — Semantic gap (no keyword overlap with the code)When item prices in ERPNext already include tax, how does the system work backwards to determine the pre-tax amount of each line? …

final answer
The back-calculation happens in `determine_exclusive_rate()`, a method of the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py` (line 303). `calculate()` calls it at line 84, after the item amounts are set and before `calculate_net_total` and `calculate_taxes`. I read this method and its helpers. I did not read `calculate_taxes`, so I haven't checked how the later tax calculation uses these net amounts. **Overall flow** - The method returns immediately unless at least one tax row has `included_in_print_rate` set. - For each item, it loads the item-specific tax overrides with `_load_item_tax_rate(item.item_tax_rate)`. This parses the item's JSON map of `account_head` to rate. - It then walks every tax row and calls `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)`. That call returns two things: - `tax_fraction_for_current_item`: the tax as a fraction of the net amount. - `inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`: a fixed amount per unit. - `_get_tax_rate` uses the item's override rate if the row's account head is in the item's map. Otherwise it uses `tax.rate`. If the override is `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`, the row contributes nothing for that item. **Multiple inclusive taxes** - The fractions of all inclusive rows are summed into `cumulated_tax_fraction`. - The fixed per-quantity amounts are summed, each multiplied by `item.qty`, into `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`. - Only rows with `included_in_print_rate` contribute. Other rows return a fraction of 0. - The net amount is computed as `(item.amount − total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty) / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)`. **Compounding rows** - `On Net Total`: the fraction is `rate/100`. - `On Previous Row Amount`: the fraction is `rate/100` times the `tax_fraction_for_current_item` of the row referenced by `row_id`. - `On Previous Row Total`: the fraction is `rate/100` times the referenced row's `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item`. - `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` is a running cumulative value: - For row 0 it is `1 + fraction`. - For later rows it is the previous row's value plus the current row's fraction. - Compounding therefore works because the previous rows' fractions are already computed when a later row refers to them. **Per-quantity taxes** - `On Item Quantity` rows have no percentage fraction. - The rate is treated as a fixed amount per unit (`inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty = rate`). - It is multiplied by the quantity and subtracted from the gross amount before the division by `1 + cumulated_tax_fraction`. **Deduction-type taxes** - If `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by −1. - A deduction therefore reduces `cumulated_tax_fraction`, or adds to the amount that is subtracted, in the opposite direction to an added tax. **Result stored on the item** - `_unrounded_net_amount` keeps the unrounded value. - `net_amount` is rounded to the field's precision. - `net_rate` is `net_amount / qty`, rounded to precision. - The values are also converted to company currency with `_set_in_company_currency`. - This is skipped if `discount_amount_applied` is true or the item's quantity is 0. In the discount case, the net amounts are handled elsewhere, in the discount-distribution code, and I did not read it.

final answer
The back-calculation is done by `determine_exclusive_rate()` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, in the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class. It calls `get_current_tax_fraction()` for each tax row. I read the Python in full. The JS version I only know from the search excerpts, where it looks like a line-for-line mirror. **Where it runs** - The Python code is `determine_exclusive_rate()` (lines ~303–343), called from `_calculate()` before the normal tax pass. - The client-side mirror is `determine_exclusive_rate()` in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js` (lines ~254–298). It does the same maths. - `validate_inclusive_tax()` in `erpnext/accounts/services/taxes.py` enforces which rows may be marked "Included in Print Rate". "Actual" and "Valuation" rows can't be inclusive. A row charged on a previous row can only be inclusive if the rows it depends on are also inclusive. **The algorithm, per item** 1. It returns immediately if no tax row has `included_in_print_rate`. 2. For each item, it loads that item's `item_tax_rate` override (`_load_item_tax_rate`). 3. For each tax row, `get_current_tax_fraction()` returns two values: - a fraction of the net amount, stored as `tax.tax_fraction_for_current_item`; - a fixed per-quantity amount. 4. It keeps a running `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` (the row's cumulative total as a multiple of net). Row 0 gets `1 + fraction`. Each later row gets the previous row's value plus its own fraction. 5. It sums the fractions into `cumulated_tax_fraction`. It sums the per-quantity amounts, multiplied by `item.qty`, into `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`. 6. Unless a discount amount has already been applied, and only if the item has a quantity and some inclusive tax applies, it computes: - `amount = item.amount - total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty` - `_unrounded_net_amount = amount / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` - `net_amount` is that value rounded to the field precision. - `net_rate = net_amount / qty`. - Both values are then converted into company currency. **How each tax type is handled** (`get_current_tax_fraction`, lines 348–382) - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** the fractions are added together. For example, 10% and 5% both "On Net Total" give net = amount / 1.15. Only rows with `included_in_print_rate` contribute. If the item's tax map marks a tax as not applicable (`NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`), that row contributes nothing. - **On Net Total:** the fraction is `rate / 100`. - **Compounding rows:** - "On Previous Row Amount" uses `rate/100 × the previous row's tax_fraction_for_current_item`. - "On Previous Row Total" uses `rate/100 × the previous row's grand_total_fraction_for_current_item`. - This compounds correctly. For example, 10% followed by 5% on the previous row's total gives a combined fraction of 0.10 + 0.05 × 1.10. - **Per-quantity taxes ("On Item Quantity"):** the fraction stays 0 and the second return value is the flat rate. The code subtracts rate × qty from the gross amount before dividing by `1 + fractions`. So a fixed amount per unit is removed first, and the percentage taxes are backed out of the remainder. - **Deduction taxes (`add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`):** both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by -1. A deducted inclusive tax therefore lowers the cumulative fraction, which makes the net amount larger, and it lowers the grand-total fraction for later rows. **Afterwards** - `get_current_tax_and_net_amount()` computes the tax amounts from the net amounts. For inclusive "On Net Total" rows it uses the unrounded net amount (`_unrounded_net_amount`) to avoid double rounding. - `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax()` absorbs any leftover rounding difference into `grand_total_diff`, but only if the difference is within ±5/10^precision. Non-inclusive deduct or valuation rows are signed correctly through `get_tax_amount_if_for_valuation_or_deduction`.

final answer
The back-calculation happens in `determine_exclusive_rate()`, in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`. It belongs to the `calculate_taxes_and_totals` class. The JS counterpart is `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`. I read `determine_exclusive_rate()` and its helpers in full. I only skimmed the code that consumes the result (the lines around 611 and 702). **Where it sits in the flow** `calculate()` calls `_calculate()`. That runs these steps in order: 1. `calculate_item_values` 2. `validate_item_tax_template` 3. `update_item_tax_map` 4. `initialize_taxes` 5. `determine_exclusive_rate` 6. `calculate_net_total` 7. `calculate_taxes` 8. `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` 9. `calculate_totals` Because `determine_exclusive_rate` runs before `calculate_net_total`, `net_total` is built from the already tax-stripped `net_amount` values. **What `determine_exclusive_rate()` does** - It returns immediately unless at least one tax row has `included_in_print_rate` set. - It then goes through each item and loads that item's per-item tax overrides with `_load_item_tax_rate(item.item_tax_rate)`. This parses the JSON map of account head to rate. - For every tax row, `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)` returns two values: - a fractional rate for the row (`tax_fraction_for_current_item`) - a fixed amount per unit of quantity. - The function adds up two running totals across all rows: - `cumulated_tax_fraction`: the sum of the fractions. - `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`: the sum of each row's per-quantity amount times `item.qty`. - If no discount has been applied yet (`discount_amount_applied` is false), the item has a quantity, and either total is non-zero, it computes the pre-tax amount: - `net = (item.amount − total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty) / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` - The unrounded value is kept in `item._unrounded_net_amount`. - `item.net_amount` is that value rounded to the field's precision. - `item.net_rate` is `net_amount / qty`. - `_set_in_company_currency` then fills in the base-currency versions. **How each case is handled** - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** the fractions of all inclusive rows are summed. Dividing by `1 + Σfractions` strips all of them at once. Rows that aren't inclusive contribute 0. - **Per-item rates and exemptions:** `_get_tax_rate` uses the rate from the item's tax map if the account head is in it, and otherwise uses `tax.rate`. If the item's rate is `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX`, the row contributes nothing. - **Compounding rows:** the fraction depends on the charge type. - On Net Total: `rate/100`. - On Previous Row Amount: `rate/100 × (previous row's tax_fraction_for_current_item)`. - On Previous Row Total: `rate/100 × (previous row's grand_total_fraction_for_current_item)`. - Each row's running total fraction is stored as `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item`. For row 0 it is `1 + fraction`. For later rows it is the previous row's total fraction plus this row's fraction. This is how a compounding row ends up taxing "price + earlier taxes". - **Per-quantity taxes:** for On Item Quantity, the fraction is 0 and the per-quantity amount is the rate itself (`flt(tax_rate)`). It is multiplied by `item.qty` and subtracted from the gross amount before the division. A fixed amount has no percentage, so it is removed first. - **Deduction taxes:** if `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by −1. A deduction therefore raises the net amount instead of lowering it. **What happens afterwards** - In `calculate_taxes`, the code around line 611 uses `_unrounded_net_amount` to compute each inclusive tax amount. For example, it uses `rate/100 × _unrounded_net_amount` for the On Net Total row. This keeps the tax amounts consistent with the net amount and avoids double-counting. - `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` (around line 702) fixes small rounding differences so the grand total matches the original inclusive amounts. - The back-calculation is skipped once a document-level discount has been applied (`discount_amount_applied`).

final answer
**Where it happens** The work is done in `calculate_taxes_and_totals.determine_exclusive_rate()` in `erpnext/controllers/taxes_and_totals.py`, at about line 303. The order in `_calculate` is: `calculate_item_values`, `initialize_taxes`, `determine_exclusive_rate`, `calculate_net_total`, `calculate_taxes`, `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax`, `calculate_totals`. The client-side twin is `determine_exclusive_rate` in `erpnext/public/js/controllers/taxes_and_totals.js`, at about line 254. It has the same logic. `PaymentEntry.determine_exclusive_rate` in `payment_entry.py` is a simplified version. It uses `included_in_paid_amount` and applies one fraction to the paid amount. It has no per-quantity handling. **Algorithm per item** 1. The method returns early unless at least one tax row has `included_in_print_rate`. 2. For each item, it loads `item_tax_rate` through `_load_item_tax_rate`. This is a JSON map of account head to rate. 3. For each tax row, `get_current_tax_fraction(tax, item_tax_map)` returns two values: a fraction of the net amount and a flat amount per quantity. 4. It sums the fractions into `cumulated_tax_fraction`. It sums the per-quantity amounts, multiplied by `item.qty`, into `total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`. 5. Unless a document-level discount amount has already been applied (`discount_amount_applied`), it works back to the net amount: - `net_amount = (item.amount − total_inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty) / (1 + cumulated_tax_fraction)` - It stores the unrounded value in `item._unrounded_net_amount`. It rounds `net_amount` and sets `net_rate = net_amount / qty`. - It also sets the company-currency values. Dividing by `1 + Σ fractions` is the same as solving `gross = net × (1 + f₁ + f₂ + …)`. **Handling each case** - **Multiple inclusive taxes:** The fractions of all inclusive rows are added together. Only rows with `included_in_print_rate` contribute. Rows that are not inclusive give a fraction of 0 and are added on top later by `calculate_taxes`. - **Item-specific rates:** `_get_tax_rate` uses the rate from `item_tax_rate` if the row's `account_head` is in the map. Otherwise it uses `tax.rate`. If the item's rate is the `NOT_APPLICABLE_TAX` marker, the row contributes (0, 0). - **Compounding rows:** `get_current_tax_fraction` handles the charge types as follows: - `On Net Total`: the fraction is `rate/100`. - `On Previous Row Amount`: the fraction is `rate/100 × tax_fraction_for_current_item` of the row at `row_id − 1`. That is a percentage of the earlier tax. - `On Previous Row Total`: the fraction is `rate/100 × grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` of the referenced row. That is a percentage of the earlier running total. - `grand_total_fraction_for_current_item` is a running total. Row 0 is `1 + fraction`. Each later row is the previous row's value plus its own fraction. - **Per-quantity taxes:** For `On Item Quantity`, the fraction stays 0. The tax rate is instead returned as a fixed amount per unit (`inclusive_tax_amount_per_qty`). It is multiplied by `qty` and subtracted from the gross amount before dividing by `1 + fractions`. A flat amount is not scaled by the percentage taxes. - **Deduction taxes:** If `add_deduct_tax == "Deduct"`, both the fraction and the per-quantity amount are multiplied by −1. Deductions reduce the divisor, and a per-quantity deduction increases the amount before dividing. **Afterwards** `calculate_taxes` then computes the actual tax amounts from the net amounts. Rounding can leave the last row's total slightly different from the gross total. `adjust_grand_total_for_inclusive_tax` handles that. It computes `diff = doc.total + non_inclusive_taxes − last_tax.total`. It subtracts any discount amount that was applied. If `diff` is non-zero and no larger than half the smallest unit of the tax amount's precision (`5 / 10**precision`), it stores it in `grand_total_diff`. Otherwise `grand_total_diff` is 0. `calculate_totals` adds `grand_total_diff` to the grand total.

Between receiving goods and receiving the supplier's bill, where does ERPNext "park" the value of the received goods in the books, and how does entering the bill later move that value out of the holding spot? Trace both the receipt side and the billing side, naming the relevant functions and files.

final answer
I'm wrapping up. I've traced both sides and have enough to answer. **Where the value is parked** ERPNext parks it in the **Stock Received But Not Billed (SRBNB)** account. This is a liability-type account set per company in `Company.stock_received_but_not_billed`. Fixed-asset items use `asset_received_but_not_billed` instead. It applies when perpetual inventory (`auto_accounting_for_stock`) is on. **Receipt side (Purchase Receipt)** - `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` has `get_gl_entries()`. It delegates to the GL composer class in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. - In that file, `compose()` calls `_make_item_gl_entries()`. - For each stock item, `_make_item_gl_entries()` debits the warehouse's stock-in-hand account (or the asset account) with the stock value difference. - The inner helper `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry(item)` posts the opposing leg to the SRBNB account (`stock_asset_rbnb`). The code passes it as a negative `debit`, which makes it a credit. - The net effect is Dr Stock In Hand / Cr SRBNB. The goods are on the balance sheet, and the offsetting credit sits in SRBNB as an unbilled liability. No supplier payable exists yet. - Valuation-type taxes, landed cost and subcontracting supplied-item costs are also posted here. `set_gl_entry_for_purchase_expense` handles the expense-related entries. **Billing side (Purchase Invoice)** - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py`, in `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account()`: - For a stock item on an invoice that doesn't update stock, it sets `item.expense_account` to the company's SRBNB account. - This applies whether the invoice is linked to a Purchase Receipt or not. When SRBNB was already credited in the receipt, it also warns that the Expense Head was changed. - If `update_stock` is on, the expense account is the warehouse's inventory account instead. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`: - `make_supplier_gl_entry()` credits the Payable account for the supplier. - `make_item_gl_entries()` debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB, for the billed amount. - The code comment says that with Update Stock off, the invoice "only clears Stock Received But Not Billed at the full billed amount against the supplier". - The net effect is Dr SRBNB / Cr Creditors. This reverses the receipt's SRBNB credit and replaces it with a real payable. - Related adjustments in `make_item_gl_entries()`: - If the billing exchange rate differs from the receipt's, it posts the difference to the exchange gain/loss account. - Item tax on valuation taxes is debited to SRBNB when it wasn't already booked in the receipt (this is the `negative_expense_booked_in_pr` check). - Provisional accounting for non-stock items is a separate mechanism. `get_provisional_accounts()` in the same file reverses the `provisional_expense_account` that the receipt booked. **Linking and billing status** - Purchase Receipt Items link to the invoice through `pr_detail` and `purchase_receipt`. - `update_billing_status` and `update_billing_percentage` (in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py`) update `billed_amt` and `per_billed` on the receipt. `update_billed_amount_based_on_po` is in `purchase_receipt/services/billing_status.py`. I didn't read the Purchase Receipt composer past line 140 or the Purchase Invoice composer between lines 298 and 330. The rest of the asset and subcontracting handling, and the exact Dr/Cr legs I describe for those paths, come from the grep hits and comments rather than a full read.

final answer
ERPNext parks the value in the **Stock Received But Not Billed** (SRBNB) account. This is the company default `stock_received_but_not_billed`, an account of type "Stock Received But Not Billed". Fixed-asset items use "Asset Received But Not Billed" instead. I read the code below but did not run it. ## Receipt side: Purchase Receipt - **Entry point:** `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (line 478). It hands off to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. The caller in `_make_item_gl_entries` (around lines 297–361) picks the SRBNB account with `doc.get_company_default("stock_received_but_not_billed")`. - **Debit side:** The stock-in-hand (inventory) account is debited with the stock value difference taken from the Stock Ledger Entry. This is done by `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry`, using `get_stock_value_difference` in `purchase_receipt.py`. - **Credit side (the holding spot):** `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry(item)` books the other half against SRBNB, using the item's `base_net_amount`. The code passes a negative debit (`debit = -1 * outgoing_amount`) to get a credit, and uses `add_gl_entry`. So the receipt posts Dr Stock In Hand / Cr SRBNB, and SRBNB carries a credit balance (a liability-like "received, not yet billed") until the bill arrives. - **Related receipt entries:** - `make_landed_cost_gl_entries` and `make_expenses_added_to_stock_entries` handle landed costs. - `make_divisional_loss_gl_entry` handles differences between the stock value and the billed amount. - Reports that read this state: `received_items_to_be_billed` (receipt amount minus `billed_amt`) and `billed_items_to_be_received`. ## Billing side: Purchase Invoice (update_stock off, linked to a receipt) - **Account selection:** `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py` (lines 20–158). With perpetual inventory on, it forces `item.expense_account` to SRBNB for stock items that are not opening entries and not fixed assets. This applies when the invoice doesn't update stock, whether or not it is linked to a receipt. - If it is linked to a receipt, it first checks that a GL Entry for that receipt exists on the SRBNB account, and only then sets SRBNB. - If there is no receipt, it also sets SRBNB, so an invoice entered before the receipt works too. - It does not set SRBNB when `update_stock` is on and the item has a warehouse. In that case the expense account is the warehouse's inventory account. - Fixed-asset items follow a separate branch and are not covered here. - **GL posting:** `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` / `get_gl_entries` in `purchase_invoice.py` (lines 671 and 703) delegate to `PurchaseInvoiceGLComposer.make_item_gl_entries` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py` (line 129). - For stock items on an invoice that doesn't update stock, the code takes the `else` branch (around lines 319–350). It debits `item.expense_account` (SRBNB) with `base_amount` against the supplier. The payable (credit to the supplier) comes from the invoice's payable entry elsewhere in the composer. This offsets the receipt's SRBNB credit and leaves SRBNB at zero, and the stock account is not touched again. - An in-code comment says that when Update Stock is off, the invoice only clears SRBNB at the billed amount. - Exchange-rate differences between receipt and invoice are posted to the exchange gain/loss account (lines 352–393). - For valuation taxes, if the receipt didn't book them (`negative_expense_booked_in_pr`), the invoice debits SRBNB directly (lines 395–439). - **If the billed rate differs from the receipt:** the receipt composer's `make_amount_difference_entry` credits SRBNB with `amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice` ("Adjustment based on Purchase Invoice rate"). This is when the Purchase Receipt is updated from the invoice. - **If the invoice has Update Stock on:** the invoice debits the inventory account directly (`make_stock_adjustment_entry`, then `item.expense_account`). SRBNB isn't involved.

final answer
ERPNext parks the value in the **Stock Received But Not Billed (SRBNB)** account. It is a liability-type clearing account, set per company as `stock_received_but_not_billed`. Fixed-asset items use the parallel **Asset Received But Not Billed** account. I read the code paths below but did not run a posting. This applies only when perpetual inventory (auto accounting for stock) is on. ## Receipt side: Purchase Receipt The GL entries are built in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `purchase_receipt.py` (line 478) delegates to it. For each stock item the composer picks `stock_asset_rbnb` from `doc.get_company_default("stock_received_but_not_billed")` (line ~300). For fixed assets it uses `asset_received_but_not_billed` instead. It then posts these entries: - **`make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry`** debits the warehouse's inventory account (or the asset account) by the stock value difference. The amount comes from `get_stock_value_difference`, and `against_account` is the SRBNB account. - **`make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry`** (line 74) posts the offsetting entry to SRBNB. It does this through `add_gl_entry` with a negative debit, which is effectively a credit, for the item's `base_net_amount`. If the item has a `from_warehouse` (internal transfer), it uses that warehouse's inventory account instead. - **`make_landed_cost_gl_entries`, `make_expenses_added_to_stock_entries`, `make_amount_difference_entry` and `make_sub_contracting_gl_entries`** handle landed cost, expenses included in valuation, amount differences and subcontracting. - **`make_divisional_loss_gl_entry`** books the difference between the stock value and the SRBNB amount. When the receipt is for an item with provisional accounting enabled, `add_provisional_gl_entry` credits the provisional expense account instead of SRBNB (line ~287). The net result is Dr Inventory and Cr SRBNB. The "received but not billed" liability sits in SRBNB until the bill arrives. ## Billing side: Purchase Invoice 1. **Choosing the account.** `ExpenseAccountService.set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py` sets each stock item's `expense_account` to SRBNB. This happens when `update_stock` is off, the item isn't a fixed asset, and the invoice isn't an opening entry. If the linked receipt already booked SRBNB, it checks the GL Entry table for that and forces the account (with a warning if it changed the user's choice). If there is no receipt yet, it also uses SRBNB, so a later receipt can clear it. - When `update_stock` is on and the item has a warehouse, it uses the inventory account directly, so SRBNB isn't involved. - Fixed assets check for Asset Received But Not Billed on the receipt and fall back to the CWIP or fixed asset account. 2. **Posting.** `PurchaseInvoice.make_gl_entries` (`purchase_invoice.py`, line 671) calls the composer in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. In that composer: - `make_supplier_gl_entry` credits Payables (the creditor account). - `make_item_gl_entries` debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB for receipt-linked items. This reverses the credit the receipt left there, so the holding balance clears and the liability moves to Accounts Payable. - Any difference between the receipt rate and the invoice rate goes to a price-difference or stock adjustment entry (`make_stock_adjustment_entry`, `get_stock_variance_account`). Exchange-rate discrepancies go to the exchange gain/loss account. - For item taxes that are part of valuation and weren't booked on the receipt, there is a reverse SRBNB debit (lines ~395–440). It increments `negative_expense_to_be_booked`. - For provisional accounting, `get_provisional_accounts` and `make_provisional_gl_entry` reverse the provisional entry made on the receipt. If the invoice comes before the receipt, the invoice debits SRBNB and the later receipt's SRBNB credit clears it. For monitoring, the reports `received_items_to_be_billed` and `billed_items_to_be_received` under `erpnext/accounts/report/` show items that are still parked in SRBNB.

final answer
ERPNext parks the value in a liability account called **Stock Received But Not Billed (SRBNB)**. The Purchase Receipt credits it, and the Purchase Invoice debits it. Fixed-asset items use **Asset Received But Not Billed (ARBNB)** instead. I read the code but did not run a posting. ## Receipt side (Purchase Receipt) 1. `PurchaseReceipt.get_gl_entries` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` hands off to `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer.compose`. The composer is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/gl_composer.py`. `StockController.make_gl_entries` in `erpnext/controllers/stock_controller.py` calls it on submit. 2. `PurchaseReceiptGLComposer._make_item_gl_entries` loops over the items. For each stock item with perpetual inventory enabled, it picks the holding account: - `stock_asset_rbnb` is the company default `stock_received_but_not_billed`. - For fixed-asset items it is the company default `asset_received_but_not_billed`. 3. For each item it posts the inventory side with `make_item_asset_inward_gl_entry`. This debits the warehouse's inventory account, or the asset account, by the stock value difference. 4. `make_stock_received_but_not_billed_entry` posts the other side. It books the SRBNB account against the inventory account for `base_net_amount`. - The code writes this as `debit = -1 * outgoing_amount`, which is a credit on the account. - For items received from another warehouse, it uses that warehouse's inventory account instead. 5. The same loop adds related entries: landed cost lines, expenses added to stock, and rounding or amount differences. It also adds subcontracting entries and the divisional loss entry. 6. If provisional accounting is on for non-stock items, `add_provisional_gl_entry` in `purchase_receipt.py` parks the value in a provisional expense account instead. So after a receipt, the books show Dr Stock in Hand and Cr SRBNB. ## Billing side (Purchase Invoice) 1. **Choosing the account.** `PurchaseInvoice.set_expense_account` hands off to `set_expense_account` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/expense_account.py`. For stock items, when the invoice does not update stock, it forces `item.expense_account` to the SRBNB account. - If the linked receipt has no SRBNB GL entry, it leaves the item's account alone. - If the invoice has no receipt at all (bill first, receipt later), it also forces SRBNB, so the receipt can later net against it. - With "Update Stock" on, it uses the warehouse inventory account instead, so the invoice books the stock itself. - Fixed assets use the ARBNB check in the same way. 2. **Posting.** `PurchaseInvoice.get_gl_entries` hands off to `PurchaseInvoiceGLComposer.compose` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/gl_composer.py`. The supplier payable is credited (Cr Creditors). For each stock item without update-stock, the item loop debits `item.expense_account`, which is SRBNB, for the billed amount. That reverses the receipt's credit, so SRBNB nets to zero. 3. **Exchange-rate gap.** If the invoice's exchange rate differs from the receipt's, the difference goes to the exchange gain/loss account. 4. **Item taxes.** If the receipt booked valuation taxes and no negative expense was posted there, the invoice adds a debit to SRBNB for `item_tax_amount` and tracks it in `negative_expense_to_be_booked`. ## Link-back to the receipt - `status_updater` in `PurchaseInvoice.__init__` updates billed quantities on the receipt items. - `update_billing_status_in_pr` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/services/billing_status.py` updates the receipt's billing percentage and status. It calls `update_billing_percentage` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/services/billing_status.py`. So SRBNB is credited at receipt and debited at billing, and its balance is the value of goods received but not yet billed.

Yes, a check runs on save. It sits in the Pricing Rule controller, `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/pricing_rule.py`. **Call path** - `PricingRule.validate()` calls `validate_duplicate_apply_on()`. - That method skips the work when `apply_on` is `"Transaction"`. - Otherwise it looks up the child table for the chosen "Apply On" in `apply_on_dict`. For "Item Code" the field is `item_code`. - It collects the non-empty `item_code` values from the rule's item list. - If the list has duplicates, it throws "Duplicate Item Code found in the table". - If `apply_on == "Item Code"`, it then calls `validate_template_with_variant(values)`. **What `validate_template_with_variant(item_codes)` checks** - It runs one `frappe.get_all("Item", ...)` query. The filter is `name in item_codes` AND `variant_of in item_codes`. - That matches any Item that is in the rule's list and whose `variant_of` template is also in the list. It does not look at `has_variants` or at attributes. - If any row comes back, it takes the first one and throws: "Variant {0} and its template {1} cannot both be added to the same Pricing Rule". The message names the variant and its template in bold. - The throw aborts the save. **Scope** - The check only runs when Apply On is "Item Code". It is not applied to Item Group or Brand. - Because it takes `variants[0]`, only the first conflicting variant is reported.
