final answer
When a Stock Entry is made from a Job Card (`make_stock_entry` in `manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py`), each item row passes through `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)`. That function is in `stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`. It does the pre-filling. I read the code but did not run it. **Which rows get pre-filled** - The Stock Entry must have a `work_order`, and the row must have a source warehouse (`s_warehouse`) and a quantity above 0. - The quantity is `qty × conversion_factor`, so it is in stock UOM. - The function does nothing if the row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. - `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch` then checks the item. It must be the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation on that Work Order, and it must have a serial number or batch tracking. Ordinary raw materials get nothing. **How the serials and batches are chosen** The pool is what earlier operations produced and what is still left in that source warehouse: 1. `_get_operation_sn_batch(..., produced=True)` reads the Serial and Batch Entry rows from submitted Stock Entries on the same Work Order. These are "Manufacture" entries where this item is a finished item (`is_finished_item`) going into that warehouse. 2. `produced=False` reads the bundles that later submitted entries on the same Work Order already took out of that warehouse (`s_warehouse`). 3. Serial numbers are kept if they were produced and not consumed. 4. For batches, the available quantity is produced minus consumed, kept only if it is above 0. 5. Entries come back ordered by bundle and row index (`orderby(sbe.parent)`, `orderby(sbe.idx)`). `_cap_pool_to_qty` then takes them in that order, so the earliest-produced are used first. **Capping to the row quantity (`_cap_pool_to_qty`)** - For serial-tracked items it takes the first `cint(qty)` serial numbers. - For batch-tracked items it goes through the batches in order. It takes `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each and stops once `remaining` reaches 0. **If fewer units are available than the row's quantity** - The function fills only what exists. It does not throw an error and does not reduce the row's `qty`. - It builds a draft Outward Serial and Batch Bundle with `SerialBatchCreation(...).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()` and `do_not_submit=True`. The bundle's `qty` is the full row quantity, but it lists only the serials or batches that were available. - It sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` to that bundle and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. - The shortfall is left for the user to fill in. The docstring says "filling what is available and leaving any shortfall blank for the user". - If nothing is available, no bundle is created and the row stays blank.

final answer
When a Job Card is mapped to a Stock Entry, the system pre-fills a semi-finished-good row with the serials or batches that an earlier operation produced and that are still available. If fewer units are available than the row needs, it fills what exists and leaves the rest blank. I traced this by reading the code; I did not run it. **Entry point.** `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` (around line 132) calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for each mapped item row. `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_type/stock_entry_type.py` has a second caller in `add_raw_materials` (line 144). That call is only made when `skip_material_transfer` is set. **How the pool is chosen.** The logic is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`. 1. `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(parent_doc, row)` returns without doing anything in these cases: - the Stock Entry has no `work_order`; - the row has no `s_warehouse`; - the row's quantity (qty × conversion_factor, so it is in stock UOM) is zero or less; - the row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. 2. It calls `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, warehouse)`. That returns an empty result unless all of these hold: - The item is the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation on that Work Order. Ordinary raw materials are therefore skipped. - The item has a serial number or batch number. 3. The function builds two sets with `_get_operation_sn_batch`, which reads the Serial and Batch Entry rows of non-cancelled bundles. Bundles come from `_get_operation_bundles`, which looks at submitted Stock Entries on the same Work Order. - **Produced:** bundles on Manufacture entries with `is_finished_item=1` whose `t_warehouse` is the row's source warehouse. - **Consumed:** bundles on entries whose `s_warehouse` is that same warehouse. 4. Available serials are the produced serials not in the consumed set. Available batches are produced qty minus consumed qty, kept only where the result is above zero. **Shortfall handling.** `_cap_pool_to_qty(pool, qty)` trims the pool to the row quantity. - **Serials:** it takes the first `cint(qty)` serials, in the order they were produced. - **Batches:** it goes through the batches in order and takes `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is met. - **If fewer are available than the row quantity:** it takes whatever exists and does not throw an error. The docstring says the shortfall is left blank for the user to complete. - **If nothing is available:** it returns without setting anything. **Bundle creation.** If any serials or batches were picked, it creates an Outward Serial and Batch Bundle with `SerialBatchCreation(...).make_serial_and_batch_bundle()` and `do_not_submit=True`. The bundle's `qty` is the full row quantity, even if fewer serials or batches were found. It then sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` to the new bundle and `use_serial_batch_fields = 0`.

final answer
When a Stock Entry is made from a Job Card, a semi-finished-good input row is pre-filled with the serial numbers or batches that an earlier operation of the same Work Order produced and that are still unconsumed. If fewer units are available than the row's quantity, it fills what exists and leaves the shortfall blank for the user. I read the code but did not run it. **Where it is called from** - The Job Card path `make_stock_entry` in `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/job_card/mapper.py` loops over the target items and calls `set_previous_operation_serial_batch(target, item)` for each (line 132). - `StockEntryType.add_raw_materials` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_type/stock_entry_type.py` calls the same function (line 144). It does so only in the `elif self.skip_material_transfer:` branch. - The function itself is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/services/manufacturing.py`. **How it picks serials and batches (`set_previous_operation_serial_batch`)** 1. It returns without doing anything if any of these hold: - the Stock Entry has no `work_order`; - the row has no `s_warehouse`; - the quantity is zero or less; - the row already has a `serial_and_batch_bundle`, `serial_no` or `batch_no`. 2. The quantity it fills against is `row.qty × conversion_factor`, so it works in stock UOM. 3. It calls `get_previous_operation_output_sn_batch(work_order, item_code, s_warehouse)` to build the pool of available serials and batches. That function returns an empty result unless all of these hold: - the item is the `finished_good` of some Work Order Operation on that Work Order, so ordinary raw materials are skipped; - the item has serial or batch tracking. 4. The pool is the output of earlier operations minus what has already been used: - **Produced:** `_get_operation_bundles(produced=True)` finds the Serial and Batch Bundles on submitted Manufacture entries for that Work Order. It keeps only finished-item rows whose target warehouse is the row's warehouse. `_get_operation_sn_batch` then reads the entries from those bundles. - **Consumed:** the same lookup with `produced=False` finds bundles on rows whose source warehouse is that warehouse. - **Serials:** a produced serial is available only if it does not appear in the consumed list. - **Batches:** the available quantity is produced minus consumed, kept only when it is above zero. 5. `_cap_pool_to_qty(pool, qty)` trims the pool to the row's quantity: - **Serials:** it takes the first `cint(qty)` serials, in the order they were produced. - **Batches:** it walks the batches in order and takes `min(batch_qty, remaining)` from each until the quantity is covered. 6. If the trimmed result has no serials and no batches, nothing is set. Otherwise it creates an Outward `SerialBatchCreation` bundle with `do_not_submit=True`, so the bundle stays a draft. It then sets `row.serial_and_batch_bundle` to that bundle and `row.use_serial_batch_fields = 0`. **If fewer units are available than the row's quantity** - The row is not rejected and no error is raised. `_cap_pool_to_qty` returns only what the pool holds, so the bundle covers just those serials or batch quantities. - The bundle's `qty` is still set to the full requested quantity. The uncovered part is left for the user to complete by hand, and the bundle is not submitted. - If the pool is empty, the row is left untouched with no bundle at all.
Q24Group E — Concurrency & database semanticsSome ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a link…
Some ERPNext flows perform a best-effort or optional database write inside a larger transaction — for example auto-creating a linked document while submitting another. On PostgreSQL, if such a write fails, every later statement in the transaction fails too. What mechanism does the codebase use to survive these failures, how does it differ from a full rollback, and where is it applied? Name concrete functions and files.

final answer
**Mechanism:** ERPNext uses SQL savepoints through Frappe's `frappe.db.savepoint(name)`. On failure it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. **How it differs from a full rollback:** - A bare `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the whole transaction. That includes the parent document and any earlier work. - On PostgreSQL, a failed statement puts the transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then fails with "current transaction is aborted". - Rolling back to a savepoint (`ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT`) undoes only the work done since that savepoint. This clears the aborted state, and everything before the savepoint stays intact and uncommitted. - The caller can then catch the error, log it or add a comment, and carry on in the same transaction. **Where it is applied:** - `erpnext/stock/utils.py`, `_create_bin`: it sets a savepoint named `create_bin` and inserts a Bin. On `frappe.UniqueValidationError` (a concurrent insert) it rolls back to the savepoint. The inline comment reads "preserve transaction in postgres". It then fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. - `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py`, the subcontracting-order mapper: it sets the savepoint `submit_subcontracting_order` around `target_doc.submit()`. On failure it rolls back to the savepoint and adds a "Submit Action Failed" comment to the document. - `erpnext/subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py`: the same pattern with the savepoint `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py`: `auto_create_fiscal_year` uses a savepoint so a duplicate-year INSERT doesn't abort the transaction. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py`: the savepoint `subscription_update`. - `erpnext/crm/frappe_crm_api.py`: `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. The comment says a failure while linking contacts or addresses must not discard the Customer. - `erpnext/erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py`: `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. These are per-record savepoints. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py`: one savepoint per invoice, so a failure undoes only that invoice. - Other bulk and best-effort flows: - `erpnext/utilities/bulk_transaction.py` - `erpnext/accounts/bulk_payment.py` - `erpnext/stock/reorder_item.py` (`reorder_mr`) - `erpnext/assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` (`depr_entry`) - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` - `erpnext/crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` - `erpnext/telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` - `erpnext/stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` - `erpnext/stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` - `erpnext/regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` - `erpnext/setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` and `taxes_setup.py` - `erpnext/setup/demo.py` - the patch `erpnext/patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` - `erpnext/controllers/ledger_preview.py`: the savepoint `ledger_preview` is used the other way round. It rolls back deliberately so a preview never persists anything. `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` mentions "savepoint discipline" as one of the PostgreSQL compatibility checks. I identified these from a search for `savepoint` and read only `stock/utils.py` and the Purchase Order mapper in full. The other entries are based on the matching lines and comments.

final answer
**Mechanism.** ERPNext uses database savepoints through Frappe's `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` and `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I identified this from the savepoint calls and the comments next to them. I did not read the Frappe implementation in this session. The pattern is the same everywhere. The code sets a named savepoint just before the optional write and wraps the write in `try/except`. On failure it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. It then logs the error or falls back, and the outer transaction carries on. **Difference from a full rollback.** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the whole transaction. That includes the parent document being submitted and any earlier work in the loop. - Rolling back to a savepoint undoes only the statements issued after that savepoint. This clears PostgreSQL's "current transaction is aborted" state, so later statements can run. The earlier work in the outer transaction stays pending and is not lost. - MariaDB tolerates a failed statement without this. PostgreSQL does not, which is why the code comments say things like "preserve transaction in postgres". There is also a CI helper, `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py`, that mentions "savepoint discipline". **Where it is applied** (all under `erpnext/`): - **Concurrent or duplicate inserts** - `stock/utils.py::_create_bin` sets a `create_bin` savepoint. On `UniqueValidationError` it rolls back to the savepoint, then fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. - `accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` uses `auto_create_fiscal_year` so a duplicate-year insert doesn't abort the transaction. - `stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py::repost_based_on_transaction` sets one savepoint per Repost Item Valuation submit. It rolls back on `DuplicateEntryError`. - **Auto-created linked documents during another flow** - `crm/frappe_crm_api.py` uses `crm_customer_links` so a failure linking contacts or addresses doesn't discard the Customer just created. It also uses `crm_create_address`. - `buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` uses `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses `subscription_update`. - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` uses `create_bank_account`. - `stock/reorder_item.py` uses `reorder_mr`. - `assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` uses `depr_entry`. - **Per-item isolation in bulk loops** - `utilities/bulk_transaction.py::job` and `retry_failed_transactions` use `before_creation_state`. - `accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` creates a hashed savepoint name for each invoice. - `accounts/bulk_payment.py` uses `bulk_pe`. - `accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` uses `ledger_merge_row`. - `regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` uses `import_invoice`. - `accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` uses `bank_entry`. - `erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` uses `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - `patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` uses `serial_no_patch`. - **Optional setup steps** - `setup/demo.py::setup_demo_data` uses `demo_data`. - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py::update_regional_tax_settings` uses `regional_tax_settings`. - **Other uses** - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` uses `ledger_preview`. final answer
ERPNext uses database savepoints. It calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. **Why it works.** On PostgreSQL, a failed statement puts the whole transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then errors with "current transaction is aborted". Rolling back to the savepoint clears that state, so the outer transaction can carry on. **How it differs from a full rollback.** A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the entire transaction. That would throw away the parent document being submitted, along with earlier work in the same request or job. `rollback(save_point=...)` undoes only the writes made since the savepoint. The comments in the code say this explicitly: - `opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` says a failure "only undoes that invoice", keeping earlier invoices and error logs, which "on postgres would be lost" under a full rollback. - `frappe_crm_api.py` says a link failure "does NOT discard the Customer". - `plaid_settings.py` says "a failed insert/submit must not discard the Bank…" record. **Where it is applied.** I read only two call sites in full (the first two below). For the rest I relied on the grep hits and the comments next to them. - **Optional or best-effort writes inside a larger transaction** - `PaymentEntry.trigger_invoice_update_for_subscriptions` in `accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` uses savepoint `"subscription_update"`. It wraps `refresh_subscription_status()` in a try/except, rolls back to the savepoint on failure and calls `frappe.log_error`. - `_create_bin` in `stock/utils.py` uses savepoint `"create_bin"`. It catches `UniqueValidationError` from a concurrent insert, rolls back to the savepoint ("preserve transaction in postgres") and then fetches the existing Bin with `get_last_doc`. - `accounts/doctype/fiscal_year/fiscal_year.py` has `auto_create_fiscal_year`, where a duplicate-year INSERT would abort the transaction. - `buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py` has `submit_subcontracting_order`. - `subcontracting/doctype/subcontracting_receipt/mapper.py` has `submit_subcontracting_receipt`. - `crm/frappe_crm_api.py` has `crm_create_address` and `crm_customer_links`. - `stock/reorder_item.py` has `reorder_mr`. - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/install_fixtures.py` has `create_bank_account`. - `setup/setup_wizard/operations/taxes_setup.py` has `regional_tax_settings`. - `telephony/doctype/call_log/call_log.py` has `link_call_logs`. - `crm/doctype/email_campaign/email_campaign.py` has `email_campaign_send`. - **Per-item isolation in loops and bulk jobs** - `accounts/doctype/opening_invoice_creation_tool/opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` uses a unique savepoint name per invoice, built from `frappe.generate_hash`. - `utilities/bulk_transaction.py` has `before_creation_state`. - `accounts/bulk_payment.py` has `bulk_pe`. - `accounts/doctype/ledger_merge/ledger_merge.py` has `ledger_merge_row`. - `erpnext_integrations/doctype/plaid_settings/plaid_settings.py` has `plaid_bank_account`, `plaid_update_account` and `plaid_sync_txn`. - `regional/doctype/import_supplier_invoice/import_supplier_invoice.py` has `import_invoice`. - `accounts/doctype/bank_transaction/bank_transaction_upload.py` has `bank_entry`. - `assets/doctype/asset/depreciation.py` has `depr_entry`. - `stock/doctype/repost_item_valuation/repost_item_valuation.py` has `repost_accounting_ledger`. - `stock/report/stock_and_account_value_comparison/stock_and_account_value_comparison.py` has `repost_value_comparison` and `repost_based_on_transaction`. The second one handles `DuplicateEntryError` from an already-queued repost. - `stock/report/stock_ledger_invariant_check/stock_ledger_invariant_check.py` has `repost_invariant_check`. - `patches/v13_0/trim_whitespace_from_serial_nos.py` has `serial_no_patch`. - **Preview and no-persist cases** - `controllers/ledger_preview.py` uses the `ledger_preview` savepoint so a preview never persists anything. - `setup/demo.py` uses `demo_data`. - **Tests** - `stock/doctype/bin/test_bin.py` uses `dup_bin`. - `stock/doctype/item/test_item.py` uses `dup_barcode`. - `manufacturing/doctype/routing/test_routing.py` uses `create_routing`. - `buying/doctype/supplier_quotation/test_supplier_quotation.py` uses `before_cancel`. The repo's `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` check also names "savepoint discipline" as a PostgreSQL compatibility rule.

ERPNext handles this with **database savepoints**. It calls `frappe.db.savepoint(name)` before the risky write. If the write fails, it calls `frappe.db.rollback(save_point=name)`. I read the code at `mapper.py` and `stock/utils.py`. For the other files I only have grep hits on the savepoint calls and comments, so what each one guards is inferred from those lines. **Why it's needed on PostgreSQL** A failed statement puts the whole transaction into an aborted state. Every later statement then fails until you roll back. MariaDB doesn't behave this way. Rolling back to the savepoint undoes only the failed part and leaves the transaction usable. **How it differs from a full rollback** - A plain `frappe.db.rollback()` discards the entire transaction. That includes the parent document being submitted and any earlier work in the same request or job. - `rollback(save_point=...)` discards only what happened after the savepoint was set. The surrounding work stays pending and can still be committed. - The comments in `opening_invoice_creation_tool.py` and `frappe_crm_api.py` say this directly. A failure there must not discard the Customer, and on PostgreSQL the other work would otherwise be lost. **Where it's applied** - **`erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/mapper.py`, auto-submitting a Subcontracting Order:** - The code is in the function that builds the Subcontracting Order from a Purchase Order and optionally saves and submits it. - It sets the savepoint `submit_subcontracting_order` and wraps `target_doc.submit()` in a try block. - Q25Group E — Concurrency & database semanticsMariaDB and PostgreSQL sort NULL values differently. In queries where that difference would change which row ERPNext picks (not ju…
MariaDB and PostgreSQL sort NULL values differently. In queries where that difference would change which row ERPNext picks (not just display order), how does the codebase explicitly control NULL ordering? Describe the mechanisms used and name concrete query sites.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't rely on the database's default NULL ordering where the choice would change which row is picked. It uses two mechanisms. The first is an explicit `IS NULL` sort key placed ahead of the real column. The second is wrapping the column in `IfNull` or `Coalesce` with a sentinel value. There's no `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` syntax anywhere in the Python code, because MariaDB doesn't support it. I found these sites with a text search and read only some of them in full. I did not trace every caller of the Python-side sorts. **Mechanism 1: `col.isnull()` as a leading sort key** This is a pypika boolean expression. It evaluates to 0 or 1, so it behaves the same on both databases. The real column is sorted as a second key. - **`get_item_price` in `erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py` (about line 1233).** The query is `LIMIT 1`, so the sort decides which price is used. - It sorts `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=asc)`, then `.orderby(ip.valid_from, order=desc)`. - Prices with a `valid_from` date come first, newest first. Prices with no `valid_from` come last, as a fallback. - The same pattern appears around line 1132 in the same file. - **`erpnext/selling/page/point_of_sale/point_of_sale.py` (about line 234).** It uses `ItemPrice.valid_from.isnull()` with `Order.asc`, which gives the same result for POS item prices. - **`get_serial_nos_based_on_filters` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (about line 2511).** This picks which serial numbers to consume, with a `LIMIT` of the requested quantity. - When the sort field is `amc_expiry_date` (and the order is not LIFO), it sorts `order_by_column.isnull()` descending, then the column ascending. - Serial numbers with no AMC expiry are therefore taken first, then the earliest expiry. This keeps the MariaDB behaviour on PostgreSQL. **Mechanism 2: `IfNull` or `Coalesce` with a sentinel value** The NULL is replaced by a value that sorts where you want it. - **`get_item_price` tie-breakers (`get_item_details.py`, about lines 1235, 1250 and 1255).** `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` are sorted descending. A specific batch, customer or supplier beats a generic price, and NULL is treated as an empty string. - **Pricing rule lookup in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py` (about line 155).** This is raw SQL: `order by coalesce(`tabPricing Rule`.priority, '') desc, name desc`. It decides which rule has priority. - **`erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py` (about line 239).** `IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01")` ascending. The code comment says it keeps NULL dates first, "as MariaDB `date asc` did". - **`erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (about line 914).** `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending, so missing stock quantities count as 0. - **`erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py` (`get_all_certifications_of_a_member`).** `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. - **`erpnext/accounts/doctype/payment_entry/payment_entry.py` (about line 2798).** `Coalesce(PR.transaction_date, PR.creation)` ascending, which falls back to the creation date when there's no transaction date.

final answer
ERPNext has no `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` clause anywhere in its Python code. A Grep for that SQL clause and its pypika forms, `NullsFirst`/`NullsLast`, found nothing. Instead it controls NULL ordering in two ways. Either it replaces NULL with a sentinel inside the `ORDER BY` using `IfNull`, or it filters NULLs out before the ordering picks a row. The target is always MariaDB's behaviour: NULLs sort first in ascending order and last in descending order. PostgreSQL is made to match it. This follows the `.greptile/config.json` review rule that "MariaDB output must never change". **1. `IfNull(col, sentinel)` inside `orderby`** - **`erpnext/setup/doctype/email_digest/email_digest.py`, `EmailDigest.get_todo_list`.** - The query orders by `IfNull(todo.date, "1000-01-01")`. The code comment says this keeps "NULL dates first, as MariaDB `date asc` did". - It also orders by a `Case()` expression on priority with `.else_(0)`. That mimics MySQL `FIELD(priority,'High','Medium','Low')`, where NULL, empty or unknown priority gives 0 and sorts first. - Both orderings affect which rows survive `.limit(20)`. - `test_email_digest.py::test_get_todo_list_priority_and_date_ordering` asserts this behaviour. - **`erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`.** It orders by `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. Here the sentinel is the lowest possible date, so NULL still sorts last in descending order. - **`erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, item price lookup** (around lines 1235, 1250 and 1255). - It orders by `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")`, all descending. - This decides which Item Price row is picked, with the more specific rows (batch, customer, supplier set) coming first and NULLs last. - **`erpnext/controllers/queries.py`** (around line 914). It orders by `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending, so rows with no Bin sort as quantity 0. **2. Excluding NULLs so the engines can't disagree** - **`erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py`.** - `first_contact` takes the first row ordered by the nullable `communication_date`. Without a guard, MariaDB (NULLs first) returned a NULL-dated Communication and PostgreSQL (NULLs last) returned the earliest real date. - The fix adds a `communication_date IS NOT NULL` filter and guards the slice that reads `row[0][0]`. - `test_lead_conversion_time.py::test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` checks that the duration is measured from the earliest real contact (22 days). **Related NULL handling that isn't ordering** - **`erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/mapper.py`, `_item_is_alive`.** It uses `end_of_life.isnull() | (end_of_life > nowdate())`. It adds the `'0000-00-00'` term only when `frappe.db.db_type != "postgres"`. This is a NULL-versus-zero-date difference, not an ordering one. I found these sites with search and one regex Grep. The Grep was capped at 60 lines, and I did not read every `orderby` call in the repo, so there may be other sites.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST`. A grep for those and for `null_order` found nothing. It controls NULL ordering in two other ways: 1. It adds an explicit `IS NULL` sort key ahead of the real sort column. 2. It filters NULLs out in the `WHERE` clause, so they can't be the `LIMIT 1` row. I also saw a third pattern, which replaces NULL with a sentinel value, but it doesn't change which row is picked. **1. `IS NULL` as a leading sort key** (`erpnext/stock/get_item_details.py`, `get_item_price()`, about line 1233) - The query selects one Item Price row with `.limit(1)`, so the row picked depends on where NULLs sort. - It chains `.orderby(ip.valid_from.isnull(), order=asc)`, then `.orderby(ip.valid_from, order=desc)`. - `IS NULL` evaluates to 0 or 1, so rows with a `valid_from` come first and open-ended prices come last. This is the same on MariaDB and PostgreSQL. - Without it, MariaDB would put NULLs last in a descending sort (and first in an ascending one), while PostgreSQL does the reverse. **2. Filtering NULLs out of the query** (`erpnext/crm/report/lead_conversion_time/lead_conversion_time.py`, `get_communication_details()`, about lines 119–125) - The query finds the first contact with `.orderby(comm.communication_date).limit(1)`. - An ascending sort would put NULLs first on MariaDB and last on PostgreSQL, so the first row could be a NULL one. - The query adds `comm.communication_date.isnotnull()` to the `WHERE` clause, so a NULL date can never be the first row. - `test_first_contact_ignores_null_communication_date` in `test_lead_conversion_time.py` covers this. **3. `IfNull` with a sentinel value** (this makes NULL sort at a known position) - `IfNull(ip.batch_no, "")`, `IfNull(ip.customer, "")` and `IfNull(ip.supplier, "")` are used as `orderby` keys in the same `get_item_price()` query. They keep empty and generic prices at a fixed position, with the more specific party or batch first. - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`, `get_all_certifications_of_a_member()`, orders by `IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01")` descending. This puts NULL `to_date` rows last on both databases. It only changes list order, not which row is chosen. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (about line 914) orders by `IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0)` descending. The `.isnull()` and `.isnotnull()` calls elsewhere in `erpnext` are `WHERE` conditions, not sort controls.

final answer
ERPNext doesn't use `NULLS FIRST` or `NULLS LAST` anywhere in the Python code I searched. It controls NULL ordering in the `ORDER BY` expression itself, in three ways. This list comes from two greps and a few targeted reads, so I may have missed some query sites. **1. A leading `col IS NULL` sort key as an explicit NULL bucket** - Site: `get_serial_nos_based_on_filters` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py`, around line 2511. It picks which Serial Nos get consumed, with `LIMIT qty` and `FOR UPDATE`. - For FIFO ordering by `amc_expiry_date`, it runs `query.orderby(order_by_column.isnull(), order=Order.desc)` and then `query.orderby(order_by_column)`. - Sorting `IS NULL` descending puts NULL rows first on both engines. MariaDB's default is NULLs first on ASC, and Postgres's is NULLs last on ASC, so without the first key they would pick different serial numbers. - The LIFO branch just sorts the column descending, with no NULL handling. **2. Replacing NULL with a sentinel value (`IfNull` or `Coalesce`)** - `erpnext/www/payment_setup_certification.py`, `get_all_certifications_of_a_member`: `.orderby(IfNull(cc.to_date, "0001-01-01"), order=desc)`. A NULL `to_date` sorts as the earliest date, so it comes last in a DESC sort on both engines. - `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pricing_rule/utils.py`, around line 155, in the raw SQL that fetches pricing rules: `order by coalesce(`tabPricing Rule`.priority, '') desc, name desc`. A NULL priority becomes `''`, so the rule order, and therefore which rule wins, is the same on both engines. - `erpnext/controllers/queries.py`, around line 914, the warehouse query that joins Bin: `orderby(IfNull(bin_dt.actual_qty, 0), desc)`, then by warehouse name. Warehouses with no Bin row sort as 0. **3. Handling NULL in filters so it doesn't depend on engine defaults** These aren't ordering, but they follow the same approach. - `.isnull()` is used alongside `== ""`, as in `erpnext/utilities/naming.py` and `erpnext/controllers/queries.py` (the `expiry_date.isnull()` filters). - `erpnext/manufacturing/doctype/work_order/mapper.py`, around line 59, says an unset `end_of_life` is NULL on Postgres, while MariaDB uses the zero-date `'0000-00-00'`. It handles this with `end_of_life.isnull() | (end_of_life > nowdate())`. - `.github/helper/postgres_compat.py` is a compatibility helper that converts `ifnull` to `coalesce` on Postgres.
Q26Group F — Trap / hallucination resistanceDoes ERPNext prevent a user from selecting an expired batch when issuing or transferring stock via a Stock Entry — i.e. is there a…

final answer
**No. ERPNext does not block an expired batch on a Stock Entry that issues or transfers stock. Material Issue and Material Transfer are explicitly exempted.** **The main check is skipped for these purposes.** It is in `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py`, in the validation loop (around lines 110–147). It throws `BatchExpiredError` with "Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired." when `Batch.expiry_date < doc.posting_date`. Before that, the loop sets `is_material_issue = True` when `doc.doctype == "Stock Entry"` and the purpose is `"Material Issue"` or `"Material Transfer"`. For those rows it runs `continue` (line 130–131), so the expiry check is never reached. So the submit-time check only applies to other cases. Examples are a Stock Entry that receives stock (Material Receipt, or a Manufacture finished-good row), and other documents that use this service. I only read this part of the file, so I haven't confirmed exactly which documents call it. **The ledger-level check also skips Stock Entry.** `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py` (lines 287–299) throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." But it only runs when `voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`. It also returns early for returns against Purchase Receipt, Purchase Invoice, Delivery Note and Sales Invoice. **What does exist for issues and transfers:** - When batches are picked automatically, expired ones are filtered out. `get_non_expired_batches` and the `expiry_date >= today() or null` conditions are in `erpnext/stock/doctype/serial_and_batch_bundle/serial_and_batch_bundle.py` (around lines 2570–2580, 3173 and 3518). This only affects auto-selection or the picker. A user can still pick an expired batch by hand. - `Pick List.validate_expired_batches()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/pick_list/pick_list.py` does throw on expired batches, but that is Pick List, not Stock Entry. I didn't look for other Stock Entry hooks outside the `stock` module's Python files.

final answer
**Partly. ERPNext does not block expired batches for every Stock Entry issue or transfer. The check only runs for four purposes, and it runs at validate time, not specifically at submit.** **Where the check is** - `StockEntry.validate_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry/stock_entry.py` (around line 1479). It is called from the Stock Entry's validation flow (line 310). - It loops over the item rows and calls `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` only when `purpose` is one of: - Material Transfer for Manufacture - Manufacture - Repack - Send to Subcontractor - `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py` (lines 88–99). It throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." when `getdate(parent.posting_date) > getdate(batch.expiry_date)`. - The same method also throws if the batch is disabled. **What this means for plain Stock Entries** - A plain **Material Issue** or **Material Transfer** (or Material Receipt) does not get this row-level check. - The expiry comparison uses the Stock Entry's posting date, not today's date. A batch that expires on the posting date itself is still allowed, because the test is a strict `>`. **The ledger-level backstop skips Stock Entries** - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_ledger_entry/stock_ledger_entry.py` (line 287) also throws "has expired". It starts with `if self.batch_no and self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so it never applies to Stock Entries. **Related expiry handling elsewhere** - Pick List has its own `validate_expired_batches()`, which blocks expired batches. - `get_expired_batches()` and `get_expired_batch_items()` in `stock_entry/services/serial_batch.py` find expired batches. I did not trace where they are used. - `get_non_expired_batches()` in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` filters out expired batches when picking serial numbers. I did not check whether the Serial and Batch Bundle validation also rejects an expired batch on a Stock Entry.

final answer
**Yes, mostly.** ERPNext throws if a Stock Entry uses a batch whose `expiry_date` is before the posting date. There is one gap, covered below. I read the code but did not run it or the tests. **1. Serial and Batch Bundle validation (main check)** - File: `erpnext/stock/services/serial_batch_bundle_service.py`, around lines 130–147. - The check applies when `qty > 0`, the row has a `batch_no`, the doc has a `posting_date`, and `docstatus < 2`. - It loads the batch's `expiry_date` and throws `BatchExpiredError` ("Row #{0}: The batch {1} has already expired.") if `expiry_date < posting_date`. - It is skipped for a material issue (`if is_material_issue: continue`). That condition is defined above the lines I read, so I did not confirm exactly which Stock Entry purposes it covers. - I did not trace what calls this method, so I can't say whether it runs for every other purpose, including Material Transfer. **2. Stock Entry row check (limited to some purposes)** - `StockEntry.validate_batch()` in `stock_entry.py` (line 1479) is called from `validate` (line 310). - It only loops over rows when the purpose is "Material Transfer for Manufacture", "Manufacture", "Repack" or "Send to Subcontractor". - For those rows it calls `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` in `stock_entry_detail.py` (line 88). - That method throws if the batch is disabled. It also throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." if `posting_date > expiry_date`. **3. Stock Ledger Entry check (skips Stock Entry)** - `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` in `stock_ledger_entry.py` (line 287) has the same expiry check. - It deliberately skips it when `voucher_type == "Stock Entry"`, so it does not protect Stock Entries. **The gap:** - A plain Material Issue is skipped by the bundle check. - A plain Material Transfer is not in the purpose list for the row-level check. - So an expired batch on a plain Material Issue is not blocked by anything I found. - A plain Material Transfer is blocked only if the bundle validation reaches it, and I did not confirm that. - For Material Issue, the skip looks intentional, so expired stock can still be written off. **Other expiry filtering:** - Batch pickers in `serial_and_batch_bundle.py` filter out expired batches (`expiry_date >= today()` or null). - `Pick List` has its own `validate_expired_batches`.

final answer
**Short answer:** Only partly. ERPNext does have a validation that throws "Batch {0} of Item {1} has expired." for a Stock Entry, but it doesn't cover plain Material Issue or Material Transfer. For those purposes the guard is mostly the batch picker, which filters expired batches out. **What exists** 1. **Stock Entry row check.** `StockEntryDetail.validate_batch()` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/stock_entry_detail/stock_entry_detail.py` (lines 88–99). - It throws if the batch is disabled. - It also throws if `getdate(parent.posting_date) > getdate(batch.expiry_date)`. The comparison uses the Stock Entry's posting date, not today's date. - It is called from `StockEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_entry.py`, line 1479), which runs inside `validate()`. That means it fires on save and submit, not only on submit. 2. **The purpose filter.** `StockEntry.validate_batch()` only loops over rows when the purpose is one of these: - Material Transfer for Manufacture - Manufacture - Repack - Send to Subcontractor So plain **Material Issue** and **Material Transfer** don't get this row-level check. 3. **Stock Ledger Entry check.** `StockLedgerEntry.validate_batch()` (`stock_ledger_entry.py`, lines 287–299) has the same expiry throw. It starts with `if self.batch_no and self.voucher_type != "Stock Entry"`, so it explicitly skips Stock Entry vouchers. Expired batches are therefore blocked at the ledger for other vouchers like Delivery Note and Sales Invoice, but not for Stock Entry. - It also has exemptions for returns. Purchase Receipt and Purchase Invoice rows with negative quantity, and Delivery Note and Sales Invoice rows with positive quantity, are skipped. 4. **Batch picker filtering.** When batches are auto-picked or selected through a Serial and Batch Bundle, expired ones are excluded. In `serial_and_batch_bundle.py`, `get_non_expired_batches` and the `get_auto_batch_nos` queries add `expiry_date >= today() OR expiry_date IS NULL`. The exception is when `for_stock_levels` is set. - This is a filter on what the picker offers, not a throw. - A user could still create a bundle with an expired batch through other routes, such as the API or legacy batch fields. I did not trace every path that creates a bundle. **Conclusion:** Issuing or transferring an expired batch through a Stock Entry is not blocked by a submit-time throw. The expiry check applies only to Manufacture, Repack, Material Transfer for Manufacture and Send to Subcontractor. For Material Issue and Material Transfer, ERPNext relies on the picker hiding expired batches. If you need a hard block, you would add the expiry check to those purposes yourself, for example by extending the purpose list in `StockEntry.validate_batch`.

Sales Order enforces a customer credit limit before submission. Does Purchase Order have an equivalent automatic spend-limit check against the supplier before submission? If something does gate Purchase Order submission, what mechanism is it, and how does it differ in nature from a credit limit?

final answer
No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier. The closest gate is the supplier scorecard standing check, and it works differently from a credit limit. **No credit-limit equivalent.** A search of `erpnext/buying` for `credit_limit` or `check_credit_limit` returns nothing. Sales Order's credit check lives in the selling and accounts code. Purchase Order has no counterpart that compares the outstanding payable plus the new order total against a cap. **What does gate it: the Supplier Scorecard standing.** - The check is `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier()` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` (around lines 272–294). - `validate()` calls it at line 200, so it runs on save and therefore before submit. It is not a separate `before_submit` hook. - It reads two flags on the Supplier: `prevent_pos` and `warn_pos`. - If `prevent_pos` is set, it looks up the Supplier Scorecard `status` (standing). If a standing is found, it calls `frappe.throw` ("Purchase Orders are not allowed for {supplier} due to a scorecard standing of {standing}"). That blocks the save and submission. - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows a `frappe.msgprint` caution (orange indicator) and lets the order proceed. - It also sets `party_account_currency`. - The Supplier Scorecard Standing (its status) and the supplier's `prevent_pos`/`warn_pos` flags are only configured separately, not in this file. **How it differs in nature from a credit limit.** - **Financial vs. qualitative:** A customer credit limit is a quantitative, monetary exposure control. It compares outstanding receivables plus the new order against a configured amount. The PO check ignores amounts and the supplier's payable balance. It is a categorical block or warning based on the supplier's performance rating, which comes from scorecard criteria such as quality and delivery. - **Direction of risk:** A credit limit protects the company from a customer not paying. The PO gate protects the company from an unreliable supplier. It is about vendor quality, not how much the company owes. - **Enforcement:** Credit limits can be bypassed by a role with the bypass permission. The PO gate is a flag-driven hard block (`prevent_pos`) or a soft warning (`warn_pos`), and it has no role-based override in this code. - **Trigger:** The PO gate fires whenever the flag is on and a standing exists, regardless of order value.

**No, Purchase Order has no automatic spend-limit check against the supplier.** I found no supplier-side counterpart to `check_credit_limit` (outstanding amount compared with a monetary limit). Nothing in the Purchase Order code compares the PO or outstanding payables to a supplier amount cap. I searched the code and didn't grep every buying file for the word "limit", so a limit check under a different name is unlikely but not ruled out. **What gates Purchase Order submission instead** Two qualitative, status-based gates run in `validate`, so they block saving and therefore submitting: 1. **Supplier hold/block.** `AccountsController.ensure_supplier_is_not_blocked` is in `erpnext/controllers/accounts_controller.py` and is called from `AccountsController.validate`. - It applies to Purchase Order and Purchase Invoice, and to Supplier payments in Payment Entry. - It reads the Supplier's `on_hold`, `hold_type` and `release_date` fields. - If the supplier is on hold, `hold_type` is "All" or "Invoices", and today is on or before `release_date` (or there is no release date), it raises "{0} is blocked so this transaction cannot proceed". - `test_po_for_blocked_supplier_all` and `test_po_for_blocked_supplier_invoices` in `test_purchase_order.py` cover this. 2. **Supplier Scorecard standing.** `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier` is in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py`. - If the Supplier's `prevent_pos` is set, it throws "Purchase Orders are not allowed for {0} due to a scorecard standing of {1}" when a scorecard status exists. - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows an orange "Caution" message. Budget checks in accounting can also stop a Purchase Order (`test_budget.py` has `test_monthly_budget_crossed_for_po`). That limit applies to a cost center or account, not to the supplier. **How this differs from a credit limit** | | Customer credit limit | Purchase Order gate | |---|---|---| | Nature | Quantitative: a number compared with exposure | Qualitative: a flag or status | | Check | `customer.check_credit_limit` computes `get_customer_outstanding` (including unbilled Sales Orders) and compares it with `get_credit_limit` | Is the supplier blocked, or is their scorecard status bad? | | Limit source | The Customer's Credit Limit table, falling back to the Customer Group, then the Company | Fields on the Supplier and Supplier Scorecard | | Where it runs | `SalesOrder.on_submit` | `validate`, which runs before submit | | Override | A user with the Accounts Settings `credit_controller` role can proceed, and a per-company "bypass credit limit check" flag exists | The throw can't be overridden by a role. The hold ends when `release_date` passes or someone clears the flag | A credit limit protects the company against a customer owing too much. The Purchase Order gates instead decide whether you are allowed to buy from that supplier at all, based on a block or a poor performance rating, regardless of how much you already owe them.

final answer
**No. Purchase Order has no automatic spend limit against a supplier.** I searched `erpnext/buying` for `credit_limit` and `check_credit_limit` and found nothing. The Sales Order check, `check_credit_limit()` in `selling/doctype/customer/customer.py`, has no buying-side counterpart. The Supplier doctype has no credit-limit or spend-cap field that Purchase Order enforces. Several other checks do run on Purchase Order, in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py`: 1. **Supplier scorecard standing** (`validate_supplier`, called during validate). - If the supplier's `prevent_pos` flag is set, it calls `frappe.throw` with the scorecard standing, which blocks the order. - If `warn_pos` is set, it only shows an orange `msgprint` caution. - This is a qualitative, flag-based gate. It is not tied to money. 2. **Budget check** (`on_submit` calls `self.validate_budget()`, defined in `BuyingController`, `controllers/buying_controller.py:978`). - It calls `validate_expense_against_budget` in `accounts/doctype/budget/budget.py`. - It compares the PO's expense with the Budget doctype, which is set per cost center, project or account. - It can stop or warn depending on how the Budget is configured. 3. **Authorization Control** (`on_submit` calls `Authorization Control.validate_approving_authority(doctype, company, base_grand_total)`). - This is a rule-based approval check. A PO above a role's or user's authorized amount needs an approver. There are also validations such as minimum order quantity and the status updater's over-ordering checks. **How these differ from a credit limit:** - A customer credit limit is a risk control on a counterparty. It caps the receivable you are exposed to. It compares the customer's outstanding balance plus the new order against a per-customer, per-company limit, and a user with the right role can bypass it. - Nothing like that exists for suppliers, because a payable is a liability you owe, not an exposure you carry. - The Purchase Order gates are internal controls. The budget check limits spend by cost center, project or account. The authorization rule limits spend by approver role or amount. The scorecard check is a performance flag on the supplier. - None of them track the running amount owed to a particular supplier. I did not read the body of `validate_budget` or `validate_expense_against_budget`, so I haven't checked exactly when each one throws and when it only warns.

final answer
No. Purchase Order has no automatic per-supplier spend or exposure limit that works like the Sales Order customer credit limit. I found nothing in `erpnext/buying` that caps outstanding or ordered value per supplier. Three mechanisms do gate Purchase Order submission, and none of them is a spend limit on the supplier. **1. Supplier Scorecard standing (the supplier-specific gate)** - `PurchaseOrder.validate_supplier()` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/purchase_order/purchase_order.py` (around line 272) does the check. - If `Supplier.prevent_pos` is set, it throws "Purchase Orders are not allowed for {supplier} due to a scorecard standing of …". This is a hard block. - If `Supplier.warn_pos` is set, it only shows an orange "Caution" message. - The flags come from `SupplierScorecard` in `erpnext/buying/doctype/supplier_scorecard/supplier_scorecard.py`. Its `validate` and `on_update` compute the weighted score from the criteria. They then pick a standing (`SupplierScorecardStanding`) that carries `prevent_pos` and `warn_pos`, and copy those flags onto the Supplier. - The default standings set `prevent_pos` on the worst band and `warn_pos` on the next band (see the dicts near lines 369–406). **2. Budget validation (the closest thing to a spend limit)** - `PurchaseOrder.on_submit` calls `validate_budget()` at line 386. It is defined in `erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py` (line 978) and runs `BudgetValidation` from `erpnext/controllers/budget_controller.py`. - `handle_purchase_order_overlimit` compares the PO against the `Budget` document, using the `applicable_on_purchase_order` and `action_if_*_on_po` fields. - The comparison covers annual and accumulated-monthly budgets. It adds the PO amount to open PO amounts not yet billed, and to open Material Requests. - Depending on the Budget settings, the action is Warn or Stop. A user with the company's `exception_budget_approver_role` is only warned. **3. Authorization Control** - `on_submit` also calls `Authorization Control.validate_approving_authority(doctype, company, base_grand_total)`. This is a role- or user-based approval-amount rule. **How this differs from a credit limit** - **What it is attached to.** A customer credit limit belongs to the party. It is a `Customer Credit Limit` row per company, checked against that customer's outstanding receivables plus undelivered or unbilled orders. Supplier-side control is not tied to an amount owed to that supplier. It is either a qualitative performance flag (scorecard standing) or an internal budget. - **What it measures.** A credit limit is financial exposure to the counterparty, which is a risk of not being paid. The scorecard measures supplier quality (price, delivery, quality and similar criteria). The Budget measures the company's own spending against allocations for an account, cost center, project or accounting dimension, so it is not specific to the supplier. - **How it is evaluated.** A credit limit is a per-party running balance checked at submit time. The scorecard flag is precomputed and periodically recalculated, so it is a static on/off state at submit. The Budget check is a live sum over the fiscal year.

Which method performs this cascade cancellation, and how does it locate the linked invoice?

final answer
I couldn't find a method that does this, because ERPNext doesn't cascade-cancel in this case. It blocks the cancellation instead. The relevant code is `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel()` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around line 437). After calling `super().on_cancel()` and checking for on-hold or closed Purchase Orders, it looks for linked invoices with this query: ```python submitted = frappe.get_all( "Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1) if submitted: frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(submitted[0][0])) ``` So the invoice is found through the `purchase_receipt` link field on `Purchase Invoice Item` rows, filtered to submitted rows (`docstatus` 1). If one exists, the system raises an error and the Purchase Receipt stays submitted. You have to cancel the Purchase Invoice yourself first. `before_cancel` only calls `remove_amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice`, which resets `amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice` on the receipt items. It cancels nothing. I also grepped the non-test `.cancel()` calls across `erpnext/` and found none that cancel a Purchase Invoice from a Purchase Receipt. The only part of this repo I read was `erpnext/`, so I can't rule out an app outside it that adds such a cascade.

final answer
I couldn't find a method in this codebase that cancels a linked Purchase Invoice when you cancel a Purchase Receipt. The behavior is the opposite of what you described. `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py` (around lines 437–468) blocks the cancellation. It runs `frappe.get_all("Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], limit=1)`. This looks for a submitted Purchase Invoice whose item rows have `purchase_receipt` set to this receipt. If it finds one, it calls `frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted"))`. So the user has to cancel the invoice first. I checked these places for a cascade and found nothing: - `BuyingController.on_cancel` (`erpnext/controllers/buying_controller.py`) only updates the last purchase rate and handles linked assets. - `PurchaseInvoice.on_cancel` (`erpnext/accounts/doctype/purchase_invoice/purchase_invoice.py`) only updates billing status on the receipt. - The `.cancel()` calls I found under `erpnext/stock` and in the controllers are for other documents, such as Serial and Batch Bundles, Stock Reservation Entries and Unreconcile Payment. I didn't search the whole repository exhaustively for a cancel call on a Purchase Invoice, so a cascade could exist somewhere I didn't look. The nearest thing to a cascade is `cancel_linked_invoices` in `erpnext/accounts/doctype/pos_invoice_merge_log/pos_invoice_merge_log.py`, which is for POS invoices and is unrelated to Purchase Receipts.

The `parent` of the first match is the Purchase Invoice name. That name goes into the error message, and nothing is cancelled. **What I checked:** - `PurchaseReceipt.before_cancel()` only calls `remove_amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice()`. That method zeroes `amount_difference_with_purchase_invoice` on the receipt's items. - `AccountsController.before_cancel` only calls `validate_einvoice_fields`. - A search for `cancel` methods and `.cancel()` calls in the stock, buying, purchase invoice and controller code turned up no Purchase Invoice cascade. My searches weren't exhaustive. I didn't search the whole repo for every `.cancel()` call on a Purchase Invoice, so a cascade could exist somewhere I didn't look. In the code I did read, you have to cancel the Purchase Invoice first and then the Purchase Receipt.

I couldn't find any method that does this. In this codebase, cancelling a Purchase Receipt does not cascade to a billed Purchase Invoice. `PurchaseReceipt.on_cancel` is in `erpnext/stock/doctype/purchase_receipt/purchase_receipt.py`, at about line 437. It does the opposite of cascading. It looks for a submitted invoice and blocks the cancellation: ```python submitted = frappe.get_all( "Purchase Invoice Item", filters={"purchase_receipt": self.name, "docstatus": 1}, fields=["parent"], as_list=True, limit=1, ) if submitted: frappe.throw(_("Purchase Invoice {0} is already submitted").format(submitted[0][0])) ``` - **How it finds the invoice:** it queries the `Purchase Invoice Item` child table for rows where `purchase_receipt` equals this receipt's name and `docstatus` is 1. The row's `parent` is the Purchase Invoice name. - **What it does with it:** it throws an error, so you have to cancel the invoice yourself before you can cancel the receipt. - **Rest of `on_cancel`:** only if no submitted invoice is found does it go on to update the PO status and billing status, reverse the stock ledger and GL entries, and so on. I only read `purchase_receipt.py` and grepped `erpnext/stock` for `.cancel()` calls. I found no Purchase Invoice cancellation there. I did not search the rest of the codebase, so I can't rule out a cascade somewhere else, such as in `erpnext/accounts`.

final answer
The check is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py`, at about lines 295–349. I read that code. I didn't run it or open the Sales Invoice or Delivery Note classes, so the inheritance below is from memory of ERPNext. **Where it runs** - `SellingController.validate()` calls `self.validate_selling_price()` at about line 65. - Sales Invoice and Delivery Note inherit from `SellingController`, so their `validate` runs this check. That happens on both save and submit. - `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py` (line 174) also calls it when items are updated after submit. **When it applies** - It is skipped if the document is a return (`is_return`). - It is skipped if **Selling Settings → "Validate Selling Price"** (`validate_selling_price`) is off. The error message names this setting as the way to bypass the check. - It skips free items (`is_free_item`) and rows with no `item_code`. **What it compares** (all in base currency, converted to the sales UOM with `conversion_factor`) 1. **Last purchase rate:** if `item.base_net_rate` is below the item's `last_purchase_rate`, it throws. 2. **Valuation rate:** this is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. - For Sales Invoice and Delivery Note it uses `item.incoming_rate`, the actual valuation cost of the stock. - For Sales Order and Quotation it uses `item.valuation_rate` instead. - If `base_net_rate` is below that rate, it throws. **How it blocks** The inner `throw_message()` calls `frappe.throw` with the title "Invalid Selling Price". The message reads "Row #… Selling rate for item … is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least …". Because it throws during validation, the document can't be saved or submitted. `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/test_delivery_note.py` has a `test_validate_selling_price` test for this.

final answer
The block is in `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (lines 295–349). I read that function and its call graph, but I didn't open `SellingController.validate` itself. The indexed callers show `validate_selling_price` is called from there. I didn't open the Sales Invoice or Delivery Note files either, so I'm assuming both inherit `SellingController.validate`. **How it works** - `SellingController.validate` calls `validate_selling_price`. The function also runs when child items are updated, via `ChildItemUpdater._post_update` in `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py`. - It returns early, so no check happens, if the document is a return (`is_return`) or if **Selling Settings → "Validate Selling Price"** (`validate_selling_price`) is turned off. - It skips free items and rows with no item code. - For each remaining item it does two checks: 1. **Last purchase rate:** it compares `base_net_rate` with the item's `last_purchase_rate`, converted to the sales UOM with `conversion_factor`. If the net rate is lower, it throws. 2. **Valuation rate:** this check is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. Otherwise it compares `base_net_rate` with the item's cost, again converted by `conversion_factor`. - On Sales Invoice and Delivery Note the cost field is `incoming_rate`, which holds the valuation cost at the time of the transaction. - On Sales Order and Quotation it is `valuation_rate`. - If the net rate is below that cost, it throws. - The error comes from the inner `throw_message` helper. It reads "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least Y", with the title "Invalid Selling Price". It also tells the user to disable the setting to bypass the check. Because this runs in `validate`, the error is raised when the document is saved or submitted, not only at submit.

The block is in `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (line 295). Sales Invoice and Delivery Note both inherit from `SellingController`, so they share it. I read this method and its call site, but I didn't run it. **When it runs** - `SellingController.validate()` calls it at line 65, so it runs on save and submit. It doesn't wait for the submit step. - It is controlled by the **"Validate Selling Price"** checkbox (`validate_selling_price`) in Selling Settings. If the box is off, the check is skipped. Returns (`is_return`) are skipped too. - `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py:174` calls it again when items are updated after submission. **What it checks, per item row** - It skips rows with no item code and rows marked as free items. - **Last purchase rate:** if `base_net_rate` is below the item's `last_purchase_rate`, the row is blocked. The rate is converted to the sales UOM using `conversion_factor` first. - **Valuation cost:** this check is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. - For Sales Invoice and Delivery Note it uses `incoming_rate`, the stock valuation cost. Sales Order and Quotation use `valuation_rate` instead. - If `base_net_rate` is below that cost multiplied by `conversion_factor`, the row is blocked. **How it blocks** The inner `throw_message()` calls `frappe.throw` with the title "Invalid Selling Price". The message reads "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least Y." It also tells the user they can turn off the setting to bypass the check. The Delivery Note test `test_validate_selling_price` in `erpnext/stock/doctype/delivery_note/test_delivery_note.py` covers this behaviour.

final answer
The check is `SellingController.validate_selling_price()` in `erpnext/controllers/selling_controller.py` (line 295). I read its source but didn't run it. **How it is triggered** - `SellingController.validate()` calls `self.validate_selling_price()` on line 65. - Sales Invoice, Delivery Note, Sales Order and Quotation all inherit from `SellingController`, so they all run it. - Because it runs in `validate`, it runs on save and on submit, and the failure happens before the document is submitted. - `erpnext/accounts/services/child_item_update.py` also calls it from `_post_update`. That is the "update items" path on submitted Sales Orders. **When it is skipped** - The check does nothing if the document is a return (`is_return`). - It also does nothing if the **Validate Selling Price** checkbox in Selling Settings (`validate_selling_price`) is off. - Free items and rows without an `item_code` are skipped. **What it checks, per item row** 1. **Last purchase rate.** It converts the item's `last_purchase_rate` to the sales UOM by multiplying by `conversion_factor`. If `base_net_rate` is lower, it throws. 2. **Valuation rate.** This check is skipped for internal customers and for non-stock items. It uses `incoming_rate` on Delivery Note and Sales Invoice, and `valuation_rate` on Sales Order and Quotation. The chosen field is multiplied by `conversion_factor`. If `base_net_rate` is lower, it throws. **The error** - The inner helper `throw_message` raises `frappe.throw` with the title "Invalid Selling Price". - The message reads "Row #N: Selling rate for item X is lower than its valuation rate (or last purchase rate). Selling net rate should be at least Y…". It also tells the user they can turn off the Selling Settings option to bypass the check. For Delivery Notes and Sales Invoices, the "valuation rate" comparison uses the row's `incoming_rate`. That is the stock-ledger cost of the item at the time of sale.
